# Rigveda Samhita — Volume 98 (Kannada → English translation)

### Printed p. 2

*Running head: Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 184], p. 2. The window begins inside the introductory essay to the fifth adhyāya of the second aṣṭaka; the previous page (1) is not in the supplied text.*

The greatness of Agastya is extraordinary, and is very famous throughout the Smṛtis, Purāṇas and the like. The manner of his origin itself indicates his greatness.

(Sanskrit verses quoted in the Kannada prose, as supplied:)

सत्रे ह जाताविषिता नमोभिः कुंभे रेतः सिषिचतुः समानं । (*satre ha jātāviṣitā namobhiḥ kuṃbhe retaḥ siṣicatuḥ samānaṃ |*)
ततो ह मान उदियाय मध्यात्ततो जातमृषिमाहुर्वसिष्ठं ॥ (*tato ha māna udiyāya madhyāttato jātamṛṣimāhurvasiṣṭhaṃ ‖*)
उक्षभृतं सामभृतं बिभर्ति ग्रावाणं बिभ्रत्प्रवदात्यग्रे । (*ukṣabhṛtaṃ sāmabhṛtaṃ bibharti grāvāṇaṃ bibhratpravadātyagre |*)
उपैनमाध्वं सुमनस्यमाना आ वो गच्छाति प्रत्यदो वसिष्ठः ॥ (*upainamādhvaṃ sumanasyamānā ā vo gacchāti pratyado vasiṣṭhaḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7. 37 13. to 14 [as supplied])

तेनैव तु मुहूर्तेन वीर्यवंतौ तपस्विनौ । (*tenaiva tu muhūrtena vīryavaṃtau tapasvinau |*)
आगस्त्यश्च वसिष्ठश्च तत्रर्षि संबभूवतुः ॥ (*āgastyaśca vasiṣṭhaśca tatrarṣi saṃbabhūvatuḥ ‖*)

(Bṛ. De. 5.-12)

Mitra and Varuṇa, two of the twelve Ādityas, were both infatuated at the time of a sacrificial session (*satra*) on seeing the celestial woman Urvaśī. Then their semen was shed and fell into the pitcher (*kumbha*) in which the *vasatīvarī* [as supplied] stood. Immediately after that, two sages, Vasiṣṭha and Agastya, ascetics and endowed with vigour, came into being. The great ascetic Agastya became one possessing only a *śamyā* [as supplied], and both Vasiṣṭha and Agastya became possessed of the radiance of the Āditya: the section that describes this episode is given elsewhere.

The part of Mitra, who has the form of this world, and the part of Varuṇa, who has the form of the celestial world, joined together and became the cause of Agastya's origin. The radiant form of Mitra, which consists of food, and the great form of vigour (*ojas*) of Varuṇa are fully blended in Agastya. And as it is described in the sentence क्रतुदक्षौ ह वा अस्य मित्रावरुणौ (*kratudakṣau ha vā asya mitrāvaruṇau*) (Śa. Brā. 4. 1. 4. 1) (my tentative gloss: "Mitra and Varuṇa are indeed his resolve and skill"), of Mitra and Varuṇa, Mitra is of the form of resolve (*saṅkalpa*) and Varuṇa is of the nature of spiritual power. These two, resolve and power, are Agastya's great qualities. Like Mitra, Agastya is one who makes the resolve for the accomplishment of an undertaking (कारसिद्धिगॆ (*kārasiddhigè*) as supplied), and like Varuṇa one who accomplishes it. Like Mitra he is of the form of Brahma, and like Varuṇa of the form of *kratu*; therefore he is also of the nature of the supreme *kratu-dakṣa*.

(Sanskrit verse quoted in the Kannada prose, as supplied:)

आगस्त्यः खनमानः खनित्रैः प्रजामपत्यं बलमिच्छमानः । (*āgastyaḥ khanamānaḥ khanitraiḥ prajāmapatyaṃ balamicchamānaḥ |*)
उभौ वर्णावृषिरुग्रः पुपोष सत्या देवेश्वाशिषो जगाम ॥ (*ubhau varṇāvṛṣirugraḥ pupoṣa satyā deveśvāśiṣo jagāma ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1. 179. 6)

In this ṛk all the special qualities of Agastya are described as stated above. The sage Agastya, an accomplisher of what is desired by means such as sacrifice and praise, one who desires sons, servants, strength and the like, a seer of the supra-sensory (the sentence continues on the next page)

### Printed p. 3

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 1] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 3. The word अतींद्रिय (*atīṃdriya*) ("supra-sensory") is broken across the page boundary in the supplied text: p. 3 begins with the rest of the compound, "seer".*

and, though immersed in worldly life, free from sin, has pursued and accomplished both *kāma* and *tapas*, two of the chief human aims. For that very reason he has acquired from the gods blessings with true fruits: thus Agastya's power is praised in this mantra.

The sūktas seen and composed by such a sage, full of power and a seer, must also be of profound expression of ideas. When the purport of mantra sentences such as “ कतरा पूर्वा कतरा परायोः ” (*“ katarā pūrvā katarā parāyoḥ ”*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-185-1), “ इदं द्यावा पृथिवी सत्यमस्तु ” (*“ idaṃ dyāvā pṛthivī satyamastu ”*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-185-11) is explained, the realization of the harmony of truth and beauty (सत्यसौंदय्यगळ (*satyasauṃdayyagaḷa*) as supplied) in Agastya's thoughts will be shown. This adhyāya begins with the mantra “ ता वामद्य तावपरं ” (*“ tā vāmadya tāvaparaṃ ”*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-184-1), which praises the Aśvin deities, who are the eyes of the world and have the forms of the sun and the moon, immediately on the appearance of Uṣas, who spreads radiance over the whole world.

*[The label line of the following block reads, as supplied, कायणभाष्यं]* (*kāyaṇabhāṣyaṃ]**) for the usual Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya.]*

**Sāyaṇa's introduction to the sūkta** (Sanskrit, transcribed; see the Anuvāda below for the sense):

> तत्र ता वामितीदं प्रथममंडलस्य चतुर्विंशेऽनुवाके पंचमं सूक्तं ।
> पूर्वसूक्ते षडित्युक्तत्वात् सूक्तसंख्यानुवर्तत इति परिभाषितत्वात् षडृचं । ऋषि-
> श्चान्यस्मादिति परिभाषयागस्त्यं । तथाश्विनं वा इत्युक्तत्वात्तुह्यादिपरिभाषयेदम-
> प्याश्विनं । अनादेशपरिभाषया त्रैष्टुभं । ता वामित्यनुक्रांतं ॥ युवो रजां-
> सीत्यादीनां पंच सूक्तानां तृतीयवर्जितानां प्रातरनुवाकाश्विनशस्त्रयोर्निनियोग
> उक्तः । तत्रेदं पंचमं ॥

> *tatra tā vāmitīdaṃ prathamamaṃḍalasya caturviṃśe 'nuvāke paṃcamaṃ sūktaṃ |*
> *pūrvasūkte ṣaḍityuktatvāt sūktasaṃkhyānuvartata iti paribhāṣitatvāt ṣaḍṛcaṃ | ṛṣi-*
> *ścānyasmāditi paribhāṣayāgastyaṃ | tathāśvinaṃ vā ityuktatvāttuhyādiparibhāṣayedama-*
> *pyāśvinaṃ | anādeśaparibhāṣayā traiṣṭubhaṃ | tā vāmityanukrāṃtaṃ ‖ yuvo rajāṃ-*
> *sītyādīnāṃ paṃca sūktānāṃ tṛtīyavarjitānāṃ prātaranuvākāśvinaśastrayorniniyoga*
> *uktaḥ | tatredaṃ paṃcamaṃ ‖*

*Gist:* the sūkta ता वामद्य तावपरं (*tā vāmadya tāvaparaṃ*) is the fifth sūkta of Anuvāka 24 of Maṇḍala 1, of six ṛks, by Agastya, to the Aśvins, in *triṣṭubh* metre; it is used at the morning litany (*prātaranuvāka*) and the Aśvina-śastra. (The grammatical tail of the introduction is transcribed, not explained. The introduction prints प्रातरनुवाकाश्विनशस्त्रयोर्निनियोग (*prātaranuvākāśvinaśastrayorniniyoga*) for the usual word for "application"; kept as supplied.)

**Anuvāda (Kannada).** The sūkta beginning with ता वामद्य (*tā vāmadya*) (the first words are joined to the label "Anuvāda—" in the supplied text) is the fifth sūkta in the twenty-fourth anuvāka of the first maṇḍala. There are six ṛks in this sūkta. Agastya is the seer of this sūkta; the Aśvin deities themselves are the deity; the metre is *triṣṭubh*. As it was said earlier that the sūktas from the sūkta beginning युवो रजांसि (*yuvo rajāṃsi*) onward, five sūktas (excepting the third sūkta), are applied for the *triṣṭubh*-metre mantras connected with the Aśvina-śastra at the time of reciting the mantras of the morning litany (*prātaranuvāka*), the same application holds for this sūkta; of those sūktas this is the fifth.

*[A stray line ":0:" follows in the supplied text; kept as an italic note in the Kannada file.]*

### Printed p. 4

*Running head: [4 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 184].*

**Sūkta—184**

Maṇḍala 1 ‖ Anuvāka 24 ‖ Sūkta 184 ‖
Aṣṭaka 2 ‖ Adhyāya 5 ‖ Varga 1 ‖
Number of ṛks in the Sūkta: 6 ·
Ṛṣi: Agastya · Devatā: the Aśvins · Chandas: *triṣṭubh*.

#### Ṛk 1

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> ता वा॑म॒द्य ताव॑प॒रं हु॑वेमो॒च्छंत्या॑मु॒षसि॒ वह्नि॑रु॒क्थैः ।
> नास॑त्या॒ कुह॑ चि॒त्संता॑व॒र्यो दि॒वो नपा॑ता सु॒दास्त॑राय ॥ १ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> ता । वां । अद्य । तौ । अपरं । हुवेम । उच्छंत्यां । उषसि । वह्निः । उक्षैः ।
> नासत्या । कुह । चित् । संतौ । अर्यः । दिवः । नपाता । सुदाःतराय ॥ १ ॥

> *tā | vāṃ | adya | tau | aparaṃ | huvema | ucchaṃtyāṃ | uṣasi | vahniḥ | ukṣaiḥ |*
> *nāsatyā | kuha | cit | saṃtau | aryaḥ | divaḥ | napātā | sudāḥtarāya ‖ 1 ‖*

*(Two differences between the supplied Saṃhitā and Pada, kept as supplied and not corrected: the Saṃhitā has वह्निरुक्थैः (*vahnirukthaiḥ*) where the Pada has the final word उक्षैः (*ukṣaiḥ*) (kt/kṣ); and the Pada prints सुदाःतराय (*sudāḥtarāya*) as one unit, without a separating stroke, for the Saṃhitā's सुदास्तराय (*sudāstarāya*).)*

*[The label line of the next block reads, as supplied, सहयभाष्यं]* (*sahayabhāṣyaṃ]**) (with a note in the Kannada file); standard label inserted.]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> ता तौ रक्षकत्वेन प्रसिद्धौ वां युवामद्यास्मिन्यागदिने हुवेम । आह्वयाम
> इत्याशीः । तथापरदुपरस्मिन्नपि दिने तौ युवां हुवेम । अपरमित्येतदपरस्मिन्नि-
> त्यर्थे समभिव्याहारात् अद्या च नो मृळयतापरं च । ऋ २-२९-२ । इत्यादि-
> मंत्रांतरे तथा दर्शनाच्च । अपरयागं प्रत्यपीति वा । कस्मिन्काले । उषस्युषो-
> देवतायामुच्छंत्यां तमो विवासयंत्यां सत्यां । उषःकाले हि प्रातरनुवाकाश्विन-
> शस्त्रे प्रयुज्येते । उक्ते काले हे नासत्यासत्यरहितौ दिवो नपाता द्युलोकस्य

*Gist so far:* the Aśvins, famed as protectors, are invoked today, and on another day too, at dawn when Uṣas dispels the darkness; the bhāṣya continues on the next page ("O Nāsatyas ... lords of heaven").

### Printed p. 5

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 1] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 5.*

#### Ṛk 1 (continued)

**Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya** (continued from p. 4; the block begins mid-sentence)

> नपातयितारौ नप्तस्थानीयौ ना कुह चित्संतौ कुत्रचिद्देशे वर्तमानौ युवां वह्निः
> स्तुतेर्पोढार्य ईरयिता स्तुतेरीश्वरो वा ईदृशोऽहं होता सुदास्तराया-
> त्यर्थं शोभनहनिर्दात्रे यजमानाय तदर्थं तौ युवामुक्थैः शस्त्रैर्हुवेमेति
> संबंधः । अत्राख्यातं पदमेकवचनतया नेतव्यं । वह्निरर्यशब्दौ वा बहुवचनत्वेन
> नेतव्या । अथवा वाक्यद्वयं । यस्माद्वह्निरर्यो होतोक्तगुणकौ युवां शंसति
> तस्माद्वयं यजमानास्तौ युवामाह्वयाम इति ॥

*Gist (whole bhāṣya of Ṛk 1):* O Nāsatyas, wherever you are, I, the Hotṛ, bearer and impeller of praise, invoke you with śastra-hymns, today and on other days at dawn, for the generous sacrificer.

*[The label line of the next block reads, as supplied, १ प्रतिपदार्थ १]* (*1 pratipadārtha 1]**); standard label inserted.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

नासत्या (*nāsatyā*) — O Aśvin deities free from untruth. ता वां (*tā vāṃ*) — you, who are famed as protectors. अद्य (*adya*) — on this day of the sacrifice. उषसि (*uṣasi*) — when the Uṣas deity. उच्छंत्यां (*ucchaṃtyāṃ*) — at the time of dispelling the darkness. हुवेम (*huvema*) — we call, praying (and likewise) अपरं (*aparaṃ*) — on other sacrifice days also, ता (*tā*) — we call those two. दिवः नपाता (*divaḥ napātā*) — O Aśvin deities, grandsons of the heavenly world. कुह चित् संतौ (*kuha cit saṃtau*) — wherever you may be. वह्निः (*vahniḥ*) — I, the bearer of praise, अर्यः (*aryaḥ*) — and the impeller of praise (the Hotṛ), सुदास्तराय (*sudāstarāya*) — for the sacrificer who offers excellent oblations, उक्षैः (*ukṣaiḥ*) — call with śastra mantras, praying.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Aśvin deities, grandsons of the heavenly world and free from untruth, you who are famed as protectors: on this day of the sacrifice, at the time when the Uṣas deity dispels the darkness, and also on other sacrifice days, wherever you may be, I, the bearer and impeller of praise (the Hotṛ), pray to you and call you with mantras in the form of śastras, for the sacrificer who offers excellent oblations.

**Printed English translation**

We invoke you two, (Ashwins), to-day ; the same two on any other day : when the morning dawns. Nasatyas, grandsons of heaven, wherever you may be, the pious reciter (of the hymn) invokes you with praises on behalf of the liberal donor (of the offering).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

The nature of the morning litany (*prātaranuvāka*) and of the śastra mantras, the occasion of reciting them, who must recite them, the time of recitation and other details, we have explained earlier, that is, in No. 1 of the Ṛ. Saṃ. part [as supplied: "ಋ. ಸಂ. ಭಾಗದ ೧ ರಲ್ಲಿ"].

### Printed p. 6

*Running head: [6 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 184].*

The Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya has stated that this sūkta has an application in the morning-litany Āśvina-śastra mantras. The story connected with the Āśvina-śastra is told in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa as follows.

प्रजापतिर्वै सोमाय राज्ञे दुहितरं प्रायच्छत्सूर्यां सावित्रीं तस्यै सर्वे (*prajāpatirvai somāya rājñe duhitaraṃ prāyacchatsūryāṃ sāvitrīṃ tasyai sarve*)
देवा वरा आगच्छंस्तस्या एतत्सहस्रं वहतुमन्वाकरोद्यदेतदाश्विनमित्या- (*devā varā āgacchaṃstasyā etatsahasraṃ vahatumanvākarodyadetadāśvinamityā-*)
चक्षतेऽनाश्विनं हैव तद्यदर्वाक्सहस्रं तस्मात्तत्सहस्रं वैव शंसेद्भूयो वा (*cakṣate 'nāśvinaṃ haiva tadyadarvāksahasraṃ tasmāttatsahasraṃ vaiva śaṃsedbhūyo vā*)
प्राश्य धृतं शंसेद्यथा ह वा इदमनो वा रथो वाक्तो वर्तत एवं है (*prāśya dhṛtaṃ śaṃsedyathā ha vā idamano vā ratho vākto vartata evaṃ hai*)
वाक्तो वर्तते । शकुनिरिवोत्पतिष्यन्नाह्वयीत । (*vākto vartate | śakunirivotpatiṣyannāhvayīta |*)

(Ai. Brā. 17-1 [the print has "ಇ ಬ್ರಾ." as supplied])

Formerly Prajāpati resolved to give his daughter Sūryā to King Soma in marriage, and then all the gods came with the wish of choosing her. Prajāpati then fixed a rule that the thousand mantras known as the Āśvina-śastra must be recited and that they must not fall short of that. As in the world a chariot wheel moves easily after it has been oiled, mantras can be recited easily after drinking ghee; so one must first drink ghee and then recite. And as a bird makes its body light when it is ready to fly, the suitor who woos her must also have that quality, he said.

तस्मिन्देवा न समजानत ममेदमस्तु ममेदमस्त्विति ते संजानाना अब्रु- (*tasmindevā na samajānata mamedamastu mamedamastviti te saṃjānānā abru-*)
वन्नाजिमस्यायामहै स यो न उज्वेष्यति तस्येदं भविष्यतीति तेऽग्नेरे- (*vannājimasyāyāmahai sa yo na ujveṣyati tasyedaṃ bhaviṣyatīti te 'gnere-*)
वाधि गृहपतेरादित्यं काष्ठामकुर्वत तस्मादाग्नेया प्रतिपद्भवत्याश्विन- (*vādhi gṛhapaterādityaṃ kāṣṭhāmakurvata tasmādāgneyā pratipadbhavatyāśvina-*)
स्याग्निर्होता गृहपतिः स राजेति । (*syāgnirhotā gṛhapatiḥ sa rājeti |*)

(Ai. Brā. 17-1 [as supplied "ಇ. ಬ್ರಾ."])

Then the gods, not wishing to fight one another, together decided that all should take part in a contest and that whoever won in that contest would be entitled to choose her. Then they ran from Agni, the household lord (*gṛhapati*), up to Sūra [as supplied].

तासां वै देवतानामाजिं धावंतीनामभिसृष्टानामग्निर्मुखं प्रथमः (*tāsāṃ vai devatānāmājiṃ dhāvaṃtīnāmabhisṛṣṭānāmagnirmukhaṃ prathamaḥ*)
प्रत्यपद्यत तमश्विनावन्वागच्छतां तमब्रूतामपोदिह्यावां वा इदं (*pratyapadyata tamaśvināvanvāgacchatāṃ tamabrūtāmapodihyāvāṃ vā idaṃ*)
जेष्याव इति स तथेत्यब्रवीत्तस्य वै ममेहाप्यस्त्विति तथेति तस्मा अप्यत्रा- (*jeṣyāva iti sa tathetyabravīttasya vai mamehāpyastviti tatheti tasmā apyatrā-*)
कुरुतां तस्मादाग्नेयमाश्विने शस्यते । (*kurutāṃ tasmādāgneyamāśvine śasyate |*)

(Ai. Brā. 17-2 [as supplied "ಇ ಬ್ರಾ."])

While all the gods were taking part in this contest and running, Agni got ahead of everyone. Then the Aśvin deities approached Agni and asked: "Give us the way; let us win in this contest; help us." Then Agni agreed, but asked that a share of the victory be given to him as well. For that very reason he too has a share in the Āśvina-śastra mantras.

### Printed p. 7

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 1] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, p. 7.*

In this context another objection is stated, and its answer is given.

तदाहुर्यच्छस्यत आग्नेयं शस्यत उषस्यं शस्यत ऐंद्रमथ कस्मादेतदाश्विन- (*tadāhuryacchasyata āgneyaṃ śasyata uṣasyaṃ śasyata aiṃdramatha kasmādetadāśvina-*)
मित्याचक्षत इत्यश्विन् । हि तदुदजयतामश्विनावाश्नुवातां यदश्विना उद- (*mityācakṣata ityaśvin | hi tadudajayatāmaśvināvāśnuvātāṃ yadaśvinā uda-*)
जयतामश्विनावाश्नुवातां तस्मादेतदाश्विनमित्याचक्षते । (*jayatāmaśvināvāśnuvātāṃ tasmādetadāśvinamityācakṣate |*)

(Ai. Brā. 17-2)

Since in this context mantras addressed to Agni, to Indra and to Uṣas also have to be recited, the objection arises how the name Āśvina-śastra came to all of these; the answer is that, because this is recited mainly with reference to the victory of the Aśvin deities, these mantras have this name.

In this context it is necessary to explain what the *prātaranuvāka* is. In performing the Agniṣṭoma sacrifice, the day on which the soma juice is pressed is called the *sutyā* or *stutyā* day. On the morning of this day, before sunrise, as soon as the Adhvaryu recites the *praiṣa* mantra देवेभ्यः (*devebhyaḥ*) प्रातरावभ्यो होतरनुब्रूहि (*prātarāvabhyo hotaranubrūhi*), the Hotṛ recites certain mantras. These are called *prātaranuvāka* mantras. The Hotṛ must recite these *prātaranuvāka* mantras. This is said in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa itself: देवेभ्यः प्रातरावभ्यो होतरनुब्रूहीत्या- (*devebhyaḥ prātarāvabhyo hotaranubrūhītyā-*) हाध्वर्युः (*hādhvaryuḥ*) (Ai. Brā 2. 15).

The procedure which the Hotṛ follows in reciting the *prātaranuvāka* mantras is now explained in detail. The Adhvaryu, who holds the chief ṛtvik office in the sacrifice, has to say again and again to the other ṛtviks, "You must do such and such a work"; such sentences are called *praiṣas*. In the present context the Adhvaryu addresses the Hotṛ: देवेभ्यः (*devebhyaḥ*) प्रातरावभ्यो होतरनुब्रूहि (*prātarāvabhyo hotaranubrūhi*), "O Hotṛ, recite the *prātaranuvāka* mantras addressed to the gods", he says. Then the Hotṛ goes near the fire-pit called अग्नीध्रवॆंब (*agnīdhravèṃba*) (*Āgnīdhra*), and there, for purification of speech (वाक्युद्धिगागि (*vākyuddhigāgi*) as supplied), he offers with the prescribed mantras one oblation of ghee, and in the Āhavanīya fire two oblations of ghee. Then he enters the hall called हविर्धानवॆंब (*havirdhānavèṃba*) (*havirdhāna*) by the eastern door, sits near the yoke of the carts there, and first recites this mantra.

आपो रेवतीः क्षयथा हि वस्वः क्रतुं च भद्रं बिभृतामृतं च । (*āpo revatīḥ kṣayathā hi vasvaḥ kratuṃ ca bhadraṃ bibhṛtāmṛtaṃ ca |*)
रायश्च स्थ स्वपत्यस्य पत्निः सरस्वती तद्गृणते वयो धात् ॥ (*rāyaśca stha svapatyasya patniḥ sarasvatī tadgṛṇate vayo dhāt ‖*)

(Pu. Saṃ. 10. 30. 12. [as supplied: "ಪು. ಸಂ."])

*[The window ends here; the account of the prātaranuvāka recitation continues on the next page.]*

### Printed p. 8

*Running head: [8 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24 Sū. 184]; the print has "ಸ್ಕೂ." for the usual "ಸೂ." as supplied.*

Thereafter the Hotṛ recites the *prātaranuvāka* mantras addressed to Agni, Uṣas and the Aśvin deities. These are in three parts. The mantras said to Agni are called आग्नेयक्रतुवॆंदू. (*āgneyakratuvèṃdū.*) [as supplied, "Āgneya-kratu"], the mantras recited to Uṣas उषस्यक्रतुवॆंदू (*uṣasyakratuvèṃdū*), and the mantras recited to the Aśvin deities आश्विन (*āśvina*) क्रतुवॆंदू (*kratuvèṃdū*). To show that the mantras addressed to each deity must be accompanied by seven metres there is the repetition (*vīpsā*) सप्तभिः सप्तभिः छंदोभिः (*saptabhiḥ saptabhiḥ chaṃdobhiḥ*) (it is stated twice). In accordance with it the explanation of these mantras is as follows.

*(The supplied text gives the following lists as HTML tables of ṛk beginnings, references and metres; they are reproduced here as lists, the references as printed. The sign ",," of the print means "ditto" for "Ṛ. Saṃ.", and for the metre column.)*

**Agni (Āgneya-kratu), seven ṛks, seven metres:**

- उप प्रयंतः (*upa prayaṃtaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-74-1) — metre: इत्यादिषु चैंदो गायत्रं (*ityādiṣu caiṃdo gāyatraṃ*)
- त्वमग्ने वसून् (*tvamagne vasūn*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-45-1) — metre: अनुष्टुप् छंदः (*anuṣṭup chaṃdaḥ*)
- अबोध्यग्निः (*abodhyagniḥ*) (ditto 5-1-1) — metre: त्रिष्टुप् (*triṣṭup*)
- एना वो अग्निं (*enā vo agniṃ*) (ditto 7-16-1) — metre: बृहती (*bṛhatī*)
- अग्ने वाजस्य (*agne vājasya*) (ditto 1-29-4) — metre: उष्टिक् (*uṣṭik*)
- जनस्य गोपा (*janasya gopā*) (ditto 5-11-1) — metre: जगती (*jagatī*)
- अग्निं तं मन्ये (*agniṃ taṃ manye*) (ditto 5-6-1) — metre: पंक्तिः (*paṃktiḥ*)

तान्येतानि सप्तच्छंदासाग्नेये क्रत्वा प्रातरनुवाके द्रष्टव्यानि । (*tānyetāni saptacchaṃdāsāgneye kratvā prātaranuvāke draṣṭavyāni |*)

These seven sūkta-mantras, accompanied by seven metres, are the mantras connected with the Āgneya-kratu in the *prātaranuvāka* addressed to the Agni deity.

**Uṣas (Uṣasya-kratu), seven ṛks, seven metres:**

- प्रति ष्या सूनरी (*prati ṣyā sūnarī*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-52-1) — metre: इत्यादिषु गायत्री छंदः (*ityādiṣu gāyatrī chaṃdaḥ*)
- उषो भद्रेभिः (*uṣo bhadrebhiḥ*) (ditto 1-49-1) — metre: अनुष्टुप् (*anuṣṭup*)
- इदं श्रेष्ठः (*idaṃ śreṣṭhaḥ*) (ditto 1-113-1) — metre: त्रिष्टुप् (*triṣṭup*)
- प्रत्यु आदर्शि (*pratyu ādarśi*) (ditto 7-82-1) — metre: बृहती (*bṛhatī*)
- उषस्तच्चित्रमाभर (*uṣastaccitramābhara*) (ditto 1-92-13) — metre: उष्णिक् (*uṣṇik*)
- एता उ त्या (*etā u tyā*) (ditto 1-92-1) — metre: जगती (*jagatī*)
- महे नो अद्य (*mahe no adya*) (ditto 5-79-1) — metre: पंक्तिः (*paṃktiḥ*)

तान्येतान्युषसः प्रातरनुवाके सप्तच्छंदांसि (*tānyetānyuṣasaḥ prātaranuvāke saptacchaṃdāṃsi*)

These seven are addressed to the Uṣas deity and are mantras in the *prātaranuvāka* connected with the Uṣasya-kratu, and,

**Aśvins (Āśvina-kratu), seven ṛks, seven metres:**

- एषो उषा (*eṣo uṣā*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-46-1) — metre: इत्यादिषु गायत्री च्छंदः (*ityādiṣu gāyatrī cchaṃdaḥ*)
- यदद्य (*yadadya*) (ditto 5-73-1) — metre: अनुष्टुप् (*anuṣṭup*)
- आभातृग्निः (*ābhātṛgniḥ*) (ditto 5-76-1) — metre: त्रिष्टुप् (*triṣṭup*)
- इमा उ वां (*imā u vāṃ*) (ditto 7-74-1) — metre: बृहती (*bṛhatī*)
- अश्विना वर्तिः (*aśvinā vartiḥ*) (ditto 1-92-16) — metre: उष्णिक् (*uṣṇik*)

### Printed p. 9

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 1. Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 9.*

- अबोध्यग्निर्जृ (*abodhyagnirjṛ*) (ditto 1-57-1) — metre: जगती (*jagatī*)
- प्रति प्रियतमं (*prati priyatamaṃ*) (ditto 5-75-1) — metre: पंक्तिः (*paṃktiḥ*)

तान्येतान्याश्विने प्रातरनुवाके सप्तच्छंदांसि । (*tānyetānyāśvine prātaranuvāke saptacchaṃdāṃsi |*)

These seven are the *prātaranuvāka* mantras addressed to the Aśvin deities and connected with the Āśvina-kratu. The greatness of these *prātaranuvāka* mantras, with its spiritual background, is stated in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa, as seen below.

देवेभ्यः प्रातर्यावभ्यो होतरनुब्रूहीत्याहाध्वर्युः । एते नाव देवाः (*devebhyaḥ prātaryāvabhyo hotaranubrūhītyāhādhvaryuḥ | ete nāva devāḥ*)
प्रातर्याणो यदग्निरुषा अश्विनौ त एते सप्तभिः सप्तभिश्चंदोभिराग (*prātaryāṇo yadagniruṣā aśvinau ta ete saptabhiḥ saptabhiścaṃdobhirāga*)
च्छंति । आस्य देवाः प्रातर्याणो हवं गच्छंति य एवं वेद । प्रजापतौ (*cchaṃti | āsya devāḥ prātaryāṇo havaṃ gacchaṃti ya evaṃ veda | prajāpatau*)
वै स्वयं होतरि प्रातरनुवाकमनुवक्ष्यत्युभये देवासुरा (*vai svayaṃ hotari prātaranuvākamanuvakṣyatyubhaye devāsurā*)
यज्ञमुपावसन्नस्मभ्यमनुवक्ष्यत्यस्मभ्यमिति स वै देवेभ्य एवान्वब्रवीत् । (*yajñamupāvasannasmabhyamanuvakṣyatyasmabhyamiti sa vai devebhya evānvabravīt |*)
ततो वै देवा अभवन्परासुराः भवत्यात्मना परास्य द्विषन् पाप्मा भ्रातृव्यो (*tato vai devā abhavanparāsurāḥ bhavatyātmanā parāsya dviṣan pāpmā bhrātṛvyo*)
भवति य एवं वेद। प्रातर्वै स तं देवेभ्योऽन्वब्रवीद्यत्पातरनुवाकस्य (*bhavati ya evaṃ veda| prātarvai sa taṃ devebhyo 'nvabravīdyatpātaranuvākasya*)
प्रातरनुवाकत्वं । महति रात्रा अनूच्यः सर्वस्य वाचः सर्वस्य ब्रह्मणः (*prātaranuvākatvaṃ | mahati rātrā anūcyaḥ sarvasya vācaḥ sarvasya brahmaṇaḥ*)
परिगृहीत्यै यो वै भवति यः श्रेष्ठतामश्नुते तस्य वाचं प्रोदितामनु- (*parigṛhītyai yo vai bhavati yaḥ śreṣṭhatāmaśnute tasya vācaṃ proditāmanu-*)
प्रवदंति तस्मान्महति रात्रा अनूच्यः .... .... .... .... .... .... .... (*pravadaṃti tasmānmahati rātrā anūcyaḥ .... .... .... .... .... .... ....*)
यदा ना अध्वरुरुपाकरोति वाचैवोपाकरोति वाचा होतान्नाह वाग्नि (*yadā nā adhvarurupākaroti vācaivopākaroti vācā hotānnāha vāgni*)
ब्रह्म तत्र स काम उपाप्तो यो वाचि च ब्रह्मणि च ॥ (*brahma tatra sa kāma upāpto yo vāci ca brahmaṇi ca ‖*)

(Ai. Brā. 7-5 [the print has "ಇ ಬರಾ," as supplied]; the supplied text has rows of dots in the middle of the quotation, and its spelling प्रातर्यावभ्यो (*prātaryāvabhyo*) (with *rya*) differs from the spelling of the same word in the praiṣa quoted on p. 7; kept as supplied)

Agni, Uṣas and the Aśvin deities are the gods who move about in the morning. Each of these deities has seven metres. These gods come and join the man who knows this principle. Once, when Prajāpati himself was the Hotṛ, the gods on one side and the asuras on the other were both initiated for the sacrifice, and both groups thought that Prajāpati would recite the *prātaranuvāka* for their sacrifice. But Prajāpati recited it only for the gods. By this the gods won and the asuras were defeated. Because it was recited in the morning (*prātaḥ*) it became famous as *prātaranuvāka*; and for its power to appear fully it must be recited after the deep middle of the night, before the light spreads. Since speech (*vāk*) is itself of the form of Brahma, the desired fruit is obtained by reciting it.

प्रजापतौ स्वयं होतरि प्रातरनुवाकमनुवक्ष्यति सर्वा देवता आशंसंत- (*prajāpatau svayaṃ hotari prātaranuvākamanuvakṣyati sarvā devatā āśaṃsaṃta-*)
मामभि प्रतिपत्स्यति मामभीति स प्रजाप्रतिरैक्षत यद्येकां देवतामादिष्टा (*māmabhi pratipatsyati māmabhīti sa prajāpratiraikṣata yadyekāṃ devatāmādiṣṭā*)

*(The quotation is broken here by the page boundary; the word at the end of p. 9, देवतामादिष्टा (*devatāmādiṣṭā*), is completed at the head of p. 10.)*

### Printed p. 10

*Running head: [10 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1 A. 24 Sū. 184].*

मभि प्रतिपत्स्यामीतरामेकेन देवता उपाप्ता भविष्यंतीति स एतामृ (*mabhi pratipatsyāmītarāmekena devatā upāptā bhaviṣyaṃtīti sa etāmṛ*)
चमपश्यदापो रेवतीरित्यापो वै सर्वा देवता रेवत्यः सर्वा देवताः स (*camapaśyadāpo revatīrityāpo vai sarvā devatā revatyaḥ sarvā devatāḥ sa*)
एतयर्चा प्रातरनुवाकं प्रत्यपद्यत ताः सर्वा देवताः प्रामोदंत (*etayarcā prātaranuvākaṃ pratyapadyata tāḥ sarvā devatāḥ prāmodaṃta*)
मामभिप्रत्यपादि मामभीति । सर्वा हास्मिन् देवताः प्रातरनुवाकमनु- (*māmabhipratyapādi māmabhīti | sarvā hāsmin devatāḥ prātaranuvākamanu-*)
ब्रुवति प्रमोदंते सर्वाभिर्हास्य देवताभिः प्रातरनुवाकः प्रतिपन्नो (*bruvati pramodaṃte sarvābhirhāsya devatābhiḥ prātaranuvākaḥ pratipanno*)
भवति य एवं वेद । (*bhavati ya evaṃ veda |*)

(Ai. Brā. 7-6)

When Prajāpati, being Hotṛ, was ready to recite the *prātaranuvāka*, each of the gods waited eagerly, thinking that he would recite addressing them. Then Prajāpati fell into a dilemma: if he addressed only one deity, how would he gain the other? Thinking so, in the end he recited, addressing water (*apaḥ*), which is of the nature of all deities, the mantra

आपो रेवतीः क्षयथा हि वस्वः क्रतुं च भद्रं बिभृतामृतं च । (*āpo revatīḥ kṣayathā hi vasvaḥ kratuṃ ca bhadraṃ bibhṛtāmṛtaṃ ca |*)
रायश्च स्थ स्वपत्यस्य पत्निः सरस्वती तद्गृणते वयोधात् ॥ (*rāyaśca stha svapatyasya patniḥ sarasvatī tadgṛṇate vayodhāt ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10. 30. 12)

By this all the gods were satisfied. Therefore the praiser who recites the *prātaranuvāka* mantra knowing this principle becomes worthy of the favour of all the gods together and obtains every desired thing.

तिस्रो देवता अन्वाह त्रयो ना इमे त्रिवृतो लोका एषामेव लोका. (*tisro devatā anvāha trayo nā ime trivṛto lokā eṣāmeva lokā.*)
नामभिजित्यै । (*nāmabhijityai |*)

(Ai. Brā. 7-8 [the print has "ಇ. ಬ್ರಾ," as supplied])

Since Agni, Uṣas and the Aśvin deities, these three alone, are the lords of the three worlds, the mantras recited addressed to them become the means of gaining the three worlds; this being so, another doubt arises here. Anticipating that doubt, the answer is stated thus.

तदाहुः कथमनूच्यः प्रातरनुवाक इति। यथाच्छंदसमनूच्यः प्रातरनुवाकः (*tadāhuḥ kathamanūcyaḥ prātaranuvāka iti| yathācchaṃdasamanūcyaḥ prātaranuvākaḥ*)
प्रजापतेर्वा एतान्यंगानि यच्छंदांस्येष उ एव प्रजापतिर्यो यजते (*prajāpatervā etānyaṃgāni yacchaṃdāṃsyeṣa u eva prajāpatiryo yajate*)
यद्यजमानाय हितं । पच्चोऽनूच्यः प्रातरनुवाकश्चतुष्पादा वै पशवः (*yadyajamānāya hitaṃ | pacco 'nūcyaḥ prātaranuvākaścatuṣpādā vai paśavaḥ*)
पशूनामवरुद्धैः । . . . . . . . आहुतिभागा वा अन्या (*paśūnāmavaruddhaiḥ | . . . . . . . āhutibhāgā vā anyā*)
देवता अन्याः स्तोमभागाश्चंदोभागास्ता या अग्नावाहुतयो हू- (*devatā anyāḥ stomabhāgāścaṃdobhāgāstā yā agnāvāhutayo hū-*)

*[The window ends mid-sentence at the end of p. 10 (the last word is broken with a hyphen); the quotation and its explanation continue on the next page.]*

### Printed p. 11

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 1.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 11.*

*(The Aitareya Brāhmaṇa quotation of p. 10 continues; the word broken at the page boundary, "…ಹೂ-" on p. 10, is completed by "ಯಂತೇ" here.)*

यंते ताभिराहुतिभागाः प्रीणातृथ यत्‌स्तुवंति च शंसंति च तेन (*yaṃte tābhirāhutibhāgāḥ prīṇātṛtha yatstuvaṃti ca śaṃsaṃti ca tena*)
स्तोमभागाश्चंदोभागाः । उभयो हास्यैता देवताः प्रीता अभीष्टा (*stomabhāgāścaṃdobhāgāḥ | ubhayo hāsyaitā devatāḥ prītā abhīṣṭā*)
भवंति य एवं वेद । (*bhavaṃti ya evaṃ veda |*)

(Ai. Brā. 7-8 [the print has "ಇ. ಬ್ರಾ." as supplied])

To the question what the order of reciting the *prātaranuvāka* is, the scriptural order is to recite it in verse form and following the order of the fixed metres. The reason: the metres are the limbs of Prajāpati; Prajāpati himself is the sacrificer; and animals are footed creatures, so, in order to obtain them, the *prātaranuvāka* must be recited in the order of the feet (*pāda*). Having thus told the order of recitation, the text anticipates another doubt. It is this:

अभूदुषा रुषत्पशुरित्युत्तमया परिदधाति । तदाहुरत्रीन्यतूनन्वाहा- (*abhūduṣā ruṣatpaśurityuttamayā paridadhāti | tadāhuratrīnyatūnanvāhā-*)
ग्नेयमुषस्यमाश्विनं कथमस्यैकयर्चा परिदधतः सर्वे त्रयः त्रतवः (*gneyamuṣasyamāśvinaṃ kathamasyaikayarcā paridadhataḥ sarve trayaḥ tratavaḥ*)
परिहिता भवंतीति । अभूदुषा रुषत्पशुरित्युषसो रूपमाग्निरधाय्यत्विय (*parihitā bhavaṃtīti | abhūduṣā ruṣatpaśurityuṣaso rūpamāgniradhāyyatviya*)
इत्यग्नेरयोजि वां वृषण्वसू रथो दस्रावमर्त्यो माध्वी मम श्रुतं (*ityagnerayoji vāṃ vṛṣaṇvasū ratho dasrāvamartyo mādhvī mama śrutaṃ*)
हवमित्यश्विनोरेवमुहास्यैकयर्चा परिदधतः सर्वे त्रयः क्रतवः परिहिता (*havamityaśvinorevamuhāsyaikayarcā paridadhataḥ sarve trayaḥ kratavaḥ parihitā*)
भवंति भवंति । (*bhavaṃti bhavaṃti |*)

(Ai. Brā. 7-8 [as supplied "ಇ. ಬ್ರಾ,"])

Since the *prātaranuvāka* has to be recited addressed to all these deities, Agni, Uṣas and the Aśvin deities, in the present case

“ अभूदुषा रुशत्पशुराग्नि रधाय्यैत्वियः । ” (*“ abhūduṣā ruśatpaśurāgni radhāyyaitviyaḥ | ”*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed म. सं. (*ma. saṃ.*)] 5. 75 9 [as supplied; the sigla "ಮ. ಸಂ." for Ṛ. Saṃ.])

when the conclusion is told with this single mantra, the question arises how all the rites addressed to all the deities are completed; the answer shows, by explaining, that in this very mantra the praise of the three deities is present. This is the greatness of the *prātaranuvāka*. Because mantras having this greatness have to be recited at dawn, Sāyaṇa has stated in the explanation of the present ṛk “ उषः काले हि प्रातरनुवाकाश्विनशस्त्रे प्रयुज्येते ” (*“ uṣaḥ kāle hi prātaranuvākāśvinaśastre prayujyete ”*). By उषसि उच्छंत्यां (*uṣasi ucchaṃtyāṃ*) the prayer addressed to the Aśvin deities is told. The mantra that indicates their time of travel to the sacrificial ground,

अबोध्यग्निर्ज्म उदेति सूर्यो व्यु १ षाश्चंद्रा मह्यावो अर्चिषा । (*abodhyagnirjma udeti sūryo vyu 1 ṣāścaṃdrā mahyāvo arciṣā |*)
आयुक्षातामश्विना यातवे रथं प्रासावीद्देवः सविता जगत्पृथक् ॥ (*āyukṣātāmaśvinā yātave rathaṃ prāsāvīddevaḥ savitā jagatpṛthak ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed म. सं. (*ma. saṃ.*)] 1. 157 1 [as supplied]; the first line is printed in the supplied text with garbled passages (अबोध्यग्निर्ज्म (*abodhyagnirjma*) and व्यु १ (*vyu 1*)), kept as supplied)

### Printed p. 12

*Running head: [12 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 1-4]; the print has "೧-೪" for the sūkta number, as supplied.*

in this ṛk the prayer is made thus: on the altar Agni is blazing; the Āditya also rises; the great goddess Uṣas, with her radiance, is making all the world glad. Therefore, O Aśvin deities, yoke your chariot with horses in order to go to the sacrificial ground. As it is thus prayed, so in the present ṛk too the prayer is made. And whenever their time of travel to the sacrificial ground is told, the blazing of Agni, the appearance of Uṣas and the rising of Sūrya are indicated. Telling this same point, the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa says:

देवेभ्यः प्रातर्यावभ्यो होतरनुब्रूहित्याहाध्वर्युः । एते वाव देवाः (*devebhyaḥ prātaryāvabhyo hotaranubrūhityāhādhvaryuḥ | ete vāva devāḥ*)
प्रातर्यापाणो यदग्निरुषा अश्विनौ त एते सप्तभिः सप्तभिश्चंदोभिरागच्छंति ॥ (*prātaryāpāṇo yadagniruṣā aśvinau ta ete saptabhiḥ saptabhiścaṃdobhirāgacchaṃti ‖*)

(Ai. Brā. [printed इ इ३, (*i i3,*)] 7-5 [as supplied; the abbreviation is garbled])

In the mantra cited above it is said that the Adhvaryu instructs the Hotṛ to pray, with the *prātaranuvāka* mantras, that Agni, Uṣas and the Aśvin deities come to the sacrificial house at dawn. The same point is described in the present ṛk, indicating the appearance of Uṣas.

For the words उषसि उच्छंति (*uṣasi ucchaṃti*) (as supplied) one meaning is "scattering and destroying the darkness", and another is "shining with radiance". The derivation of the word उषा (*uṣā*) too is through उच्चति (*uccati*). By the sentence उषाः कस्मादुच्छतीति सत्या (*uṣāḥ kasmāducchatīti satyā*) रात्रेरपरः कालः (*rātreraparaḥ kālaḥ*) the Nirukta authors have stated this derivation (Ni. 2. 18). But by the sentence उषा वष्टेः कांतिकर्मण उच्छतेरितरा माध्यमिका (*uṣā vaṣṭeḥ kāṃtikarmaṇa ucchateritarā mādhyamikā*) (Ni 12 7.) the Nirukta authors have also stated that the word शब्दवु (*śabdavu*) may be formed from the root वश कांत् (*vaśa kāṃt*) or from the root उच्छे विवासे (*ucche vivāse*). In the present ṛk the words उषसि उच्छंत्यां (*uṣasi ucchaṃtyāṃ*) may mean "the Uṣas who fully scatters the darkness and appears outside" or "the Uṣas shining with radiance".

दिवः नपाता—नपात् (*divaḥ napātā—napāt*) — the word नपाता—नपात् (*napātā—napāt*) can mean son and also grandson. The Nirukta authors, by the sentence नपादित्यनंतरायाः प्रजायाः नामधेयं (*napādityanaṃtarāyāḥ prajāyāḥ nāmadheyaṃ*) (Ni. 8. 5.), have stated that the word means not the actual son but grandsons and the like. In the present case Sāyaṇa has given for दिवः (*divaḥ*) the sense नप्तृस्थानीयौ (*naptṛsthānīyau*) (the word द्युलो- (*dyulo-*) is broken across the line in the supplied text). The origin of the Aśvin deities is told differently in different places. For example,

### Printed p. 13

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 1] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 13.*

इहेह जाता समवादशीतामरेपसा तन्वा ३ नामभिः स्वैः । (*iheha jātā samavādaśītāmarepasā tanvā 3 nāmabhiḥ svaiḥ |*)
जिष्णुर्वामन्यः सुमखस्य सूरिर्दिवो अन्यः सुभगः पुत्र ऊहे ॥ (*jiṣṇurvāmanyaḥ sumakhasya sūrirdivo anyaḥ subhagaḥ putra ūhe ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed म. सं. (*ma. saṃ.*)] 1. 181. 4 [as supplied])

In this ṛk it is described that, of the Aśvin deities, one, as the son of the middle world and in the form of the moon, and the other, as the son of the heavenly world and in the form of the sun, are protectors of the world. It is known in some further contexts that of these two, one deity has the name Nāsatya and the other the name Dasra. This explanation has been given earlier, in the special topics of the fourth ṛk of Ṛ. Saṃ. 1. 181.

नासत्या—नासत्या चाश्विन् । सत्या वेव नासत्यावित्यौर्णवाभः सत्यस्य प्रणेतारा (*nāsatyā—nāsatyā cāśvin | satyā veva nāsatyāvityaurṇavābhaḥ satyasya praṇetārā*)
वित्याग्रायणः । नासिका प्रभवौ बभूवतुरिति वा । (*vityāgrāyaṇaḥ | nāsikā prabhavau babhūvaturiti vā |*)

(Ni. 6 ग) (*ga)*) [as supplied])

According to the view of Aurṇanābha, those who are of the nature of truth and not untrue are Nāsatyas; according to Āgrāyaṇa, because they create and increase truth they are Nāsatyas; or because they were born from the nose they are Nāsatyas. The Nirukta authors have thus stated, each according to one opinion, one way each, since this peculiarity applies to the Aśvin deities.

अरः - ईरयिता स्तुतेरीश्वरो ना ; (*araḥ - īrayitā stuterīśvaro nā ;*) — the sense is explained as "stimulator of praise, or master (*īśvara*) of praise-making". This is an epithet of the Hotṛ. By this epithet the Hotṛ shows his own devotion and faith: "I who praise most excellently." The Nirukta authors have read this word among the names of Īśvara (Ni 3. 11), and the word is used in the sense of Īśvara:

प्रतत्ते अद्य शिपिविष्ट नामार्यः शंसामि वयुनानि विद्वान् । (*pratatte adya śipiviṣṭa nāmāryaḥ śaṃsāmi vayunāni vidvān |*)
तं त्वा गृणामि तवसमतव्यान् क्षयंतमस्य रजसः पराके ॥ (*taṃ tvā gṛṇāmi tavasamatavyān kṣayaṃtamasya rajasaḥ parāke ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed म. सं. (*ma. saṃ.*)] 7 100 5 [as supplied])

Having cited this ṛk, they explain: अर्योऽहमीश्वरः स्तोमानामरस्यमसीति वा (*aryo 'hamīśvaraḥ stomānāmarasyamasīti vā*) (Ni. 5. 9). Here the Vṛttikāra has explained, with यस्मादरोऽहमस्मि ईश्वरः स्तुतीनामुदीरणे (*yasmādaro 'hamasmi īśvaraḥ stutīnāmudīraṇe*) युष्मद्गुणाभिज्ञः अथवा अर्यस्त्वमसि अर्य ईश्वरः मदनुग्रहाय समर्थः । (*yuṣmadguṇābhijñaḥ athavā aryastvamasi arya īśvaraḥ madanugrahāya samarthaḥ |*), that "I am able to make praise and to know your qualities; or you are able to bestow grace." The use of this word in the sense "worshipful" occurs in many places. For example,

*[The window ends at "For example"; the examples continue on the next page.]*

### Printed p. 14

*Running head: [14 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 184].*

पृथू रथो दक्षिणाया अयोज्यैनं देवासो अमृतासो अस्तुः । (*pṛthū ratho dakṣiṇāyā ayojyainaṃ devāso amṛtāso astuḥ |*)
कृष्णादुदस्थादर्या ३ विहायाश्चिकित्यंती मानुषाय क्षयाय ॥ (*kṛṣṇādudasthādaryā 3 vihāyāścikityaṃtī mānuṣāya kṣayāya ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 1. 123. 1.)

In this ṛk, in describing the qualities of the Uṣas deity understood from the context, there is the epithet अर्या (*aryā*). Sāyaṇa has given this word the sense पूज्या (*pūjyā*) ("worshipful"), and its use is more frequent in the sense of सर्वॆश्वर (*sarvèśvara*) ऎंबर्थदल्लिये (*èṃbarthadalliye*) ("Lord of all", as supplied).

मघवाना (*maghavānā*) — for this epithet Sāyaṇa has given the sense हविर्लक्षणान्नयुक्तौ (*havirlakṣaṇānnayuktau*), "endowed with food in the form of an oblation" (the Aśvin deities). Ordinarily the epithet मघवन् (*maghavan*) refers to Indra, but even when the qualities of other deities are described, this epithet is used here and there in the Ṛksaṃhitā. Or, since the word मघ (*magha*) is read among the names of wealth (Ni 3. 9.), here it can be called wealth in the form of food. The Nirukta authors, having stated the derivation of the word मघ (*magha*) by मघमिति धननामधेयं महतेर्दानकर्मणः (*maghamiti dhananāmadheyaṃ mahaterdānakarmaṇaḥ*), have explained the sense of the word मघोनी (*maghonī*) (Ni 1 7). In the same way, in the present ṛk too an etymology can be given for the epithet मघवाना (*maghavānā*). Or:

If मघवाना (*maghavānā*) is taken to mean "the Aśvin deities accompanied by Viṣṇu", that will not be wrong either. How: the identity of Viṣṇu and Makha, stated in the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa and elsewhere, has been told earlier (Śa. Brā. 14. 1. 1. 13). The companionship of the Aśvin deities with Viṣṇu, who has the form of Makha, is famous. This too has been told earlier,

तमस्य राजा वरुणस्तमश्विना क्रतुं संचत मारुतस्य वेधसः । (*tamasya rājā varuṇastamaśvinā kratuṃ saṃcata mārutasya vedhasaḥ |*)
दधार दक्षमुत्तममहर्विदं व्रजं च विष्णुः सखिवा अपोर्णुते ॥ (*dadhāra dakṣamuttamamaharvidaṃ vrajaṃ ca viṣṇuḥ sakhivā aporṇute ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 1. 156. 4.)

in explaining the special topics of this ṛk (Ṛgveda Saṃhitā, part 11; pp. 656 to 657 [printed in Latin digits with a hyphen]) [the supplied text prints "।ವಿವರಿಸುವಾಗ" with a stray stroke]. Moreover, in the Ṛksaṃhitā itself this companionship is described thus:

यदिंद्रेण सरथं याथो अश्विना यद्वा वायुना भवधः समोकसा । (*yadiṃdreṇa sarathaṃ yātho aśvinā yadvā vāyunā bhavadhaḥ samokasā |*)
वयादित्येभिर्यभुभिः सजोषसा यद्वा विष्णोर्विक्रमणेषु तिष्यथः ॥ (*vayādityebhiryabhubhiḥ sajoṣasā yadvā viṣṇorvikramaṇeṣu tiṣyathaḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ ಸಂ.] 8 9. 12.)

### Printed p. 15

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 1.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 15 (the print has "ಆ:" for the second "ಅ." as supplied).*

In ṛks like this too, the companionship of the Aśvin deities with Viṣṇu is stated. For this reason it is possible to take मघवाना (*maghavānā*) to mean "the Aśvin deities accompanied by Viṣṇu". But in the present context it seems fitting to the occasion to take the sense stated by Sāyaṇa, "endowed with the wealth of food in the form of an oblation".

सुवृक्ति (*suvṛkti*) — सुष्टु पापवर्जनं यथाभवति तथा । यद्वा । (*suṣṭu pāpavarjanaṃ yathābhavati tathā | yadvā |*) सुसमाप्ति (*susamāpti*) (Kannada: complete freedom from sin, or having a pure conclusion). This word is an epithet of the word for *stoma*. This epithet is used in one sense in one place and in another sense in another. The Nirukta authors, citing

इयं शुष्मेभिर्बिसखा इवारुजत्सानु गिरीणां तविषेभिरूर्मिभिः । (*iyaṃ śuṣmebhirbisakhā ivārujatsānu girīṇāṃ taviṣebhirūrmibhiḥ |*)
पारावतघ्नीमवसे सुवृक्तिभिः सरस्वतीमा विवासेम धीतिभिः ॥ (*pārāvataghnīmavase suvṛktibhiḥ sarasvatīmā vivāsema dhītibhiḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಪು. ಸಂ] 6 61. 2),

have taken the word सुवृक्तिभिः (*suvṛktibhiḥ*) there as सुप्रवृत्ताभिः शोभनाभिः स्तुतिभिः (*supravṛttābhiḥ śobhanābhiḥ stutibhiḥ*), that is, simply as excellent praise. The word व्यक्तिशब्दवु (*vyaktiśabdavu*) is also used in other places in the sense of abandoning. For example,

कुविदंग यवमंतो यवं चिद्यथा दांत्यनुपूर्वं वियूय । (*kuvidaṃga yavamaṃto yavaṃ cidyathā dāṃtyanupūrvaṃ viyūya |*)
इहेहैषां कृणुहि भोजनानि ये बर्हिषो नमोवृक्तिं न जग्मुः ॥ (*ihehaiṣāṃ kṛṇuhi bhojanāni ye barhiṣo namovṛktiṃ na jagmuḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10. 131. 2)

here, in नमोव्यक्तिं (*namovyaktiṃ*) नमसः हनिर्लक्षणस्यान्नस्य नमस्कारात्मकस्य स्तोत्रस्य वा (*namasaḥ hanirlakṣaṇasyānnasya namaskārātmakasya stotrasya vā*) वर्जनम- (*varjanama-*) करणं न जग्मुः (*karaṇaṃ na jagmuḥ*): "they did not abandon the praise in the form of salutation": thus Indra is prayed to. Here the word व्यक्तिशब्दवु (*vyaktiśabdavu*) (as supplied; the verse has the spelling नमोव्यक्तिं (*namovyaktiṃ*)) is used in the sense of abandoning. But its use in the sense of praise is far more frequent. For example,

अस्मा इदु त्र्यमुपमं स्वर्षां भराम्यांगूषमास्येन । (*asmā idu tryamupamaṃ svarṣāṃ bharāmyāṃgūṣamāsyena |*)
वंहिष्ठमच्छोक्तिभिर्मतीनां सुवृक्तिभिः सूरिं नावृधध्यै ॥ (*vaṃhiṣṭhamacchoktibhirmatīnāṃ suvṛktibhiḥ sūriṃ nāvṛdhadhyai ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಪು. ಸಂ.] 1. 61. 3.)

In places like this everywhere the sense is "excellent praise".

वर्तिः (*vartiḥ*) — वर्तनहेतुभूतं गृहं (*vartanahetubhūtaṃ gṛhaṃ*) — Sāyaṇa has taken the sense "a house serving as a basis for dwelling" in all places where this word occurs. But Skandasvāmin has generally everywhere taken the sense "a well-known path". For example,

### Printed p. 16

*Running head: [16 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24 Sū. 184].*

त्रिवर्तिर्यातं त्रिरनुव्रते जने त्रिः सुप्राव्ये त्रेधेव शिक्षतं । (*trivartiryātaṃ triranuvrate jane triḥ suprāvye tredheva śikṣataṃ |*)
त्रिर्नांद्यं वहतमश्विना युवं त्रिः पृक्षो अस्मे अक्षरेव पिन्वतं ॥ (*trirnāṃdyaṃ vahatamaśvinā yuvaṃ triḥ pṛkṣo asme akṣareva pinvataṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1. 34. 4.)

In all ṛks like this, for the word वर्तियॆंबुदक्कॆ (*vartiyèṃbudakkè*) Sāyaṇa has given the sense "house" and Skandasvāmin the sense "path". It is one special point that all the sūktas having ṛks in which the word occurs in forms such as त्रिवर्तिः, (*trivartiḥ,*) and अश्विनावर्तिः (*aśvināvartiḥ*) (1.1-6.18 [printed with spaces and a range sign, as supplied]; 1. 117. 2; 1. 119. 4) are addressed to the Aśvin deities. But,

इषं दुहन्तु दुघां विश्वधायसं यज्ञप्रिये यजमानाय सुक्रतो । (*iṣaṃ duhantu dughāṃ viśvadhāyasaṃ yajñapriye yajamānāya sukrato |*)
अग्ने धृतस्नु स्त्रिर्यतानि दीद्यद्वर्तिर्यज्ञं परियन्तु सुक्रतूयसे ॥ (*agne dhṛtasnu striryatāni dīdyadvartiryajñaṃ pariyantu sukratūyase ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10. 122. 6)

only in this ṛk, a mantra addressed to Agni, does this word occur. Here it is explained as the house of the sacrifice. Sāyaṇa too has in some places taken the sense "path" for this word. For example,

यो ह स्य वां रथिरा वस्त उस्रा रथो युजानः परियाति वर्तिः । (*yo ha sya vāṃ rathirā vasta usrā ratho yujānaḥ pariyāti vartiḥ |*)
तेन नः शंयोरुषसो व्युष्टौ न्यश्विना वहतं यज्ञे अस्मिन् ॥ (*tena naḥ śaṃyoruṣaso vyuṣṭau nyaśvinā vahataṃ yajñe asmin ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7. 6) (Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 7. 69. 5.) [both references as supplied]

here वर्तिः (*vartiḥ*) is explained as मार्गं (*mārgaṃ*) ("path"). This is a mantra praying to the Aśvin deities themselves. In the present ṛk the sense is "house". Ordinarily this is a synonym of the word for the house of the sacrifice.

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page, noted briefly)

ता (*tā*) (*tad*, dual): the substitution of *a* by the rule त्यदादीनामः (*tyadādīnāmaḥ*) (no sūtra number printed), अतोगुणे (*atoguṇe*) for the single form, and the elision of the case ending, सुपांसुलुक् (*supāṃsuluk*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-39). हुवेम-ह्वेञ् (*huvema-hveñ*) (root हुवेम-ह्वेञ् (*huvema-hveñ*), in the sense of challenge and sound; perfect/optative-type first-person plural as printed): the accent *nighāta* arises because it follows a non-verb; उच्छंत्याम् (*ucchaṃtyām*) (root उच्छंत्याम् (*ucchaṃtyām*) in the sense of shining forth): the substitute for the present participle, तुदादिभ्यः शः (*tudādibhyaḥ śaḥ*) gives the *śa* suffix, and the feminine उगितश्च (*ugitaśca*) (Pā. Sū. 4-1-6) gives the suffix printed as "ಜೀಪ್"; locative singular. वह्निः (*vahniḥ*) (root *vah*, in the sense of carrying): the suffix *ni* by the rule वहि श्रि (*vahi śri*) (Uṇ. Sū. 4-491 [printed "ಉ ಸೂ ೪-೪೯೧"]); its *nit* quality gives the first-syllable-acute accent.

*[The window ends within the grammar page; it continues on the next page.]*

### Printed p. 17

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 1.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 17. The window begins within the Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā (grammar page) of Ṛk 1, continued from p. 16.*

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (continued; grammar page, noted briefly)

संतौ (*saṃtau*) (root अस (*asa*), "to be", in the sense of existing): the present-participle substitute for the tense suffix, *luk* for the connecting suffix, and by श्नसोरल्लोपः (*śnasorallopaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-4-111) the elision of the root's *a*; the suffix has the first-syllable-acute accent. After the dual ending the *num* augment (because the suffix has the mark *uk*), then anusvāra and assimilation to the following class consonant.

आर्यः (*āryaḥ*) (root *ṛ*, in the sense of going): by धातु आर्य (*dhātu ārya*) (Pā. Sū. 3-103 [as printed]) the suffix *yat* in the sense of master; with it as occasion the root takes *guṇa*, followed by *r*.

सुदास्तराय (*sudāstarāya*): शोभनं ददातीति (*śobhanaṃ dadātīti*) ("one who gives well"), hence सुदाः. (*sudāḥ.*); the root डुदाण् (*ḍudāṇ*) in the sense of giving, suffix *asun*; the accent of the base before a *kṛt* ending. Then the suffix तरप् (*tarap*) in the sense of excess; being marked *pit* it is unaccented; the *s* becomes *ru*, visarga and *sa*-change; dative singular. ‖ 1 ‖

#### Ṛk 2

*[As supplied, the label line reads: संहापादि; (*saṃhāpādi;*) ("saṃhitā-pāṭha", garbled); the Saṃhitā was a picture in the supplied text and was read from it by zoom. A page footer artefact "[2] [ Vol. 14" appears twice at the end of p. 17 in the supplied text.]*

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अ॒स्मे ऊ॒ षु वृ॑षणा मादयेथा॒मुत्प॒णीर्ह॑तमू॒र्म्या मदं॑ता ।
> श्रु॒तं मे॒ अच्छो॑क्तिभिर्मती॒नामेष्टा॑ नरा॒ निचे॑तारा च॒ कर्णैः॑ ॥ २ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अस्मे इति । ऊं इति । सु । वृषणा । मादयेथां । उत् । पणीन् । हतं । ऊर्म्या । मदंता ।
> श्रुतं । मे । अच्छोक्तिभिः । मतीनां । एष्टा । नरा । निचेतारा । च । कर्णैः ॥ २ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे वृषणा कामानां वर्षकावश्विन् युवामस्मे उ अस्मास्वेव सु माद-
> येथां । सुष्टु तृप्यतं । अस्मभ्यं तर्पयतमभिमतं । तथा पणीन्वणिजो लुब्ध-
> कानयष्ट्वानुदुन्यूल्य हतं । नाशयतं । कीदृशौ युवां । ऊर्म्या रात्रिना-
> मैतत् । रात्रौ मदंता माद्यंत् । यद्वा । ऊर्मिरिति सोमनाम । ऊर्मि-
> र्यस्ते पवित्र आ । ऋ. ९-६४-११ । इत्यादिषु तथा प्रयोगात् । अस्सदीयेन

*Gist:* O showering Aśvins, rejoice fully among us; destroy the niggardly traders; *ūrmyā* means night (or Soma); leaders, hear with your ears my praises made with pure words.

### Printed p. 18

*Running head: [18 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. Ā. 24 Sū. 184] (the print has "ಆ." for "ಅ." as supplied).*

(The Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya of Ṛk 2 continues on this page.)

> सोमेन मदंतौ । तादृशौ युवां मे ममाच्छोक्तिभिराभिमुख्यकरैर्निर्मलवेद-
> वाक्यै रचिता मतीनां ॥ कर्मणि षष्ठी ॥ मननसाधनाः स्तुतीः कर्णैराकर्णनसाधनैः
> श्रोत्रैः श्रुतं । शृणुतं । हे नरा नेतारौ युवां खल्वेष्टान्वेष्यारावस्मत्सुतीनां
> तथा निचेतारा लब्धानां तासां संचयकर्तारौ । निरित्येष समित्येतस्य स्थाने ।
> यस्मादेवंविधौ तस्माच्छृणुतमिति ॥

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

ऊर्मा (*ūrmā*) — in the time of night (or in the soma offered by us). मदंता (*madaṃtā*) — those who obtain satisfaction. वृषणा (*vṛṣaṇā*) — O Aśvin deities, showerers of desires. अस्मे उ (*asme u*) — among us alone (or for our satisfaction alone). सु मादयेथां (*su mādayethāṃ*) — you also obtain complete satisfaction. तथा (*tathā*) — likewise. पणीन् (*paṇīn*) — miserly ones who do not perform sacrifice. उत् हतं (*ut hataṃ*) — uproot and destroy. नरा (*narā*) — O Aśvin deities, leaders. एष्टा (*eṣṭā*) — (those who look out for our praises). निचेतारा च (*nicetārā ca*) — you who gather the obtained praises. अच्चोक्तिभिः (*accoktibhiḥ*) — (in order to obtain your grace), made of Vedic words. मे (*me*) — my (composed by me). मतीनां (*matīnāṃ*) — meditative praises. कर्णॆः (*karṇèḥ*) — with your ears. श्रुतं (*śrutaṃ*) — listen.

*[The printed forms ऊर्मा (*ūrmā*), अच्चोक्तिभिः (*accoktibhiḥ*) and कर्णॆः (*karṇèḥ*) differ in spelling from the Saṃhitā and Pada; kept as supplied. The label of the following section reads, as supplied, भाषार्थ]* (*bhāṣārtha]**); standard label inserted.]*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Aśvin deities, who obtain satisfaction in the soma juices offered by us and are showerers of desires: in order to make us satisfied, you also obtain complete satisfaction. Likewise uproot and destroy the misers who do not perform sacrifice. O Aśvin deities, leaders, you who look out for our praises and gather the praises received: for obtaining your grace, listen with love to my praise-words, made of Vedic sentences and meditative.

**Printed English translation**

Showerers (of benefits), rejoicing in (our) libations, make us happy and destroy the niggard (withholder) of offerings ; hear, leaders, with your ears, my praises addressed to you with pure words, for you are seekers (after praise), you are retentive (of laudation).

*[The label of the next section reads, as supplied, विशेषयगळु]* (*viśeṣayagaḷu]**); standard label inserted.]*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

ऊर्म्या—रात्रिनामैतत् । यद्वा । ऊर्मिरिति सोमनाम । (*ūrmyā—rātrināmaitat | yadvā | ūrmiriti somanāma |*) — "this is a name of night; or *ūrmi* is a name of Soma." The Nirukta authors have read this word among the names of night (Ni 2-18). Therefore here too the sense "night" may be taken; this is one alternative. If this sense is given, how is the word *ūrmi* formed? Explaining this, Sāyaṇa elsewhere, where this word is used, shows the example and explains thus:

### Printed p. 19

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 1.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 19.*

अस्मा उषास अतिरंत याममिंद्राय नक्तमूर्म्याः सुवाचः । (*asmā uṣāsa atiraṃta yāmamiṃdrāya naktamūrmyāḥ suvācaḥ |*)
अस्मा आपो मातरः सप्त तस्थुर्नृभ्यस्तराय सिंधवः सुपाराः ॥ (*asmā āpo mātaraḥ sapta tasthurnṛbhyastarāya siṃdhavaḥ supārāḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-96-1)

In explaining the meaning of the word *ūrmi* in this ṛk he says: रात्रिनामैतत् । ऋ गतिप्रापणयोः। (*rātrināmaitat | ṛ gatiprāpaṇayoḥ|*) “ अर्तॆरू च ” (*“ artèrū ca ”*) (Uṇ. Sū. 4-48 [printed "ಉ ಸೂ."]) इति ‘ मि ’ प्रत्ययः सर्वैरभिगंतव्यः । (*iti ‘ mi ’ pratyayaḥ sarvairabhigaṃtavyaḥ |*) रात्रौ हि सर्वॆ (*rātrau hi sarvè*) स्वनिवासं गच्छंति । स्वनिलयप्राप्तिहेतुभूता रात्रयः । (*svanivāsaṃ gacchaṃti | svanilayaprāptihetubhūtā rātrayaḥ |*): "the word is formed from the root *ṛ*, which means going and reaching. In the night all creatures go and gather in their own dwellings; since it brings everything to its own places, night has the famous name *ūrmi*." For this word in the present ṛk Sāyaṇa states the second alternative: यद्वा ऊर्मिरिति सोमनाम--अथवा (*yadvā ūrmiriti somanāma--athavā*): *ūrmi* may mean Soma. To support this explanation, citing

तम्म तम्म निवासक्कॆ होगि सेरिकॊळ्ळुत्तवॆ. स्वस्थानगळिगॆ ऎल्लवन्नू सेरिसुवुदरिंद रात्रिगॆ (*tamma tamma nivāsakkè hogi serikòḷḷuttavè. svasthānagaḷigè èllavannū serisuvudariṃda rātrigè*)
ऊर्मियॆंब हॆसरु प्रसिद्धवागिदॆ ऎंदु विवरिसिद्दारॆ ई पदक्कॆ प्रकृतवाद ऋक्किनल्लि सायणरु, (*ūrmiyèṃba hèsaru prasiddhavāgidè èṃdu vivarisiddārè ī padakkè prakṛtavāda ṛkkinalli sāyaṇaru,*)

he has shown that since *ūrmi* there means Soma, the same sense may arise in the present ṛk too. Here another point must be noticed. *Ūrmi* cannot mean Soma; *ūrmi* means a wave of water. Since in the cited ṛk Soma is addressed, the sense "Soma juice in the form of waves" is understood. The Nirukta authors have used this word in this same sense, that is, "water-wave",

मा नः समस्य दूढ्यं ः नि परिद्वेषसो अंहतिः । ऊर्मिर्न नावमा वधीत् ॥ (*mā naḥ samasya dūḍhyaṃ ḥ ni paridveṣaso aṃhatiḥ | ūrmirna nāvamā vadhīt ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-75-9)

and, citing this ṛk, have said ऊर्मिरूर्णोतेः (*ūrmirūrṇoteḥ*) (Ni. 5-23) that the word is formed from the root ऊर्ण् (*ūrṇ*), which gives the sense "to cover, conceal, envelop". It means a wave, or a mass of water; the word is also used in the sense "collection or mass of water".

धन्वन्नोऽतः कृणुते गातुमूर्मिं शुक्रैरूर्मिभिरभि नक्षति क्षां । (*dhanvanno 'taḥ kṛṇute gātumūrmiṃ śukrairūrmibhirabhi nakṣati kṣāṃ |*)
विश्वा सनानि जठरेषु धत्तेऽंतर्ननासु चरति प्रसूषु ॥ (*viśvā sanāni jaṭhareṣu dhatte 'ṃtarnanāsu carati prasūṣu ‖*)

((म. सं. (*(ma. saṃ.*) 1-95-10 [as supplied])

*[The window ends here; the Special Topics continue on the next page.]*

### Printed p. 20

*Running head: [20 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 184]. The window begins inside the Special Topics of Ṛk 2, after the verse that ended p. 19.*

In ऎंबल्लि (*èṃballi*) ऊर्मिं कृणुते, (*ūrmiṃ kṛṇute,*), ऊर्मिभिः अभिनक्षति (*ūrmibhiḥ abhinakṣati*) it says. Here, the fire makes the mass of water in the atmosphere flow in the form of a stream; with the clear wave-like masses of water he makes the earth be soaked with water, it says. In the present ṛk, either "night" or "Soma juice in the form of waves" is the sense.

पणीन् (*paṇīn*) — for the word *paṇi* Sāyaṇa has explained "merchants who hesitate to spend" (पणि शब्दक्कॆ (*paṇi śabdakkè*)) (merchants, vaiśyas who shrink from spending); thereafter he has cited the opinion of Yāska, पणिर्वणिग्भवतीति यास्कः (*paṇirvaṇigbhavatīti yāskaḥ*). The Nirukta authors, in explaining the sense of this word, cite

इंद्रो, विश्वान्नेकनाटा अहर्दृश उत क्रत्वा पणेरभि । (*iṃdro, viśvānnekanāṭā ahardṛśa uta kratvā paṇerabhi |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-66-10 [printed with the abbreviation "ಪು ಸಂ"])

and have said that the word पणि (*paṇi*) there means वणिजः (*vaṇijaḥ*), "those who carry on trade" (Ni. 6-26). And पणिः पणनात् । वणिक् पण्यं नेनेक्ति (*paṇiḥ paṇanāt | vaṇik paṇyaṃ nenekti*): because he carries on trade, the vaiśya is called *paṇi*; and that trader always keeps his goods clean so that they may be fit for trade (Ni. 2-17). Besides, in some other places Sāyaṇa has explained this word as पणयॆ इति लुब्बका अबुध्यमाना यागादीनकुर्वाणा अदानशीला (*paṇayè iti lubbakā abudhyamānā yāgādīnakurvāṇā adānaśīlā*) अस्मच्छत्रवः (*asmacchatravaḥ*): "greedy ones, who do not perform sacrifices and the like, who are of ungiving nature, our enemies", that is, misers, non-performers of rites such as sacrifice, enemies without charity. In the present ṛk he has taken it as लुब्बकानयष्टन् (*lubbakānayaṣṭan*). (The matter of the asura group called *paṇis* will be told later.)

For the word अस्मे उ-ऎंब (*asme u-èṃba*) he has given the sense अस्मास्वेव (*asmāsveva*). The Nirukta authors have said that although the word अस्मे (*asme*) is of one form, it is used in all case-meanings (Ni. 6-7 [the print has "ಸಿ." for "ನಿ."]). As in the case of this word, it was told earlier that the word अस्माकं (*asmākaṃ*) also is used in all the cases. Durgācārya, commenting here (Ni. 6-7), says: अस्मे इत्येतत्पदं सर्व विभक्त्यंतं । तस्मादनेकार्थं । एकमेव हैतच्छब्दरूपं सप्तस्वपि विभक्त्यर्थेषु वर्तते । प्रकरणादिवशात् तस्य नियमो भवति । तद्यथा प्रथमायास्तावत् अस्मे ते बंधुः । वयमित्यर्थः. (*asme ityetatpadaṃ sarva vibhaktyaṃtaṃ | tasmādanekārthaṃ | ekameva haitacchabdarūpaṃ saptasvapi vibhaktyartheṣu vartate | prakaraṇādivaśāt tasya niyamo bhavati | tadyathā prathamāyāstāvat asme te baṃdhuḥ | vayamityarthaḥ.*). That is: the word used in the seven cases must be interpreted according to each context. Of these, अस्मे ते बंधुः । (*asme te baṃdhuḥ |*) is an example of the nominative case. And in the same way,

### Printed p. 21

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 1] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 21.*

आ श्येनस्य जवसा नूतनेनास्मे यातं नासत्या सजोषाः । (*ā śyenasya javasā nūtanenāsme yātaṃ nāsatyā sajoṣāḥ |*)
हवे हि वामश्विना रातहव्या शश्वत्तमाया उषसो व्युष्टौ ॥ (*have hi vāmaśvinā rātahavyā śaśvattamāyā uṣaso vyuṣṭau ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-118-11)

The Nirukta authors, citing this ṛk, have said that the word अस्मे (*asme*) means अस्मान् इत्यर्थः (*asmān ityarthaḥ*), and that this word is used here in the sense of the accusative case;

भूरि चकर्थ युज्येभिरस्मे समानेभिर्वृषभ पौंस्येभिः । (*bhūri cakartha yujyebhirasme samānebhirvṛṣabha pauṃsyebhiḥ |*)
भूरीणि हि कृणवामा शविष्ठेंद्र क्रत्वा मरुतो यद्वशाम ॥ (*bhūrīṇi hi kṛṇavāmā śaviṣṭheṃdra kratvā maruto yadvaśāma ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-165-7)

citing this ṛk (the print has "ಮಕ್ಕನ್ನು" for "ಋಕ್ಕನ್ನು", as supplied) they have said अस्माभिः (*asmābhiḥ*) is the sense of the instrumental case;

अस्मे प्रयंधि मघवन्नृजीषिन्निंद्र रायो विश्ववारस्य भूरेः । (*asme prayaṃdhi maghavannṛjīṣinniṃdra rāyo viśvavārasya bhūreḥ |*)
अस्मे शतं शरदो जीवसे धा अस्मे वीराञ्छश्वत इंद्र शिप्रिन् ॥ (*asme śataṃ śarado jīvase dhā asme vīrāñchaśvata iṃdra śiprin ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-36-10)

here अस्मभ्यं (*asmabhyaṃ*) means "for us", the sense of the dative case; and

तस्य वयं सुमता यज्ञियस्मापि भद्रे सौमनसे स्याम । (*tasya vayaṃ sumatā yajñiyasmāpi bhadre saumanase syāma |*)
स सुत्रामा स्वना इंद्रो अस्मे आराच्चिद्द्वेषः सनुतर्युयोतु ॥ (*sa sutrāmā svanā iṃdro asme ārācciddveṣaḥ sanutaryuyotu ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-47-13)

here अस्मत् (*asmat*) means "from us", the sense of the ablative case;

आ नो भर भगमिंद्र द्युमंतं नि ते देष्णस्य धीमहि प्ररेके । (*ā no bhara bhagamiṃdra dyumaṃtaṃ ni te deṣṇasya dhīmahi prareke |*)
ऊर्व इव पप्रथे कामो अस्मे तमा पृण वसुपते वसूनां ॥ (*ūrva iva paprathe kāmo asme tamā pṛṇa vasupate vasūnāṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-30-19)

in this ṛk अस्माकं (*asmākaṃ*) means "our", the sense of the genitive case; and likewise,

अग्ने वाजस्य गोमत ईशानः सहसो यहो । (*agne vājasya gomata īśānaḥ sahaso yaho |*)
अस्मे धेहि जातवेदो महि श्रवः ॥ (*asme dhehi jātavedo mahi śravaḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-79-4)

here it is also in the sense of the locative case; thus they have stated that the word has the same form in all the cases (Ni. 6-7).

मतीनां (*matīnāṃ*) — मननसाधनाः स्तुतीः (*mananasādhanāḥ stutīḥ*): "meditative praise" is the sense. In the sense of the accusative, the use of the genitive is found. The meaning is: "listen to our meditative praises".

### Printed p. 22

*Running head: [22 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 184].*

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page, noted briefly)

अस्मे (*asme*) (from अस्मद् (*asmad*)): by सुपां सुलुक् (*supāṃ suluk*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-39) the locative plural takes the substitute *śe*. मादयेथाम् (*mādayethām*) (root *mad*, in the sense of satisfaction): imperative, second person dual; because it is a finite verb the *nighāta* accent arises. हतम् (*hatam*) (root *han*, in the sense of killing and going): the substitute *tam* for the second person dual; *luk* for the connecting suffix; by अनुदात्तोपदेशवनतितनो (*anudāttopadeśavanatitano*) (Pā. Sū. 6-4-37) the elision of the root's nasal; the *nighāta* accent arises. ऊर्म्या (*ūrmyā*): by सुपां सुलुक् (*supāṃ suluk*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-39) the substitute *ā* for the locative singular; the *yaṇ* substitute; by single substitution the case ending takes the acute. मदंता (*madaṃtā*) (root *mad*): the present-participle substitute for the tense suffix; for the dual *sulug* of the case endings by सुपां सुलुक् (*supāṃ suluk*) and the substitute *ā*; by तास्यनुदात्तेत् (*tāsyanudāttet*) the present-tense personal suffix is unaccented, so the accent of the root remains. श्रुतम् (*śrutam*) (root *śru*, in the sense of hearing): imperative, second dual, substitute *tam*; by बहुलं (*bahulaṃ*) छंदसि (*chaṃdasi*) *luk* for the connecting suffix; because the suffix has the mark *ñ* there is no *guṇa*; because the word stands at the beginning of the pāda there is no *nighāta*; by the suffix accent it has the last-syllable acute. मतीनाम् (*matīnām*): by नामन्यतरस्याम् (*nāmanyatarasyām*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-177) the case ending takes the acute; the genitive in the sense of the object. एष्टा (*eṣṭā*) (root *iṣ*, in the sense of going): the suffix *tṛn* in the agent sense; *guṇa* of the root with the short penultimate vowel; *ṣṭutva* of the suffix; because of the mark *n* it has the first-syllable acute. निचेतारा (*nicetārā*) (root *ci*, in the sense of collecting): the suffix *tṛn*, *guṇa* of the root's *i* by the rule "sārvadhātukārdhadhātukayoḥ" (no number printed); the preposition *ni* gives the sense of the preposition *sam* by interchange; the substitute *ā* of the dual by the rule of the case endings. ‖ 2 ‖

*[A stray line "-৪০৪-" (page number in other numerals) follows in the supplied text; kept as an italic note in the Kannada file.]*
