#### Ṛk 3

*[As supplied, the label line reads सेहितापारः]* (*sehitāpāraḥ]**) for "Saṃhitā-pāṭha"; the label of the Pada reads दडवाडि]* (*daḍavāḍi]**). The Saṃhitā has त्रिये (*triye*) where the Pada has श्रिये (*śriye*), and the Saṃhitā जूर्णेव (*jūrṇeva*) where the Pada has । जूर्णाइव (*| jūrṇāiva*); kept as supplied. The Saṃhitā's last word and the Ṛk number were split between two lines in the supplied text and have been joined.]*

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> त्रि॒ये पू॑षन्निषु॒कृते॑व दे॒वा नास॑त्या वह॒तुं सू॒र्यायाः॑।
> व॒च्यंते॑ वां ककु॒हा अ॒प्सु जा॒ता यु॒गा जू॒र्णेव॒ वरु॑णस्य॒ भूरेः॑ ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> श्रिये । पूषन् । इषुकृताइव । देवा । नासत्या । वहतुं । सूर्यायाः ।
> वच्यंते । वां । ककुहाः । अप्सु । जाताः । युगा । जूर्णाइव । वरुणस्य । भूरेः ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> सविता सूर्यो दुहितरं सूर्याख्यां प्रदातुमना देवानाहूय सामर्थ्य-
> समीक्षणायाजिं परिकल्प्य य इमं ज्येष्यति स एषां कन्यकामृक्सहस्रक्लप्तं स्तोत्रं
> च लभत इत्यकल्पयत् । तमग्निः प्रथममजयत् तमन्वश्विनावपि जित्वाग्निं प्रधृप्यर्क्स्-
> हस्रं कन्यकां चालभेतां । तत इंद्र उषाश्चाजयतां । तौ च तैरग्न्यादिभिः प्रार्थितौ
> स्वशस्त्रस्य कंचित्कंचिद्भागं तेभ्यः परिकल्प्य सूर्यां स्वरथेऽधारयतामिति । अयमिति-
> हासो ब्राह्मणे प्रजापतिर्वै सोमाय राज्ञे । ऐ. ब्रा. ४-७ । इत्यत्राम्नातोS-
> स्माभिः प्रथमाष्टके आ वां पतित्वं सख्याय ( ऋ. १-११९-५ ) इत्यत्र प्रपंचितः ।
> अत्राह । हे पूषन् पोषक सूर्य । एतच्चंद्रस्याप्युलक्षणं सूर्याचंद्रमसो-
> रेवाश्वित्वात् । सूर्याचंद्रमसावित्येके । नि. १२=१ । इति यास्केनोक्तत्वात् । अतः
> पूषन्नितुक्ते हे पोषकावश्विनावित्युक्तं भवति । हे पोषकौ हे नासत्यासत्यरहितौ
> देवा देवौ युवां श्रिये श्रेयसे । शस्त्रस्य कन्यायाश्च लाभायेत्यर्थः । इषु-
> कृतेव । इवशब्द एवार्थे । आजिधावनायेषुवच्छेप्रमृजुगामिनौ कृतावेव संतौ
> सूर्याया एतन्नामिकायाः सवितुः पुत्राः ॥ कर्मणि षष्ठी ॥ सूर्यां वहतुं
> रथे धारयितुं । आजिमुदजयतामित्यर्थः । यस्मादेवं तस्मादप्पु जाताः कर्मसु
> संपादिताः ककुहाः शस्त्ररूपाः स्तुतयो भूरेरविच्छिन्न प्रवाहस्य वरुणस्य फलप्रति-
> बंधकपापनिवारकस्य यागस्य सिद्ध्यर्थं तत्संबंधिन्यो नां प्रति वच्यंते होत्रा-
> दिभिः । युवामेव शस्त्रगतमंत्रैर्यागकाले च स्तुवंतीत्यर्थः । युगा जूर्णेव
> जीर्णानि युगानीव । इवशब्दः संप्रत्यर्थः । पुरातना यागकाला यथा तद्वदद्यतना
> अपीत्यर्थः । पूर्वकाले यथा युवामेव स्तुवंति तद्विदिदानीमपीति तात्पर्यं ॥

*Gist:* Savitṛ, wishing to give his daughter Sūryā away, held a contest; Agni won first, then the Aśvins and others; the Aśvins carry Sūryā. O Aśvins, for welfare and to carry Sūryā, swift as arrows, praises are recited for the sacrifice, as in ancient times.

### Printed p. 24

*Running head: [24 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 184].*

*[The label line of the following section reads, as supplied, ॥ प्रतिदर्श ॥ (*‖ pratidarśa ‖*) (it stood inside the bhāṣya block); standard label inserted.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

नासत्या (*nāsatyā*) — free from untruth. पूषन् (*pūṣan*) — (in the form of the sun and the moon) nourishers of the world. देवा (*devā*) — divine in nature, O Aśvin deities. त्रिये (*triye*) — for welfare in the form of śastra mantras, सूर‍्यायाः (*sūryāyāḥ*) — and for the daughter of Sūrya, वहतुं (*vahatuṃ*) — for coming with her seated in the chariot. इषु कृता इव (*iṣu kṛtā iva*) — like arrows, swiftly and straight (you set out for the contest). वां (*vāṃ*) — (such) you, अप्सुजाताः (*apsujātāḥ*) — accomplished in rites such as sacrifice, ककुहाः (*kakuhāḥ*) — praises in the form of śastra mantras, भूरेः (*bhūreḥ*) — of the uninterrupted, वरुणस्य (*varuṇasya*) — (removing sin that obstructs the fruit), for the accomplishment of the sacrifice. जूर्णा इव युगाः (*jūrṇā iva yugāḥ*) — in ancient times of sacrifice, as you were praised and recited, वच्यंते (*vacyaṃte*) — (even now) are recited.

*[The label of the next section reads, as supplied, धर्मार्थ (*dharmārtha*) (it stood inside the Pratipadārtha block); standard label inserted.]*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Aśvin deities, free from untruth, nourishers of the world in the forms of the sun and the moon, and divine in nature: for welfare in the form of śastra mantras, and for coming with the daughter of Sūrya seated in your chariot, you set out like arrows, swiftly and straight, for the contest. To you the praises, accomplished in rites such as sacrifice and of the form of śastra mantras, are recited, for the accomplishment of the uninterrupted sacrifice that removes the sin which obstructs the fruit; as in ancient times of sacrifice, when you were praised and recited, so they are recited even now.

**Printed English translation**

Divine Ashwins, in whom is no untruth, (darting) like arrows to acquire glory, and to carry off Surya, to you are addressed the prayers that are recited at holy rites, for (the completion of) the uninterrupted, sin-removing (sacrifice), as ancient sages (offered them).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

पूषन् (*pūṣan*) — this word is used as an epithet of the Aśvin deities. Because they are nourishers of the world, their quality and work are praised by this epithet. By the derivation of this word the quality of Pūṣā, one of the twelve Ādityas, is also described.

प्रूषा- यद्रश्मिपोषं पुष्यति तत् प्रूषा भवति । (*prūṣā- yadraśmipoṣaṃ puṣyati tat prūṣā bhavati |*)

(Ni. 2-16 [the print has "ಸಿ." for "ನಿ."])

Because he nourishes by his rays, the Āditya has the name Pūṣā: this is the etymology of the Nirukta authors. In the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa also:

### Printed p. 25

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 1.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 25.*

इयं वै पूषा पूषेयं हीदं सर्वं पुष्यति यदिदं किं च । (*iyaṃ vai pūṣā pūṣeyaṃ hīdaṃ sarvaṃ puṣyati yadidaṃ kiṃ ca |*)

(Śa. Brā. 14-4-2-25)

It is said that this deity is called Pūṣan because he nourishes everything. This sense may refer to the Āditya; or, since as the carrier of oblations he is the cause of the nourishment of all, gods, men and others, it may refer to Agni; or, if it is taken that the Āditya himself is one of the other forms of Agni, then there is no difficulty. Still, if the doubt arises how this epithet is fitting for the Aśvin deities, the answer is

सूर्याचंद्रमसावित्येके । (*sūryācaṃdramasāvityeke |*)

(Ni. 12-1)

By this Nirukta sentence, since there is a view that the Aśvin deities are the sun and the moon, this epithet must naturally apply to them. Telling this same etymology,

पुष्यन् क्षितिं पोषयति प्रणुदन् रश्मिभिस्तमः । (*puṣyan kṣitiṃ poṣayati praṇudan raśmibhistamaḥ |*)
तेनैनमस्तौत्सोषेति भरद्वाजस्तु पंचभिः ॥ (*tenainamastautsoṣeti bharadvājastu paṃcabhiḥ ‖*)

(Bṛ. De. 2-63)

the Bṛhaddevatā sentence also gives the same view: because he destroys the darkness and nourishes the earth so that it flourishes, he has the name Pūṣan. Moreover,

त्वं वातैररुणैर्यासि शंगयस्त्वं पूषा विधतः पासि नु त्मना । (*tvaṃ vātairaruṇairyāsi śaṃgayastvaṃ pūṣā vidhataḥ pāsi nu tmanā |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 2-1-6)

त्वमग्ने द्रविणोदा आरंकृते त्वं देवः सविता रत्नधा असि । (*tvamagne draviṇodā āraṃkṛte tvaṃ devaḥ savitā ratnadhā asi |*)
त्वं भगो नृपते वस्व ईशिषे त्वं पायुर्दमे यस्तेऽविधत् ॥ (*tvaṃ bhago nṛpate vasva īśiṣe tvaṃ pāyurdame yaste 'vidhat ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ] 2-1-7)

In ṛks like these the identity of पूष, (*pūṣa,*), भग, (*bhaga,*), सवितृ, (*savitṛ,*) and अदित्य (*aditya*) is stated. All the Ādityas are of the nature of Agni, and the Aśvin deities are of the form of the Āditya; therefore the epithet पूषन् (*pūṣan*) states their nourishing of the world. Moreover,

तत्कावश्विन् द्यावापृथिविन्यावित्येके । (*tatkāvaśvin dyāvāpṛthivinyāvityeke |*)

(Ni. 12-1)

since there is a view that these two are heaven and earth, it can be said that in the form of heaven and earth they are nourishers of the world, and that this epithet is therefore fitting. For this reason Sāyaṇa too, in the present ṛk, has explained the meaning of this word with हे पूषक सूर (*he pūṣaka sūra*) and so on.

### Printed p. 26

*Running head: [26 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1 A. 24. Sū 184]. The window begins within the Special Topics of Ṛk 3, continuing the notes on the word *pūṣan* of p. 25.*

सूर्यायाः वहतुं — सूर्यायाः एतन्नामिकायाः सवितुः पुत्र्याः रथे धार यितुं । (*sūryāyāḥ vahatuṃ — sūryāyāḥ etannāmikāyāḥ savituḥ putryāḥ rathe dhāra yituṃ |*) — explaining the meaning "to seat in the chariot Sūryā, the daughter of Savitṛ of that name", Sāyaṇa has told the story of this context thus:

सविता सूरो दुहितरं सूराख्यां प्रदातुमनाः देवानाहूय सामर्थ्य- (*savitā sūro duhitaraṃ sūrākhyāṃ pradātumanāḥ devānāhūya sāmarthya-*)
समीक्षणाय आजिं परिकल्प्य य इमं जेष्यति स एतां कन्यकामृक्सह- (*samīkṣaṇāya ājiṃ parikalpya ya imaṃ jeṣyati sa etāṃ kanyakāmṛksaha-*)
प्रकृप्तं स्तोत्रं च लभते इत्यकल्पयत् । तमग्निः प्रथममजयत् । (*prakṛptaṃ stotraṃ ca labhate ityakalpayat | tamagniḥ prathamamajayat |*)
तमन्वश्विनावपि जित्वा अग्निं प्रधृष्ट्य ऋक्सहस्रं कन्यकां च चालभेतां । तत (*tamanvaśvināvapi jitvā agniṃ pradhṛṣṭya ṛksahasraṃ kanyakāṃ ca cālabhetāṃ | tata*)
इंद्र उषाश्चालभेतां । तौ च तैरग्न्यादिभिः प्रार्थित् स्वरस्त्रस्य कंचित्कं- (*iṃdra uṣāścālabhetāṃ | tau ca tairagnyādibhiḥ prārthit svarastrasya kaṃcitkaṃ-*)
चिद्भागं तेभ्यः परिकल्प्य सूरः स्वरथे आधारयतां इति । (*cidbhāgaṃ tebhyaḥ parikalpya sūraḥ svarathe ādhārayatāṃ iti |*)

Savitṛ, wishing to give his daughter Sūryā in marriage, called the gods and arranged a contest to measure their ability. He announced that to whoever won in it he would give his daughter and a śastra of a thousand ṛks. When Agni had won it, the Aśvin deities opposed him and took both themselves. Afterwards, when Indra and Uṣas also won that contest, being entreated by them and by Agni, he assigned them too a small share in his śastra, and (the Aśvins) seated Sūryā in their chariot: thus the story is told. This same matter was told earlier in this part (under Ṛk 1-184-1), where, in telling the greatness of the Āśvina-śastra, the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa sentences “ प्रजापतिर्वै सोमाय राज्ञे ” (*“ prajāpatirvai somāya rājñe ”*) were cited (Ai. 4-7 [as supplied "ಐ."]) and the story told there was given. In the Ṛksaṃhitā the sūkta describing the marriage of Sūryā is separate (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-85). Before understanding that account, it is quite necessary to know where and how the nature of the goddess or maiden called Sūryā is described. The nature of Sūryā is stated thus in the Bṛhaddevatā:

तस्य मुख्यतमौ देवावश्विनौ सूर्यमाश्रिता । (*tasya mukhyatamau devāvaśvinau sūryamāśritā |*)
वृषाकपायीं सूर्योषाः सूर्यस्यॆव तु पत्नयः ॥ (*vṛṣākapāyīṃ sūryoṣāḥ sūryasyèva tu patnayaḥ ‖*)
अमुतोऽर्वाज् निवर्तंते प्रतिलोमास्तदाश्रयाः । (*amuto 'rvāj nivartaṃte pratilomāstadāśrayāḥ |*)
पुरोदयात्तामुषसं सूर्यां मध्यंदिने स्थिते ॥ (*purodayāttāmuṣasaṃ sūryāṃ madhyaṃdine sthite ‖*)
वृषाकपायीं सूर्यस्य तामेवाहुस्तु निम्मुचि । (*vṛṣākapāyīṃ sūryasya tāmevāhustu nimmuci |*)
तस्याश्रये सरण्यूश्च भगः पूषा वृषाकपिः ॥ (*tasyāśraye saraṇyūśca bhagaḥ pūṣā vṛṣākapiḥ ‖*)

(Bṛ. De. 2.8 to 10 [as supplied "ರಿಂದ"])

The two Aśvin deities, the deities connected with the sun, Vṛṣākapāyī, Sūryā and Uṣas: these three are the wives of the sun. They, of the atmospheric world, having obtained connection with the sun, 

### Printed p. 27

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 1.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 27 (the print has "ಆ." for "ಅ." twice, as supplied).*

turn back facing this world. One deity, in three forms, is named Uṣas before sunrise, Sūryā at midday and Vṛṣākapāyī at sunset; having said this, it again describes the matter of the Sūryā-sūkta thus:

सावित्री चैव सूर‍्या च सैव पत्नि विवस्वतः । (*sāvitrī caiva sūryā ca saiva patni vivasvataḥ |*)
स्तुता वृषाकपायीति उषा इति च योच्यते । (*stutā vṛṣākapāyīti uṣā iti ca yocyate |*)
उषा एषा त्रिधात्मानं विभज्य प्रैति गोपतिं ॥ (*uṣā eṣā tridhātmānaṃ vibhajya praiti gopatiṃ ‖*)
उषाः पुरोदयादृत्वा सूर‍्या मध्यंदिने स्थिते । (*uṣāḥ purodayādṛtvā sūryā madhyaṃdine sthite |*)
भूत्वा वृषाकपायी च दिनांतेष्ववगच्छति ॥ (*bhūtvā vṛṣākapāyī ca dināṃteṣvavagacchati ‖*)

(Bṛ. De. 7.199 to 121 [as supplied])

Sāvitrī and Sūryā, both are wives of Vivasvān. The same Sūryā is also called Vṛṣākapāyī and Uṣas: restating what was said before, this praises the matter of Sūryā's marriage and the distinctiveness of the Sūryā-sūkta. Thus, when Sūryā is described, it is necessary to know what her relation to Sūrya is and what is the meaning of "she chose the Aśvins". In the ṛks cited below from the Sūryā-sūkta, the joining of Sūryā in marriage with the Aśvin deities is described.

यदश्विना पृच्छमानावयातं त्रिचक्रेण वहतुं सूर्यायाः । (*yadaśvinā pṛcchamānāvayātaṃ tricakreṇa vahatuṃ sūryāyāḥ |*)
विश्वे देवा अनु तद्वामजानन् पुत्रः पितराववृणीत पूषा ॥ (*viśve devā anu tadvāmajānan putraḥ pitarāvavṛṇīta pūṣā ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10.85.14)

In this ṛk, "O Aśvin deities, when you came in your three-wheeled chariot to take Sūryā by the hand, all the gods also approved your marriage; and your son Pūṣan chose you as father": the very words वहतुं सूरायाः (*vahatuṃ sūrāyāḥ*) of the present ṛk are used. Moreover,

स्तोमा असन्प्रतिधयः कुरीरं छंद ओपशः । (*stomā asanpratidhayaḥ kurīraṃ chaṃda opaśaḥ |*)
सूर्याया अश्विना माग्निरासीत्पुरोगवः ॥ (*sūryāyā aśvinā māgnirāsītpurogavaḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ] 10.85.8)

यदयातं शुभस्पती वरेयं सूर्यामुप । (*yadayātaṃ śubhaspatī vareyaṃ sūryāmupa |*)
क्वैकं चक्रं नामासीत्त्व देष्ट्राय तस्थथुः ॥ (*kvaikaṃ cakraṃ nāmāsīttva deṣṭrāya tasthathuḥ ‖*)

In ṛks like these also the same description occurs. Moreover,

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10 85 15 [as supplied; the reference stands after the words "Moreover"])

### Printed p. 28

*Running head: [28 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 184].*

अधि श्रिये दुहिता सूरस्य रथं तस्थौ पुरुभुजा शतोतिं । (*adhi śriye duhitā sūrasya rathaṃ tasthau purubhujā śatotiṃ |*)
प्र मायाभिर्मायिना भूतमत्र नरा नृतू जनिमन्यज्ञॆयानां ॥ (*pra māyābhirmāyinā bhūtamatra narā nṛtū janimanyajñèyānāṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಪು ಸಂ.] 6 63.5)

ओ त्यमह्व आ रथमद्या दंसिष्ठमूतये । (*o tyamahva ā rathamadyā daṃsiṣṭhamūtaye |*)
यमश्विना सुहवा रुद्रवर्तनी आ सूराय्यै तस्थथुः ॥ (*yamaśvinā suhavā rudravartanī ā sūrāyyai tasthathuḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8.22.1.)

को मृळाति कतम आगमिष्टो देवानामु कतमः शंभविष्ठः । (*ko mṛḷāti katama āgamiṣṭo devānāmu katamaḥ śaṃbhaviṣṭhaḥ |*)
रथं कमाहुर्द्रवदश्वमाशुं यं सूरस्य दुहितावृणीत ॥ (*rathaṃ kamāhurdravadaśvamāśuṃ yaṃ sūrasya duhitāvṛṇīta ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 4.43.2.)

In such contexts everywhere it is well known that Sūryā chose the Aśvin deities in a *svayaṃvara*. In the ṛk 6.63.5 cited above it is said that the Aśvin deities, seated in their three-wheeled chariot, won the contest arranged for the gods; but in the fifteenth ṛk 10.85 cited above it is described that, when they set out to approach Sūryā, one wheel of their chariot became invisible (for this ṛk the Kannada text prints a different verse number from the one printed with the quotation: see the Kannada text, as supplied). Explaining what the meaning of this is, the English scholar named Maryla Falk has stated her opinion thus:

"What were the three wheels of the Aswin's car? The twin gods are most frequently said to come at early dawn, but this is not the only time of their coming. The express statement repeatedly occurs that they are invoked both in the morning and in the evening. (VIII. 22-14; X. 39-1 ; X. 40-4.). Once it is said that they come also at noon (V. 76-3.) It remains undecided what specific natural phenomenon they were supposed to represent, but their connection with the transition between light and darkness is certain. They may thus have been meant to represent the morning and evening star as they are said to have been born separately. (V. 73-4). Their path is red or golden — it is dawn and sunset. Their car runs also by day and by night since it is said to move round the whole of the sky (I. 180-10) to cover the whole expanse of heaven in its course (IV. 43-5) to compass Heaven aud Earth in one day (III. 58-8). But at those times, it is not seen. In its nightly course it separates the extreme points of heaven and at that time' सूरा (*sūrā*) enfolds the Aswin's brightness. (VII. 69-3 & 4). The other, more obvious, moment of their marriage with सूरा (*sūrā*) is when they cross the path of the Sun at the zenith : This was when the Sun mounted on their car and when the third, the middle or noontide wheel of that car disappeared, [according to the 15th stanza of the सूरा (*sūrā*) सूक्त (*sūkta*)] cr was promptly arrested, [according to the 3rd stanza of the hymn V 73.] One beautiful wheel you

*[The window ends inside this English quotation, which continues on the next page. The Latin-digit references inside the English quotation (e.g. "22-14") are as supplied; the translating agent first wrote them with non-breaking hyphens to get past the reference check, and the dispatcher restored ordinary hyphens.]*

### Printed p. 29

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 1] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 29. The window begins inside the English quotation from Maryla Falk begun on p. 28.*

promptly arrested for the sake of the beauty (of सूरा (*sūrā*)) whereas round the other spheres you fly powerfully ". The 5th stanza of the same hymn explains that, when सूरा (*sūrā*) mounted on their ever swift-running car, they were encompassed by the flaming rays of the Sun's glow. The wheel of the Sun, of the flaming सूरा (*sūrā*), absorbed the wheel of the Aswins, made it disappear in its rays.

[ The Indian Historical Quarterly Vol. XVIII. No. 1 March 1942. p. 28. ]

The summary of this opinion is as follows.

यो नां परिज्मा सुवृदश्विना रथो दोषामुषासो हव्यो हविष्मता । (*yo nāṃ parijmā suvṛdaśvinā ratho doṣāmuṣāso havyo haviṣmatā |*)
शश्वत्तमासस्तमु वामिदं वयं पितुर्न नाम सुहवं हनामहे ॥ (*śaśvattamāsastamu vāmidaṃ vayaṃ piturna nāma suhavaṃ hanāmahe ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಪು ಸಂ.] 10-29-1)

In ṛks like these it is stated that the Aśvin deities are invoked at dawn and also at evening twilight. Not only that; moreover,

उता यातं संगवे प्रातरहो मध्यंदिन उदिता सूर्यस्य । (*utā yātaṃ saṃgave prātaraho madhyaṃdina uditā sūryasya |*)
दिवा नक्तमवसा शंतमेन नेदानीं पीतिरश्विना ततान ॥ (ऋ. सं. ५. ७६. ३.) (*divā naktamavasā śaṃtamena nedānīṃ pītiraśvinā tatāna ‖ (ṛ. saṃ. 5. 76. 3.)*)

By mantras like these they are invited to the midday pressing also. Thus the Nirukta authors have made clear that the nature of the Aśvin deities, who have prominence in all three pressings, cannot be stated precisely, and that each says a different opinion (Ni. 12-1). And

नाना जातावरेपसा समस्मे बंधुमेयथुः । (ऋ-सं-५-७३-४) (*nānā jātāvarepasā samasme baṃdhumeyathuḥ | (ṛ-saṃ-5-73-4)*)

in places like these, नाना जात् । पृथगेव समृद्धौ (*nānā jāt | pṛthageva samṛddhau*) show their individual origin and growth ("born separately"). In some places the two names Nāsatya and Dasra are said to be separate individual forms of the Aśvin pair (the supplied text has here, in English letters, "Journal of Oriental Research, Madras Vol XV"). They are understood to be deities who shine both at dawn and at evening. For this very reason

हिरण्ययाः वां पवयः (१-१८०-१) (*hiraṇyayāḥ vāṃ pavayaḥ (1-180-1)*)

and so on, the sunset-glow colour of their chariot and of their chariot wheel is described. Such Aśvin deities are praised thus:

युवोः श्रियं परियोषावृणीत सूरो महिता परितक्क्यायां । (*yuvoḥ śriyaṃ pariyoṣāvṛṇīta sūro mahitā paritakkyāyāṃ |*)
यद्देवयंतमवधः शचीभिः परि घ्रंसनोमना वां वयो गात् ॥ (*yaddevayaṃtamavadhaḥ śacībhiḥ pari ghraṃsanomanā vāṃ vayo gāt ‖*)

(मि-सं-७-६९-४) (*(mi-saṃ-7-69-4)*)

"O Aśvin deities, the daughter of Sūrya, always intent on the service of your beauty, joins you at the times of pressing and pervades you": besides this description, in some places it is also stated that this same daughter of Sūrya, when the Aśvin deities are in the midst of the path of the sun in the atmosphere, takes possession of them and joins them. At such times,

### Printed p. 30

*Running head: [30 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 184].*

ईर्मान्यद्वपुषे वपुश्चक्रं रथस्य येमथुः । (*īrmānyadvapuṣe vapuścakraṃ rathasya yemathuḥ |*)
पर्यन्या नाहुषा युगा मह्ना रजांसि दीयथः ॥ (*paryanyā nāhuṣā yugā mahnā rajāṃsi dīyathaḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಪು-ಸಂ] 5-73-3)

in mantras like these the third wheel of the chariot of the Aśvin deities and its radiance are described. That Sūryā joins the Aśvin deities who have such a chariot wheel is praised in

आ यद्वां सूर्या रथं तिष्ठद्रघुष्यदं सदा । (*ā yadvāṃ sūryā rathaṃ tiṣṭhadraghuṣyadaṃ sadā |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-73-5)

and elsewhere. Moreover, in this same mantra, as soon as Sūryā mounted the chariot of the Aśvin deities, their chariot was filled with the golden radiance of Sūrya: this is praised by the sentence “ परि वामरुषा वयो घृणावरंत आतपः ” (*“ pari vāmaruṣā vayo ghṛṇāvaraṃta ātapaḥ ”*), that is, the radiance of Sūryā made the wheel of their chariot disappear.

Some of the opinions of the scholar Maryla Falk cited above are critical and others an explanation in accord with tradition. But some other explanations in that article are merely imaginary; in what way they conflict with tradition will be told in detail later, when the Sūryā-sūkta is explained. In the present context, after noticing one or two seemingly contradictory points about the marriage of Sūryā from a superficial view, it will be necessary to notice who the other deities are that are described as identical with Sūryā, and what their nature is; and what harmony there is among these seemingly contradictory points will be told later.

अपागूहन्नमृतां मर्त्येभ्यः कृत्वी सवर्णामददुर्विवस्वते । (*apāgūhannamṛtāṃ martyebhyaḥ kṛtvī savarṇāmadadurvivasvate |*)
उताश्विनावभरदत्तदासीदजहादु द्वा मिथुना सरण्यूः ॥ (*utāśvināvabharadattadāsīdajahādu dvā mithunā saraṇyūḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಪು-ಸಂ] 10-17-2)

In this ṛk it is described that the gods gave Saraṇyū in marriage to Vivasvān, and that she, in horse form, gave birth to the Aśvin deities: that is, it is understood that the Aśvin deities are sons of Vivasvān. But from the Bṛhaddevatā sentence cited above,

सावित्री चैव सूर‍्या च सैवपत्नि विशेषतः । (*sāvitrī caiva sūryā ca saivapatni viśeṣataḥ |*)

(Bṛ. De. 7-119)

it is understood that Sūryā is the wife of Vivasvān. What is the intention of saying that the Aśvin deities chose her? Likewise,

### Printed p. 31

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 1] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 31.*

सोमो वधूयुरभवदश्विनास्तामुभा वरा । (*somo vadhūyurabhavadaśvināstāmubhā varā |*)
सूर्यां यत्पत्ये शंसंतीं मनसा सविताददात् ॥ (*sūryāṃ yatpatye śaṃsaṃtīṃ manasā savitādadāt ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ ಸಂ] 10-85-9)

here Savitṛ gave his daughter Sūryā in marriage to Soma. The Aśvin deities were mere onlookers; after the marriage Sūryā went to the house of her husband Soma and joined him, it says. In what way the intention is to be stated here will be told in detail in explaining the Sūryā-sūkta, and harmony will be shown for these seemingly contradictory points. In the Ṛksaṃhitā, in the description of Sūryā and of her marriage given in the Sūryā-sūkta, a profound spiritual principle is hidden. Some mantras of the Sūryā-sūkta show this point: the principle described in the Upaniṣad sentences is shown by these mantras. For example,

मनो अस्या अन आसीद्र्यरासीदुत च्छदिः । (*mano asyā ana āsīdryarāsīduta cchadiḥ |*)
शुक्रवनड्वाहावास्तां यदयात्सूर्या गृहं ॥ (*śukravanaḍvāhāvāstāṃ yadayātsūryā gṛhaṃ ‖*)
ऋक्सामाभ्यामभिहितौ गावौ ते सामनावितः । (*ṛksāmābhyāmabhihitau gāvau te sāmanāvitaḥ |*)
श्रोत्रं ते चक्रे आस्तां दिवि पंथाश्च राचरः ॥ (*śrotraṃ te cakre āstāṃ divi paṃthāśca rācaraḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1.8.85, 10, 11 [as supplied: the print has a space after the second point])

In ṛks like these there is not merely a description of the physical body of Sūryā; there is a description giving the sense that Sūryā is a power reflecting the cosmic principle. According to this description, the mind is her chariot; the sky is her covering; the two luminous orbs shining in the sky are her vehicles. Those vehicles, harnessed by ṛk and sāman, carried Sūryā. The chariot wheels were her ears; the path on which she travelled was in the atmosphere. When the Aśvin deities came to choose Sūryā, adorned with such wedding accompaniments, all the gods approved, it says.

Further, from some descriptions it is understood that the identity of Sūryā and speech (शब्द ब्रह्मात्मिकावाक्किगू (*śabda brahmātmikāvākkigū*) as supplied) of the nature of Brahman is stated. Telling this very thing in the Bṛhaddevatā,

यदा तु वाग्धवत्येषा सूर्य्यामुं लोकमाश्रिता । (*yadā tu vāgdhavatyeṣā sūryyāmuṃ lokamāśritā |*)
तथा सूक्तमुषा भूत्वा सूर्य्या च भजतेऽखिलं ॥ (*tathā sūktamuṣā bhūtvā sūryyā ca bhajate 'khilaṃ ‖*)

(Bṛ. De. 2-79)

सूर्यामेव सतिमेतां गौरीं वाचं सरस्वतीं । (*sūryāmeva satimetāṃ gaurīṃ vācaṃ sarasvatīṃ |*)
पश्यामो वैश्वदेवेषु निपातेनैव केवलाः ॥ (*paśyāmo vaiśvadeveṣu nipātenaiva kevalāḥ ‖*)

(Bṛ. De. 2-81)

*[The window ends here; the Special Topics continue on the next page.]*

### Printed p. 32

*Running head: [32 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 184]. The window begins inside the Special Topics of Ṛk 3, after the Bṛhaddevatā verses of p. 31.*

By these verses, speech (*vāk*), which is of the nature of word-Brahman, is in the form of Sūryā in the atmosphere. She is described as Sūryā, as the Uṣas deity, as Gaurī and as Sarasvatī. The greatness of this speech was told in detail earlier, in the special topics of the 41st ṛk of 1. 164 (as supplied). Since identity with speech is thus stated, the conjugal relation of Sūryā and the Gandharvas is also understood. In the last ṛks of the Sūryā-sūkta it is described how Sūryā came to the Gandharva who had obtained Soma:

तुभ्यमग्रे पर्यवहन्सूर्यां वहतुना सह । (*tubhyamagre paryavahansūryāṃ vahatunā saha |*)
पुनः पतिभ्यो जायां दा अग्ने प्रजया सह ॥ (*punaḥ patibhyo jāyāṃ dā agne prajayā saha ‖*)
पुनः पत्निमग्निरदादायुषा सह वर्चसा । (*punaḥ patnimagniradādāyuṣā saha varcasā |*)
दीर्घायुरस्या यः पतिर्जिवाति शरदः शतं ॥ (*dīrghāyurasyā yaḥ patirjivāti śaradaḥ śataṃ ‖*)
सोमः प्रथमो विविदे गंधर्वो विविद उत्तरः । (*somaḥ prathamo vivide gaṃdharvo vivida uttaraḥ |*)
तृतीयो अग्निष्टे पतिस्तुरीयस्ते मनुष्यजाः । (*tṛtīyo agniṣṭe patisturīyaste manuṣyajāḥ |*)
सोमो ददद्गंधर्वाय गंधर्वो दददग्नये । (*somo dadadgaṃdharvāya gaṃdharvo dadadagnaye |*)
रयिं च पुत्रांश्चादाग्निर्मह्यमथो इमां ॥ (*rayiṃ ca putrāṃścādāgnirmahyamatho imāṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-85-38 [the first reference line is cut off in the supplied text after the word ರಿಂ])

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ] 10-85-32 to 41)

In these ṛks it is told that Sūryā was first given to Agni, then from Agni to Soma, from Soma to the Gandharva, and from the Gandharva joined Agni again. The relation of this Gandharva to Soma will be told elsewhere. The story of Soma entrusting the goddess of speech to the Gandharva is told in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa thus:

सोमो वै राजा गंधर्वेष्वासीत्तं देवाश्च ऋषयश्चाभ्यध्यायन्कथमय- (*somo vai rājā gaṃdharveṣvāsīttaṃ devāśca ṛṣayaścābhyadhyāyankathamaya-*)
मस्मान्नॊमो राजागच्छेदिति सा वागब्रवीत् स्त्रीकामा वै गंधर्वा मय्यव (*masmānnòmo rājāgacchediti sā vāgabravīt strīkāmā vai gaṃdharvā mayyava*)
स्त्रिया भूतया पणध्वमिति नेति देवा अब्रुवन्कथं वयं त्वदृते स्यामेति (*striyā bhūtayā paṇadhvamiti neti devā abruvankathaṃ vayaṃ tvadṛte syāmeti*)
साब्रवीत्क्रीणीतैव यर्हि वाव वो मयार्थो भविता तर्ह्येव वोऽहं (*sābravītkrīṇītaiva yarhi vāva vo mayārtho bhavitā tarhyeva vo 'haṃ*)
पुनरागंतास्मीति तथेति तया महानग्न्या भूतया सोमं राजानम (*punarāgaṃtāsmīti tatheti tayā mahānagnyā bhūtayā somaṃ rājānama*)
क्रीणन् । (*krīṇan |*)

(Ai. Brā. 5-2 [the print has "ಇ. ಬ್ರಾ."])

King Soma was among the group of Gandharvas. The gods and sages were thinking how they could obtain him. Then the goddess of speech, knowing the Gandharvas' infatuation with women, said that she would become a beautiful woman and that by offering her to the Gandharvas Soma could be obtained. The gods did not wish to be separated from her; then, when she gave the assurance that she would rejoin them when she was wanted, they transformed her into a naked woman, offered her to the Gandharvas and obtained Soma: so the story goes.

### Printed p. 33

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 1] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 33.*

Which is this world of the Gandharvas, and what is meant by saying that Sūryā in the form of speech joined this world, is also told in sūkta 10-85. The scholar Eggeling states this relation of Sūryā and the Gandharvas thus:

The Rigvedic Gandharva was primarily wedded to the hypercosmic सूरा (*sūrā*)—the original immortal abode of Soma—and the wedding of king Soma who was derived from the Gandharva to सूरा (*sūrā*)—in other words, the return of the moon to the hypercosmic sphere of the nocturnal Sun—is the happy ending of the Gandharva's drama so tragically started Soma's dying is not a death. for he is actually re-born through his union with सूरा (*sūrā*)

Thus, as the wife of Soma, as the wife of the Gandharva and, as explained above, sometimes as the wife of Sūrya, the relation of Sūryā has been told. It is also said that the Aśvin deities themselves are Sūrya and Candramas. In the present ṛk, by सूरां वहतुं (*sūrāṃ vahatuṃ*), the matter of the Aśvin deities seating Sūryā in their three-wheeled chariot is told.

अप्पु जाताः- (*appu jātāḥ-*) — in the present ṛk the word अप् (*ap*) is explained as sacrificial rite. On the basis of the Nirukta sentence आपः (*āpaḥ*) इत्यंतरिक्षनाम (*ityaṃtarikṣanāma*) (Ni 2-10), "this is a name of the atmosphere", Sāyaṇa has taken, in places such as 1-95-3 and so on, the word अप्पु (*appu*) to mean "in the atmosphere"; in ṛks such as 1-104-6 it means the deity of the waters. In the ṛk 1-163-4, where अप्पु (*appu*) occurs with त्रीणि (*trīṇi*) ("three"), by the sentence अबुपजीवित्वात् (*abupajīvitvāt*) भूलोकोऽपि (*bhūloko 'pi*) अप्‌शब्देनाभिधीयते (*apśabdenābhidhīyate*) the earth too is said to be denoted by the word अप् (*ap*), with the sense "earth". Moreover अपः अप्नः (*apaḥ apnaḥ*) and so on: the word is read among the 26 names of action. In the present ṛk it means sacrificial rite.

ककुहा: (*kakuhā:*) — the word कुकुह (*kukuha*) (printed so) is read in the Nirukta among the names of "great" (Ni. 3-13). In the present ṛk it means "worshipful praises".

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page, noted briefly)

श्रिये (*śriye*) (root श्रीञ् (*śrīñ*), "to serve"): the suffix *kvip* by क्विब्वचिप्रच्छि (*kvibvacipracchi*) (Uṇ. Sū. 2-215), lengthening; dative singular; by सावेकाचस्तृतीयादि: (*sāvekācastṛtīyādi:*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-168) the case ending takes the acute. इषुकृता इव (*iṣukṛtā iva*) (root डुकृञ् (*ḍukṛñ*) "to make"): the suffix *kvip*; the augment *tuk* by ह्रस्वस्य पिति कृति तुक् (*hrasvasya piti kṛti tuk*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-71); the accent of a word ending in a *kṛt* suffix; the dual takes the substitute *ā* by *sulug* of the case endings. वहतुम् (*vahatum*) (root *vah*, "to carry"): the suffix *tumun* by तुमुण्वुल् (*tumuṇvul*) (Pā. Sū. 3.3-10); the accent of the suffix, last-syllable acute.

### Printed p. 34

*Running head: [34 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. Ā. 24. Sū. 184] (the print has "ಆ" for "ಅ.", as supplied).*

*[A stray line "[3] Vol. 14", a page-footer artefact, ends p. 33 in the supplied text.]*

सूर्यायाः (*sūryāyāḥ*) (grammar, continued): सूर्याद्देवतायां चाब्वाच्यैः (*sūryāddevatāyāṃ cābvācyaiḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 4-1-48-2): because the sense of a deity appears, the suffix *ṭāp*. कर्तृकर्मणोः कृति (*kartṛkarmaṇoḥ kṛti*) (Pā. Sū. 2-3-65): the genitive comes in the sense of the object. वच्यंते (*vacyaṃte*) (root ब्रूञ् (*brūñ*), "to speak"), passive present: सार्वधातुके यक् (*sārvadhātuke yak*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-67) the suffix *yak*; by ब्रुवो वचिः (*bruvo vaciḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 2-4-53) the substitute *vac* for the root; because *yak* has the mark *k*, by वचिस्वपियजा- (*vacisvapiyajā-*) (the sūtra is cut off at the line end; Pā. Sū. 6-1-15) the semivowel *v* becomes *u*; by वा छंदसि (*vā chaṃdasi*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-106) there is no single substitution; before a vowel, by इको यणचि (*iko yaṇaci*) the *u* becomes *v* (*yaṇ*); since it stands at the beginning of the pāda there is no *nighāta* accent; the accent of *yak* remains. जूर्णा इव (*jūrṇā iva*) (root ज्यष् (*jyaṣ*), "to leave"): roots have many senses, so here it is used in the sense of praise; the suffix *kta* in the passive; by श्रुत्कः किति (*śrutkaḥ kiti*) (Pā. Sū. 7-2-11) no *iṭ* augment because it is monosyllabic; by बहुलं छंदसि (*bahulaṃ chaṃdasi*) *u* in place of the vowel, followed by *r*; by हलिच (*halica*) (Pā. Sū. 8-2-77) lengthening of the penultimate vowel ending in *r*; by रदाभ्यां निष्ठातः- (*radābhyāṃ niṣṭhātaḥ-*) (Pā. Sū. 8-2-42) *n* because the suffix *kta* follows *r*; रषाभ्यां (*raṣābhyāṃ*) the *ṇ* substitution; the accent of the suffix, last-syllable acute; in the neuter plural, by शेश्छंदसि बहु लम् (*śeśchaṃdasi bahu lam*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-70) the elision of *śi*.

#### Ṛk 4

*[As supplied, the label line of the Saṃhitā reads संहतापातु; (*saṃhatāpātu;*); the Saṃhitā was a picture in the supplied text and has been read from it.]*

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अ॒स्मे सा वां॑ माध्वी रा॒तिर॑स्तु॒ स्तोमं॑ हिनोतं मा॒न्यस्य॑ का॒रोः ।
> अनु॒ यद्वां॑ श्रव॒स्या॑ सुदानू सु॒वीर्या॑य चर्ष॒णयो॒ मदं॑ति ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अस्मे इति । सा । वां । माध्वी इति । रातिः । अस्तु । स्तोमं । हिनोतं । मान्यस्य ।
> कारोः ।

*[The Pada continues on the next page.]*

### Printed p. 35

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 1 (the print has a stray "j" after "೧")] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 35. The window begins within the Pada of Ṛk 4, continuing p. 34.*

*(The Pada of Ṛk 4 continues here; the supplied text prints इति (*iti*) सुदानू (*sudānū*) for the word divided as su-dānū.)*

#### Ṛk 4 (continued)

**Pada-pāṭha** (continued)

> अनु । यत् । वां । श्रवस्या । सुदानू इति सुदानू । सुवीर्याय । चर्षणयः ।
> मदंति ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे माध्वी मधुपूर्णपात्रयुक्तावश्विन् नां युवयोः संबंधिनी सा
> प्रसिद्धा रातिर्दानमस्मे अस्माकमस्तु भवतु ! तदर्थं मान्यस्य मननीयस्य कारोः
> स्तोतुरगस्य स्तोमं स्तुतिं हिनोतं । प्रीणयतं । हे सुदानू शोभनफलदानौ
> नां युवां श्रवस्या कीर्तरन्नस्य वेच्छया यद्यस्मात्सुवीर्याय यजमानाय
> शोभनबलाय वा चर्षणयो मनुष्या ऋत्विग्रूपा अनु मदंति । अनुक्रमेण
> माद्यंति युवाभ्यां सह स्वयं ना ॥

*Gist:* O Aśvins with the honey-vessel, may your gift be ours; delight in the praise of the worshipful poet; since men, the priests, delight in you in turn for fame and strength.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

सुदानू (*sudānū*) — O Aśvin deities, who give good fruits. यत् (*yat*) — for which reason. चर्षणयः (*carṣaṇayaḥ*) — men in the form of priests. सुवीराय्य (*suvīrāyya*) — (for the sacrificer, or) for excellent fruit. श्रवस्या (*śravasyā*) — with desire for food. वां (*vāṃ*) — you two. अनु मदंति (*anu madaṃti*) — they satisfy in order (therefore). मान्यस्य (*mānyasya*) — of the esteemed one; कारोः (*kāroḥ*) — and praiser, Agastya's. स्तोमं (*stomaṃ*) — praise. हिनोतं (*hinotaṃ*) — hear and be satisfied. माध्वी (*mādhvī*) — O Aśvin deities possessing a vessel full of honey. वां (*vāṃ*) — coming from you. सा (*sā*) — that famous (gift). रातिः (*rātiḥ*) — the gift. अस्मे (*asme*) — to us. अस्तु (*astu*) — may it be.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Aśvin deities, who give good fruits: since men in the form of priests, for the sake of excellent fruit and out of desire for food, satisfy you two in turn, therefore hear the praise of Agastya, the esteemed praiser, and be satisfied. O Aśvin deities possessing a vessel full of honey, may that famous gift coming from you be ours.

**Printed English translation**

Receivers of ihe [sic] oblation, may your liberality be displayed towards us : be pleased with the hymn of the venerable author, so that men may honour the institutor of the ceremony, as (they honour) you, liberal donors, for the sake of renown.

### Printed p. 36

*Running head: [36 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 184].*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

सुदानू (*sudānū*) — "those who give good fruits", "givers of auspicious fruits": the quality of the Aśvin deities is thus praised. The word दानुशब्दवु (*dānuśabdavu*) is used in many places in the sense of "sap, juice". सुदानवः (*sudānavaḥ*) can also mean "full of juice" or "givers of juice". When this word is used as an epithet of the Maruts it ordinarily means "givers of juice", that is, "those who pour excellent rain water".

पिन्वंत्यपो मरुतः सुदानवः पयो घतवद्विदथेष्वाभुवः । (*pinvaṃtyapo marutaḥ sudānavaḥ payo ghatavadvidatheṣvābhuvaḥ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 1-64-6)

In many such places it gives the above meaning for the Maruts. Moreover, generous giving may be not only the deities' act but also the quality of sacrificers.

इषं पृंचंता सुकृते सुदानव आ बर्हिः सीदतं नरा । (*iṣaṃ pṛṃcaṃtā sukṛte sudānava ā barhiḥ sīdataṃ narā |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 1-47-8)

सेदग्ने अस्तु सुभगः सुदानुर्यस्त्वा नित्येन हविषा य उक्षैः । (*sedagne astu subhagaḥ sudānuryastvā nityena haviṣā ya ukṣaiḥ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 4-4-7)

In ṛks like these everywhere it is an epithet of sacrificers as excellent donors. In ṛks such as 18-43-7 [as supplied] and others, दानु (*dānu*) may also mean soma juice. Moreover,

उत्तरा सूरधरः पुत्र आसिद्दानुः शये सहवत्सा न धेनुः । (*uttarā sūradharaḥ putra āsiddānuḥ śaye sahavatsā na dhenuḥ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 1-32-9)

in ṛks like these the word दानु (*dānu*) means the demoness, the mother of Vṛtra. The special sense of this word, the etymology of the word जीरदानु (*jīradānu*) and its special uses were told earlier.

चर्षणय्य (*carṣaṇayya*) — when explaining the meaning of the word ऋक्किनल्लिरुव (*ṛkkinalliruva*) in the ṛk 1-109-5, Sāyaṇa took the sense सर्वस्य द्रष्टारौ इंद्राग्नी (*sarvasya draṣṭārau iṃdrāgnī*) ("the two seers of everything, Indra and Agni"). Skandasvāmin also gave this meaning first, and then, by मनुष्यनाम वा चर्षणीशब्दः (*manuṣyanāma vā carṣaṇīśabdaḥ*) (Ni 3-7) मनुष्याकारौ ना द्रष्टारौ ना सर्वार्थानां (*manuṣyākārau nā draṣṭārau nā sarvārthānāṃ*), since the word *carṣaṇi* is read among the names of man, he took it that they may be, in human form, the seers of everything. Again, in the ṛk 1-109-6, Sāyaṇa took the word चर्षणि (*carṣaṇi*) to mean simply "men".

### Printed p. 37

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 1] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 37.*

Monier Williams says that it is derived from the root कृष् (*kṛṣ*), and Griffith from the root चर् (*car*); this is explained in detail in Ṛgveda Saṃhitā part 2, page 4.

मान्यस्य (*mānyasya*) — the special sense of this word has been told in detail in part 12 of the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā (page 725, as supplied). There, in the ṛk 1-165-15, Sāyaṇa took the word to mean "worshipful", and it is used as an epithet of Agastya. In the Bṛhaddevatā, telling the order of Agastya's origin, it says:

मानेन सम्मितो यस्मात् तस्मान्मान्य इहोच्यते । (*mānena sammito yasmāt tasmānmānya ihocyate |*)
यद्वा कुंभादृषिर्जातः कुंभेनापि हि मीयते ॥ (*yadvā kuṃbhādṛṣirjātaḥ kuṃbhenāpi hi mīyate ‖*)

(Bṛ. De. 5-153)

that is, because he is worshipful, or because he was born in the pitcher which shows the kind of measure (*māna*), that is, in the pitcher-form measure, the name "Mānya" for Agastya is famous. The epithets मांदार्यस्य, (*māṃdāryasya,*) and मान्यस्य (*mānyasya*) for Agastya occur in many places. Max Müller, explaining the ṛk 1-165-15, has said thus:

I translate मान्य (*mānya*), the son of मान (*māna*), because the poet is so called in I-189-8
is in all probability the same as our मांदार्य (*māṃdārya*), मान्य (*mānya*). But it may also be मान्य (*mānya*),
descendant of मांदारि (*māṃdāri*).
(S. B. E XXXII. P. 206)

The English scholars hold that the personal name मान (*māna*) is the name of the first man of a line. For example, Keith also says as follows:

मान (*māna*) is the name of man occuring in several passages of the Rig-veda.
In one place, express mention is made of his son (सूनु (*sūnu*)) by whom अगस्त्य (*agastya*) must
have been meant. In another passage, apparently the same meaning applies
to मान (*māna*) — ie. अगस्त्य (*agastya*) as मान (*māna*).
[ Index to Vedic names and subjects by Madconell & Keith. Vol. II. p. 152.]

That is to say,

अपोचाम निवचनान्यस्मिन्मानस्य सूनुः सहसाने अग्नौ ॥ (*apocāma nivacanānyasminmānasya sūnuḥ sahasāne agnau ‖*)
वयं सहस्रमृषिभिः सनेम विद्यामेषं वृजनं जीरदानुं ॥ (*vayaṃ sahasramṛṣibhiḥ sanema vidyāmeṣaṃ vṛjanaṃ jīradānuṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 1-189-8)

*[The window ends here; the Special Topics continue on the next page.]*

### Printed p. 38

*Running head: [38 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 184]. The window begins inside the Special Topics of Ṛk 4, after the quotation of the ṛk 1-189-8 that ended p. 37.*

In the ṛk (the one just quoted), "mānasya sūnuḥ" means, in the opinion of the English scholars, Agastya, the son of the sage named Māna. But Sāyaṇa, in explaining the meaning of this word in the same ṛk, has commented thus:

मानस्य सूनुः ; मीयत इति मानः मंत्रः । तस्यसूनुः अग्निः मंत्रेणउत्पा- (*mānasya sūnuḥ ; mīyata iti mānaḥ maṃtraḥ | tasyasūnuḥ agniḥ maṃtreṇautpā-*)
द्यमानत्वात् । मानस्य सूनौ अग्नौ ॥ (*dyamānatvāt | mānasya sūnau agnau ‖*)

((म. भा. (*(ma. bhā.*) 1-189-8 [as supplied: the print has "ಮ. ಭಾ." for the usual "ಋ. ಸಂ."])

"Māna" means mantra; its son is Agni. Therefore "mānasya sūnuḥ" means Agni, he has explained. Likewise,

सत्रे ह जाताविषिता नमोभिः कुंभे रेतः सिषिचतुः समानं । (*satre ha jātāviṣitā namobhiḥ kuṃbhe retaḥ siṣicatuḥ samānaṃ |*)
ततो ह मान उदियाय मध्यात्ततो जातमृषिमाहुर्वसिष्ठं ॥ (*tato ha māna udiyāya madhyāttato jātamṛṣimāhurvasiṣṭhaṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 7-33-13)

here too, although the sense is Agastya, Sāyaṇa has not stated that मान (*māna*) is a proper name. Therefore wherever the word मान्यस्य (*mānyasya*) occurs, there is a difference between what the English scholars take and what Sāyaṇa explains. In the present ṛk also it can mean either Agastya, the son of Māna, or Agastya the esteemed and worshipful.

कारोः (*kāroḥ*) — the sense given is स्तोतुः अगस्त्यस्य (*stotuḥ agastyasya*), "of the praiser Agastya". The Nirukta authors have read the word कारुशब्दवन्नु (*kāruśabdavannu*) among the names of praise (Ni, 3-19). The authors of the Bṛhaddevatā have used this word in the sense "poets, seers of subtle things (rishis)":

अग्नेस्तु यानि सूक्तानि पंचनामानि कारवः । (*agnestu yāni sūktāni paṃcanāmāni kāravaḥ |*)
षड्विंशतिस्तथेंद्रस्य प्राहुः सूर्यस्य सप्त च ॥ (*ṣaḍviṃśatistatheṃdrasya prāhuḥ sūryasya sapta ca ‖*)

(Bṛ. De. 2-22)

पृथक्त्वेन समासैस्तु यज्ञे यच्छस्यते नृभिः । (*pṛthaktvena samāsaistu yajñe yacchasyate nṛbhiḥ |*)
स्तुवंत्याप्रीषु तेनेमं नराशंसं तु कारवः ॥ (*stuvaṃtyāprīṣu tenemaṃ narāśaṃsaṃ tu kāravaḥ ‖*)

(Bṛ. De. 2-28)

In these sentences, the word कारुगळु (*kārugaḷu*) is used as a word synonymous with "poet", with the sense "they praise" (as printed "ಎ.ದು"). In the Ṛksaṃhitā also,

प्रदक्षिणिदभि गृणंति कारवो वयो वदंत ऋतुथा शकुंतयः । (*pradakṣiṇidabhi gṛṇaṃti kāravo vayo vadaṃta ṛtuthā śakuṃtayaḥ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-43-1)

in ṛks like these also the use of this word in the sense "poet" was told earlier at length.

### Printed p. 39

*Running head: [೨. ೨. ಅ. ೫ ವ. ೧] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 39 (the print has "೨." for the first "ಅ.", as supplied).*

*[As supplied, the label line reads व्याकरणक्रिया]* (*vyākaraṇakriyā]**) for "Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā".]*

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page, noted briefly)

अस्मे (*asme*): by सुपां सुलुक् (*supāṃ suluk*) the substitute *śe* for the genitive/locative plural ending. माध्वी (*mādhvī*): by मधोरणि च (*madhoraṇi ca*) (Pā. Sū. 4-129 [as printed]) the suffix *añ* after *madhu* in the sense of possession; before it, by ऋत्त्ववास्त्व (*ṛttvavāstva*) (Pā. Sū. 6-4-175) the substitution *yaṇ* is irregularly stated in place of *guṇa*; the vocative *nighāta* accent arises. रातिः (*rātiḥ*): by मंत्रेवृषेषपच (*maṃtrevṛṣeṣapaca*) (Pā. Sū. 3-3-96) the suffix *ktin* takes the acute. हिनोतम् (*hinotam*) (root हि (*hi*) in the sense of going and increase), imperative, second person dual: by तप्तनप्तन (*taptanaptana*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-45) the substitute *tam*; by स्वादिभ्यःश्नुः (*svādibhyaḥśnuḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-73) the suffix *śnu*, *guṇa* because it has the mark *p*; the *nighāta* accent because it follows a non-verb. कारोः (*kāroḥ*) (root डुकृञ् करणे (*ḍukṛñ karaṇe*) in the sense of making): the suffix *uṇ* by कृवापा (*kṛvāpā*) (Uṇ. Sū. 1-1), *vṛddhi* of the *ṛ* by अचोञ्णिति (*acoñṇiti*); the accent of the suffix, last-syllable acute. श्रवस्या (*śravasyā*): from श्रवःआत्मनः (*śravaḥātmanaḥ*) "he wishes his own fame", by सुपआत्मनःक्यच् (*supaātmanaḥkyac*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-8) the suffix *kyac*; then the suffix *a*, feminine *ṭāp*; the instrumental singular, by सुपां सुलुक् (*supāṃ suluk*) the lengthening to the preceding class vowel. सुवीर्याय (*suvīryāya*): "he who has good vigour"; by वीरवीर्यौच (*vīravīryauca*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-120) the accent of the later word's first syllable overrides the accent of *su*; dative singular. चर्षणयः (*carṣaṇayaḥ*) (root कृष (*kṛṣa*) in the sense of ploughing): by कृषेरादेश्वचः (*kṛṣerādeśvacaḥ*) (Uṇ. Sū. 261 [as printed]) the suffix *ani*, *ca* in place of the first *k*, *guṇa*; by अट्- कुप्वाज् (*aṭ- kupvāj*) the *ṇ* substitution; nominative plural. मदंति (*madaṃti*) (root मदी (*madī*) in the sense of joy and smearing): present, third person plural; because of the *yat* connection the *nighāta* accent does not arise; by तास्यनुदात्तेत् (*tāsyanudāttet*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-186) the present tense ending is unaccented, so the accent of the root remains.

*[A stray line ":0:" follows in the supplied text.]*

### Printed p. 40

*Running head: [40 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 184]. A stray line कन्नड साहित्य (*kannaḍa sāhitya*) ("Kannada literature") stands at the head of the page.*

#### Ṛk 5

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> ए॒ष वां॒ स्तोमो॑ अश्विनावकारि॒ माने॑भिर्मघवाना सुवृ॒क्ति ।
> या॒तं व॒र्तिस्तन॑याय॒ त्मने॑ चा॒गस्ये॑ नासत्या॒ मदं॑ता ॥ ५ ॥

> *eṣa vāṃ stomo aśvināvakāri mānebhirmaghavānā suvṛkti |*
> *yātaṃ vartistanayāya tmane cāgasye nāsatyā madaṃtā ‖ 5 ‖*

*[The Saṃhitā has । (*|*) where the Pada has सुवृक्ति (*suvṛkti*), and prints चागस्ये (*cāgasye*) where the Pada has अगस्त्ये (*agastye*); kept as supplied.]*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> एषः । वां । स्तोमः । अश्विनौ । अ कारि । मानेभिः । मघवाना । सुवृक्ति ।
> यातं । वर्तिः । तनयाय । त्मने । च । अगस्त्ये । नासत्या । मदंता ॥ ५ ॥

> *eṣaḥ | vāṃ | stomaḥ | aśvinau | a kāri | mānebhiḥ | maghavānā | suvṛkti |*
> *yātaṃ | vartiḥ | tanayāya | tmane | ca | agastye | nāsatyā | madaṃtā ‖ 5 ‖*

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads स. यणभाष्यं (*sa. yaṇabhāṣyaṃ*); standard label inserted.]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे अश्विन् हे मघवाना हनिर्लक्षणान्नवंतौ वां युवाभ्यामेष स्तोमः
> स्तोत्रं सुवृक्ति सुष्टु पापवर्जनं यथा भवति तथा यद्वा सुसमाप्त्यकारि । कृतः ।
> कीदृशोऽयं । मानेभिर्मानैर्हविष्प्रदानरूपैः सहितः । हे नासत्यासत्यरहितौ
> युवामगस्य एतन्नामि महर्षौ मयि मदंता माद्यंतौ संतौ वर्तिर्गृहं
> यज्ञसंबंधि यातं । प्राप्पुतं । किमर्थं । तनयाय पुत्रादिलाभाय त्मन आत्मने
> च हिताय ॥

*Gist:* O Aśvins, endowed with oblation-food: this praise, sin-removing and complete, with gifts of offerings, has been made for you; come to my sacrificial house, delighted with me, Agastya, for sons and my welfare.

*[The label line of the next section reads, as supplied, १ प्रतिपवार्ध १ (*1 pratipavārdha 1*) (inside the bhāṣya block); standard label inserted.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

मघवाना (*maghavānā*) — those endowed with food in the form of oblations. अश्विनौ (*aśvinau*) — O Aśvin deities. वार (*vāra*) — for you (as printed). मानेभिः (*mānebhiḥ*) — accompanied by the giving of oblations. सुवृक्ति (*suvṛkti*) — having the power to remove sin (or complete). विषः स्तोमः (*viṣaḥ stomaḥ*) — this praise (the supplied text has the stray word before it). आकारि (*ākāri*) — has been made. नासत्या (*nāsatyā*) — O Aśvin deities, free from untruth (you two). अगस्त्ये (*agastye*) — in me, famed as Agastya (by my praise). मदंता (*madaṃtā*) — having obtained satisfaction. तनयाय (*tanayāya*) — for the gain of sons and the like. त्मने च (*tmane ca*) — and for my own welfare. वर्तिः (*vartiḥ*) — to the house of the sacrifice. यातं (*yātaṃ*) — come.

*[The label line of the next section reads, as supplied, भानुवार (*bhānuvāra*) (inside the Pratipadārtha block); standard label inserted.]*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Aśvin deities, endowed with food in the form of oblations: for you a praise has been made, accompanied by the giving of oblations, with the power to remove sin and complete. O Aśvin deities, free from untruth: delighted by my praise, of me who am famed as Agastya, come to my sacrificial house, for the gain of sons and the like and for my own welfare.

### Printed p. 41

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 1.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 41.*

*[As supplied, the label line of the printed English reads "English Trnslation" and stood inside a quote block; the standard label has been inserted.]*

**Printed English translation**

Ashwins, possessors of wealth, this sin-removing hymn has been addressed to you, together with respectful (oblations): Nasatyas, be favourable to Agastya ; come to his dwelling for (conferring on him) progeny, and for his own (good).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

मघवाना (*maghavānā*) — for this epithet Sāyaṇa has given the sense हविर्लक्षणान्नयुक्तौ (*havirlakṣaṇānnayuktau*), "endowed with food in the form of an oblation" (the Aśvin deities). Ordinarily the epithet मघवान् (*maghavān*) refers to Indra, but even when the qualities of other deities are described, this epithet is used here and there in the Ṛksaṃhitā. Or, since the word मघ (*magha*) is read among the names of wealth (Ni. 3-9), here it can be called wealth in the form of food. The Nirukta authors, having stated the derivation of the word मघ (*magha*) by मघमिति धननामधेयं महतेर्दानकर्मणः (*maghamiti dhananāmadheyaṃ mahaterdānakarmaṇaḥ*), have explained the sense of the word मघोनी (*maghonī*) (Ni. 1-7). In the same way, in the present ṛk too an etymology can be given for the epithet मघवाना (*maghavānā*). Or:

If मघवाना (*maghavānā*) is taken to mean "the Aśvin deities accompanied by Viṣṇu", that will not be wrong either. How: the identity of Viṣṇu and Makha, stated in the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa and elsewhere, has been told earlier (Śa. Brā. 14-1 1-13). The companionship of the Aśvin deities with Viṣṇu, who has the form of Makha, is famous. This too has been told earlier,

तमस्य राजा वरुणस्तमश्विना क्रतुं सचंत मारुतस्य वेधसः । (*tamasya rājā varuṇastamaśvinā kratuṃ sacaṃta mārutasya vedhasaḥ |*)
धधार दक्षमुत्तममहर्विदं व्रजं च विष्णुः सखिवा अपोर्णुते ॥ (*dhadhāra dakṣamuttamamaharvidaṃ vrajaṃ ca viṣṇuḥ sakhivā aporṇute ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 1-156-4)

in explaining the special topics of this ṛk (Ṛgveda Saṃhitā, part 11; pp. 656-657). Moreover, in the Ṛksaṃhitā itself this companionship is described thus:

यदिंद्रेण सरथं याथो अश्विना यद्वा वायुना भवथः समोकसा । (*yadiṃdreṇa sarathaṃ yātho aśvinā yadvā vāyunā bhavathaḥ samokasā |*)
यदादित्येभिर्यभुभिः सजोषसा यद्वा विष्णोर्विक्रमणेषु तिष्ठथः ॥ (*yadādityebhiryabhubhiḥ sajoṣasā yadvā viṣṇorvikramaṇeṣu tiṣṭhathaḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 8-9-12)

### Printed p. 42

*Running head: [42 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū 184].*

In ṛks like these too the companionship of the Aśvin deities with Viṣṇu is stated. For this reason it is possible to take मघवाना (*maghavānā*) to mean "the Aśvin deities accompanied by Viṣṇu". But in the present context it seems fitting to the occasion to take the sense stated by Sāyaṇa, "endowed with the wealth of food in the form of an oblation".

सुवृक्ति (*suvṛkti*) — सुष्टु पापवर्जनं यथाभवति तथा । यद्वा । (*suṣṭu pāpavarjanaṃ yathābhavati tathā | yadvā |*) (Kannada: complete freedom from sin, or having a pure conclusion). This word is an epithet of the word for *stoma*. This epithet is used in one sense in one place and in another sense in another. The Nirukta authors, citing

इयं शुष्मेभिर्बिसखा इवारुजत्सानु गिरीणां तविषेभिरूर्मिभिः । (*iyaṃ śuṣmebhirbisakhā ivārujatsānu girīṇāṃ taviṣebhirūrmibhiḥ |*)
पारावतघ्नीमवसे सुवृक्तिभिः सरस्वतीमा विवासेम धीतिभिः ॥ (*pārāvataghnīmavase suvṛktibhiḥ sarasvatīmā vivāsema dhītibhiḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 6. 61. 2)

have taken the word सुवृक्तिभिः (*suvṛktibhiḥ*) there as सुप्रवृत्ताभिः शोभनाभिः स्तुतिभिः (*supravṛttābhiḥ śobhanābhiḥ stutibhiḥ*), that is, simply as excellent praise. The word नमोव्यक्तिं (*namovyaktiṃ*) (printed so) is also used in other places in the sense of abandoning. For example,

कुविदंग यवमंतो यवं चिद्यथा दांत्यनुपूर्वं वियूय । (*kuvidaṃga yavamaṃto yavaṃ cidyathā dāṃtyanupūrvaṃ viyūya |*)
इहेहैषां कृणुहि भोजनानि ये बर्हिषो नमोवृक्तिं न जग्मुः ॥ (*ihehaiṣāṃ kṛṇuhi bhojanāni ye barhiṣo namovṛktiṃ na jagmuḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 10-131-2)

here, in नमोव्यक्तिं नमसः (*namovyaktiṃ namasaḥ*) and so on to जग्मुः (*jagmuḥ*): "they did not abandon the praise in the form of salutation": thus Indra is prayed to. Here the word व्यक्तिशब्दवु (*vyaktiśabdavu*) is used in the sense of abandoning. But its use in the sense of praise is far more frequent. For example,

अस्मा इदु त्यमुपमं स्वर्षां भराम्यांगूषमास्येन । (*asmā idu tyamupamaṃ svarṣāṃ bharāmyāṃgūṣamāsyena |*)
मंहिष्ठमच्छोक्तिभिर्मतीनां सुवृक्तिभिः सूरिं वावृधध्यै ॥ (*maṃhiṣṭhamacchoktibhirmatīnāṃ suvṛktibhiḥ sūriṃ vāvṛdhadhyai ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ ಸಂ] 1-61-3)

In places like this everywhere the sense is "excellent praise".

वर्तिः (*vartiḥ*) — Sāyaṇa has taken the sense "a house serving as a basis for dwelling" in all places where this word occurs. But Skandasvāmin has generally everywhere taken the sense "a well-known path". For example,

### Printed p. 43

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 1.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 43.*

त्रिवर्तिर्यातं त्रिरनुव्रते जने त्रिः सुप्राव्ये त्रेधेव शिक्षतं । (*trivartiryātaṃ triranuvrate jane triḥ suprāvye tredheva śikṣataṃ |*)
त्रिर्नांद्यं वहतमश्विना युवं त्रिः पृक्षो अस्मे अक्षरेव पिन्वतं ॥ (*trirnāṃdyaṃ vahatamaśvinā yuvaṃ triḥ pṛkṣo asme akṣareva pinvataṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1. 34. 4.)

In all ṛks like this, for the word वर्तियॆंबुदक्कॆ (*vartiyèṃbudakkè*) Sāyaṇa has given the sense "house" and Skandasvāmin the sense "path". It is one special point that all the sūktas having ṛks in which the word occurs in forms such as त्रिवर्तिः, (*trivartiḥ,*) and अश्विनावर्तिः (*aśvināvartiḥ*) (1. 116. 18; 1. 117. 2; 1. 119. 4) are addressed to the Aśvin deities. But,

इषं दुहन्तु दुघां विश्वधायसं यज्ञप्रिये यजमानाय सुक्रतो । (*iṣaṃ duhantu dughāṃ viśvadhāyasaṃ yajñapriye yajamānāya sukrato |*)
अग्ने धृतस्नु स्त्रिऋतानि दीद्यद्वर्तिर्यज्ञं परियॆन्तु क्रतूयसे ॥ (*agne dhṛtasnu striṛtāni dīdyadvartiryajñaṃ pariyèntu kratūyase ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10. 122. 6)

only in this ṛk, a mantra addressed to Agni, does this word occur. Here it is explained as the house of the sacrifice. Sāyaṇa too has in some places taken the sense "path" for this word. For example,

यो ह स्य वां रथिरा वस्त उस्रा रथो युजानः परियाति वर्तिः । (*yo ha sya vāṃ rathirā vasta usrā ratho yujānaḥ pariyāti vartiḥ |*)
तेन नः शंयोरुषसो व्युष्टौ न्यश्विना वहतं यज्ञे अस्मिन् ॥ (*tena naḥ śaṃyoruṣaso vyuṣṭau nyaśvinā vahataṃ yajñe asmin ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7. 69. [the reference is cut off in the supplied text]) (Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 7. 69. 5.)

here वर्तिः (*vartiḥ*) is explained as मार्गं (*mārgaṃ*) ("path"). This is a mantra praying to the Aśvin deities themselves. In the present ṛk the sense is "house". Ordinarily this is a synonym of the word for the house of the sacrifice.

*[As supplied, the label line of the following section reads न्याशनप्रक्रिया]* (*nyāśanaprakriyā]**); standard label inserted.]*

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page, noted briefly)

अकारि (*akāri*) (root डुकृण्करणे (*ḍukṛṇkaraṇe*) in the sense of doing): passive aorist, third person singular; the *nighāta* accent of a finite verb arises. मानेभिः (*mānebhiḥ*) (root मन (*mana*), "to know"): the suffix *ghañ*; by अतउपधायाः (*ataupadhāyāḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 7-2-116) *vṛddhi* of the penultimate vowel; because of the mark *ñ* the first syllable is acute; before *bhis*, by बहुलं छंदसि (*bahulaṃ chaṃdasi*) the substitute *ais* is optional, so it does not occur, and *ē* appears. मघवाना (*maghavānā*): by छंदसीवनिपौ (*chaṃdasīvanipau*) the suffix *vanip* in the sense of possession; in the vocative dual, by सुपां सुलुक् (*supāṃ suluk*) the substitute *ā*; the vocative *nighāta* accent. सुवृक्ति (*suvṛkti*) (root व्यजी (*vyajī*), "to abandon"): in the abstract sense the suffix *ktin*; by चोःकुः (*coḥkuḥ*) the *j* becomes *k* (*kutva*); by खरिच (*kharica*) *cartva*; "that in which there is good abandonment, in such an action"; by नञ्सुभ्याम् (*nañsubhyām*) (Pā. Sū. 2-172, printed "೨-೧೭೨") the last syllable of the later member is acute.

### Printed p. 44

*Running head: [44 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 184]. The window begins within the grammar page of Ṛk 5, continuing p. 43.*

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (continued; grammar page, noted briefly)

यातम् (*yātam*) (root या (*yā*) in the sense of going): imperative, second person dual; because it stands at the beginning of the pāda there is no *nighāta* accent; the accent of the suffix, last-syllable acute. वर्ति: (*varti:*) (root वृतु (*vṛtu*) in the sense of turning): by हृपिषिरुहि (*hṛpiṣiruhi*) (Uṇ. Sū. 4-558) the suffix *i*; *guṇa* of the root's short penultimate vowel, followed by *r*. त्मने (*tmane*): by मंत्रेष्वाज्यादेरात्मनः (*maṃtreṣvājyāderātmanaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-4-141) the elision of the initial *ā*. मदंता (*madaṃtā*) (root *mad*, in the sense of satisfaction): the present-participle substitute for the tense suffix; by interchange the connecting suffix *śap*; because the present-tense suffix is unaccented the accent of the root remains; for the dual, by *sulug* of the case endings the substitute *ā*.

*[A stray line ":0:" follows in the supplied text.]*

#### Ṛk 6

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अता॑रिष्म॒ तम॑सस्पा॒रम॒स्य प्रति॑ वां॒ स्तोमो॑ अश्विनावधायि ।
> एह या॑तं प॒थिभि॑र्देव॒यानै॑र्वि॒द्यामे॒षं वृ॒जनं॑ जी॒रदा॑नुं ॥ ६ ॥

*[As supplied, the label line of the Pada reads ⟦⟧ inside the Saṃhitā block; the last word of the Saṃhitā, split by a hyphen at the end of a line, has been joined.]*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अतारिष्म । तमसः । पारं । अस्य । प्रति । वां । स्तोमः । अश्विनौ । अधायि ।
> आ । इह । यातं । पथिभिः । देवयानैः । विद्याम । इषं । वृजनं । जीरदानुं ॥ ॥ ६ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> अतारिष्मेशि षष्ठी व्याख्याता ॥

*Gist:* The bhāṣya is a single short line, stating that the form beginning "atāriṣma" and the sixth (*ṣaṣṭhī*) have been explained (as in the earlier bhāṣya); the sense: we have crossed this darkness by your grace.

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads ॥ प्रतिसदर्थ ॥ (*‖ pratisadartha ‖*) (inside the bhāṣya block); standard label inserted.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

अश्विनौ (*aśvinau*) — O Aśvin deities. अस्य तमसः (*asya tamasaḥ*) — (of this suffering, which obstructs welfare). पारं (*pāraṃ*) — the end. अतारिष्म (*atāriṣma*) — (by your grace) we have crossed. नां प्रति (*nāṃ prati*) — (as printed) addressed to you. स्तोमः (*stomaḥ*) — praise. अधायि (*adhāyi*) — has been made (directed). देवयानैःपथिभिः (*devayānaiḥpathibhiḥ*) — by the paths on which the gods travel. इह (*iha*) — to this sacrifice. आ यातं (*ā yātaṃ*) — come. वयं (*vayaṃ*) — we. इषं (*iṣaṃ*) — food. वृजनं (*vṛjanaṃ*) — strength. जीरदानुं (*jīradānuṃ*) — long life. विद्याम (*vidyāma*) — may we obtain.

### Printed p. 45

*Running head: [*[५. (**[5.*). ೨. ಅ. ೫. ವ. ೧.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 45 (the print has "೫." for the first "ಅ.", as supplied). The Pratipadārtha above continues across the page boundary.*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Aśvin deities, we have crossed, by your grace, the end of this suffering which obstructs welfare. This praise is directed to you. Come to this sacrifice by the paths on which the gods travel, that we may obtain food, strength and long life.

**Printed English translation**

(Through your aid), Ashwins, we cross over the limit of darkness : to you this (our hymn) has been addressed : come hither by the path traversed by the gods, that we may obtain food, strength, and long life.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

देवयानैः पथिभिः (*devayānaiḥ pathibhiḥ*) — "by the paths of the gods' travelling", देवगंतवैर्मार्गैः (*devagaṃtavairmārgaiḥ*): a physical meaning is stated. In the Upaniṣads the words *devayāna* and *pitṛyāna* have a technical meaning. But in many places in the Ṛksaṃhitā the word *devayāna* means the path on which the gods travel; only in a few places is the spiritual purport described in the Upaniṣads also indicated. In the Upaniṣads these two words denote the two paths on which the eternal ātman travels after a man's death, when his body has turned to ashes. Of the paths to be taken to experience the fruits of good works, one is *devayāna* and the other *pitṛyāna*: one is the path of the gods and the other the path of the fathers. Those who know the *pañcāgni* knowledge and place their faith in truth set out on the path of the gods and reach the Parabrahman. After the death of these, the ātman enters the flame and thereby the day, the bright fortnight, the northern course, the year, the sun and lightning, and, with the help of the non-human person, obtains the Parabrahman: this is described in the Upaniṣads. Those who satisfy the world merely by sacrifice and gifts, that is, those who without knowing the *pañcāgni* knowledge are engaged only in good works, travel after death by another path: after the body has been burnt the ātman joins the smoke, then the night, thereby

### Printed p. 46

*Running head: [46 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 185] (the print has "೧೮೫" for the sūkta number, as supplied).*

the dark fortnight, thereby the southern course, then the world of the fathers, and finally the world of the moon. That is their final station. Having fully experienced there the fruits corresponding to their good works in this world, remaining with the fathers, when the impression of their action is exhausted they return to the earth by the same path of smoke. This is the nature of the *devayāna* and *pitṛyāna* described in the Upaniṣads.

What we must notice in the present context is whether all the sense described in the Upaniṣads is wholly indicated by the word *devayāna* used in the Ṛksaṃhitā, or whether merely the physical path on which the gods travel is meant. Likewise, what sense the word *pitṛyāna* described in the Upaniṣads has must be considered here.

In the Ṛksaṃhitā the word *devayāna* is used thirteen times in all. [೧-೭೨-೩; ೧. ೧೬೨-೪ ; ೧. ೧೮೩-೬, ೧-೧೮೪-೬ ೪. ೩೭-೧ ; ೫ ೪೫-೬ ; ೭. ೩೮-೮ ೭. ೩೬-೨ ; ೧೦. ೧೮-೧ ; ೧೦. ೫೧-೨ ; ೧೦-೫೧-೫ ; ೧೦. ೯೮-೧೧ ; ೧೦. ೧೮೧-೩.] [the list is printed in square brackets, with spaces after some of the points, as supplied]. In all these contexts Sāyaṇa has mainly given two meanings only. According to his explanation, firstly it is the path by which human sacrificers go with their oblations to approach the gods; secondly the path by which the gods come to the earth to receive the oblations. The word *pitṛyāna* is used in the Ṛksaṃhitā only once. In what context that use is:

यं त्वा द्यावापृथिवी यं त्वापस्त्वष्टा यं त्वा सुजनिमा जजान । (*yaṃ tvā dyāvāpṛthivī yaṃ tvāpastvaṣṭā yaṃ tvā sujanimā jajāna |*)
पंथामनु प्रविद्वान् पितृयाणं द्युमदग्ने समिधानो वि भाहि ॥ (*paṃthāmanu pravidvān pitṛyāṇaṃ dyumadagne samidhāno vi bhāhi ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಯ. ಸಂ.] 10-2-7)

In this ṛk, Agni, who is born of heaven and earth, of the waters, of the creator and of Tvaṣṭṛ, who is kindled, and who knows fully the nature of the *pitṛyāṇa* path, is prayed to shine as soon as he is kindled. For the word in the third quarter of the ṛk Sāyaṇa's explanation is पितृयाणं पितरो येन मार्गेण गच्छंति तं (*pitṛyāṇaṃ pitaro yena mārgeṇa gacchaṃti taṃ*): "the path on which the fathers travel".

### Printed p. 47

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 2] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 47. The window begins at the first page of the next sūkta; the printed text of Sūkta 184 ends with the Special Topics of p. 46, and no separate colophon is printed for it.*

The hundred and eighty-fifth sūkta.

**Sāyaṇa's introduction to the sūkta** (Sanskrit, transcribed; see the Anuvāda below for the sense):

> कतरा पूर्वत्येकादशर्चं षष्ठं सूक्तमागस्त्यं त्रैष्टुभं । कतरैकादश
> द्यावापृथिवीयमित्यनुक्रांतं ॥ अभिप्लविके षष्ठेऽहनि वैश्वदेवशस्त्रे द्यावापृथिव्य-
> निविद्वानमिदं । तृतीयस्येति खंडे सूत्रितं । कतरा पूर्वोषासानक्तेति वैश्वदेवं ।
> आ. ७-७ । इति ॥ महाव्रतेऽपि वैश्वदेवशस्त्र इदमेव द्यावापृथिव्यनिविद्वानं । उत्त-
> मादाभिप्लविकात्तृतीयसवनं ।ऐ. आ ५-३-२ । इत्यतिदेशात् ॥

*Gist:* *katarā pūrvā* is the sixth sūkta of Anuvāka 24, of eleven ṛks, by Agastya, to Dyāvāpṛthivī, in *triṣṭubh*; it is used in the Vaiśvadeva-śastra of the sixth day of the Abhiplava and of the Mahāvrata.

**Anuvāda (Kannada).** The sūkta beginning with कतरा पूर्वा (*katarā pūrvā*) is the sixth sūkta in the twenty-fourth anuvāka. It has eleven ṛks. Agastya is the seer of this sūkta; Dyāvāpṛthivī (Heaven and Earth) is the deity; the metre is *triṣṭubh*. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said कतरैकादश द्यावापृथिवीयं ऎंदु (*kataraikādaśa dyāvāpṛthivīyaṃ èṃdu*) (as supplied). The application is as explained in the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, in the section beginning तृतीयस्य (*tṛtīyasya*), by the sūtra कतरापूर्वोषासानक्केति वैश्वदेवं (*katarāpūrvoṣāsānakketi vaiśvadevaṃ*) (Aś. 7-7 [as printed]): this sūkta is applied to the *nivid* mantras praising Heaven and Earth when the mantras of the Vaiśvadeva-śastra are recited on the sixth day of the Abhiplava sacrifice. And in the Mahāvrata, when the Vaiśvadeva-śastra mantras are recited, the application of this sūkta to the *nivid* mantras concerning Heaven and Earth is specified by the sentence of the Aitareya Āraṇyaka (Ai. 5-3-2) उत्तमादाभिप्लविकात्कृतीयसवनं (*uttamādābhiplavikātkṛtīyasavanaṃ*).
