#### Ṛk 1

*[The Saṃhitā and Pada of this ṛk were a picture in the supplied text; the picture was read and agrees with the machine text, which has been used with the lines joined.]*

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> क॒त॒रा पूर्वा॑ कत॒राप॑रा॒योः क॒था जा॒ते क॑वयः॒ को वि वे॑द ।
> विश्वं॒ त्मना॑ बिभृतो॒ यद्ध॒ नाम॒ वि व॑र्तेते॒ अह॑नी च॒क्रि ये॑व ॥ १ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> कतरा । पूर्वा । कतरा । अपरा । अयोः । कथा । जाते इति । कवयः । कः । वि वेद ।
> विश्वं । तना । बिभृतः । यत् । ह । नाम । वि । वर्तेते इति । अहनी इति । चक्रिया इव ॥ १ ॥

> *katarā | pūrvā | katarā | aparā | ayoḥ | kathā | jāte iti | kavayaḥ | kaḥ | vi veda |*
> *viśvaṃ | tanā | bibhṛtaḥ | yat | ha | nāma | vi | vartete iti | ahanī iti | cakriyā iva ‖ 1 ‖*

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads सयणभाष्यं (*sayaṇabhāṣyaṃ*) (inside the Pada block); standard label inserted.]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> अयोरेनयोर्द्यावापृथिव्योर्मध्यॆ कतरा पूर्वा पूर्वमुत्पन्ना । कतरा
> वापरा पश्चाद्भाविनी । एवं पौर्वापर्यप्रश्नः । उभयोरविनाभावेन सहैव वर्त-
> मानत्वादिति भावः । तथा कथा केन हेतुना जाते । किमनयोरुत्पादनमित्यर्थः ।
> हे कवयः क्रांतदर्शिनोऽतींद्रियज्ञाः यूयं वदत को वि वेद। पौर्वापर्य०
> कारणं च विशेषेण विविच्य वा जानाति । न केनापि ज्ञायत इत्यर्थः । अज्ञाने कारण-
> माह । यद्ध यस्मात्पलु हेतोर्नाम प्रसिद्धं विश्वं कृत्समपि जगदविशेषेण त्मनात्म
> नैवान्यनैरपेक्ष्येण बिभृतः धारयतः । यद्वा । यद्द नाम यत्किंचित्वदार्थजात-
> मस्ति विश्वं तत्सर्वमात्मना बिभृतः । अनेन यत्कारणं ब्रूमस्तदप्याभ्यामेव भियत
> इति कारणाभावः प्रतिपादितः । उत्तरेण पौर्वापर्यभाव उच्यते । अहनी एते
> द्यावापृथिव्यौ चक्रियेव चक्रयुक्ते इव वि वर्तेते । अत्र कतरा पूर्वा कतरास-
> रैनयोरित्यादि निरुक्तं द्रष्टव्यं । नि. ३-२२ ॥

*Gist:* Which of heaven and earth came first, which later, and how were they born? O seers, who knows? They uphold the universe unaided; day and night revolve like wheeled things.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

अयोः (*ayoḥ*) — of these two, heaven and earth. कतरा (*katarā*) — which. पूर्वा (*pūrvā*) — was born first. कतरा (*katarā*) — which. अपरा (*aparā*) — was born afterwards. कथा (*kathā*) — from what material cause. जाते (*jāte*) — these two were born. कवयः (*kavayaḥ*) — O seers who see beyond ordinary sight. कः (*kaḥ*) — who indeed. वि वेद (*vi veda*) — has fully known this principle. यद्ध (*yaddha*) — whatever aggregate of things can be called their cause. विश्वं (*viśvaṃ*) — the whole universe comprising all those things. त्मना (*tmanā*) — (heaven and earth, needing no other help) by themselves. बिभृतः (*bibhṛtaḥ*) — uphold. अहनी (*ahanī*) — (belonging to these) day and night also. चक्रिया इव (*cakriyā iva*) — as if provided with wheels. वि वर्तेते (*vi vartete*) — are revolving in the form of recurrence.

### Printed p. 49

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 2.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 49. The Pratipadārtha continues across the page boundary.*

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads भाषार्ध]* (*bhāṣārdha]**); standard label inserted.]*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

Which of these two, Heaven and Earth, was born first? Which was born afterwards? From what material cause were these two born? O seers who see beyond ordinary sight, who indeed has fully known this principle? The whole universe, comprising all the things that can be called the cause of these, Heaven and Earth uphold by themselves, without needing any other help; and day and night, which belong to them, revolve in the form of recurrence as if provided with wheels.

**Printed English translation**

Which of these two, (Heaven and Earth), is prior, which posterior ? how were they engendered ? (declare), sages, who knows this ? Verily, you uphold the Universe of itself, and the days (and nights) revolve as if they had wheels.

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads विजे विषयगळु]* (*vije viṣayagaḷu]**); standard label inserted.]*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In stating the application of this sūkta, Sāyaṇa cites अभिप्लविके षष्ठेऽहनि वैश्वदेवशस्त्रे द्यावापृथिव्यनिविद्दानमिदं” (*abhiplavike ṣaṣṭhe 'hani vaiśvadevaśastre dyāvāpṛthivyanividdānamidaṃ”*) (as printed) and “महाव्रतेपि वैश्वदेवशस्त्रे इदमेव द्यावा- पथिव्यनिविद्दानं ” (*“mahāvratepi vaiśvadevaśastre idameva dyāvā- pathivyanividdānaṃ ”*) (as printed), and the Āśvalāyana sūtra ((आ-श्रौ-७-७) (*(ā-śrau-7-7)*)), saying that the recitation is prescribed for the Vaiśvadeva-śastra both in the Abhiplava and in the Mahāvrata. If mantras praising Heaven and Earth are to be recited at the time of the rite called Abhiplava and at the Mahāvrata, one must know what these two rites are and what the importance of reciting these mantras at that time is. The root प्लु (*plu*) meaning "to go", with the prefix अभि (*abhi*), gives the sense "to cross", and its use in this sense is quite usual. In the same way, when the word अभिप्लव (*abhiplava*) has a technical use connected with a rite, it gives the same purport, the śruti (the sentence continues on the next page).

*[A stray line "[4] Vol. 14", a page-footer artefact, ends p. 49 in the supplied text.]*

### Printed p. 50

*Running head: [50 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 185]. The window begins inside the Special Topics of Ṛk 1, in the account of the word *abhiplava*, continuing p. 49.*

… is understood from the sentences themselves. Having learned how the explanation of this word is stated in the Brāhmaṇas and elsewhere, let us then examine what the form of this rite is.

ते देवा एतमभिप्लवं समभरन् । तेनाभ्यप्लवंत । तदभिप्लवस्याभिप्लवत्वं । (*te devā etamabhiplavaṃ samabharan | tenābhyaplavaṃta | tadabhiplavasyābhiplavatvaṃ |*)

(Jaiminīya Brāhmaṇa. II. 440. 3 [printed in Latin letters and digits])

By these sentences the Jaiminīya Brāhmaṇa states the purport of the word *abhiplava*. When the asuras of the group called Paṇis carried off the cows, Indra sent the divine bitch Saramā to search for them. She found the place of those asuras and held a conversation with them, and afterwards told the gods the news: this matter is told elsewhere. Some hold the opinion that this same Saramā is described in some places as having acted in favour of the gods, and in others as having acted from self-interest in a way that caused opposition among the gods. In the present context, in the sentence of the Jaiminīya Brāhmaṇa cited above it is said that with the help of Saramā the gods crossed their difficulty and, because she helped their *abhiplava*, performed the Abhiplava sacrifice. The purport and the purpose are given thus: having performed this sacrifice they crossed their calamity, and therefore it has the name *abhiplava*. (The supplied text has "ಎಂದಿರೋಣದರಿಂದಲೇ" here, as supplied.) In the same way,

त आदित्याः । चतुर्भिस्तोमैश्चतुर्भिः पृष्टैर्लघुभिः सामभिः (*ta ādityāḥ | caturbhistomaiścaturbhiḥ pṛṣṭairlaghubhiḥ sāmabhiḥ*)
स्वर्गं लोकमभ्यप्लवंत यदभ्यप्लवंत तस्मादभिप्लवाः । (*svargaṃ lokamabhyaplavaṃta yadabhyaplavaṃta tasmādabhiplavāḥ |*)

(Śa. Brā. 12 2-2-10)

in the days of the Abhiplava sacrifice, by reciting the four *pṛṣṭha* sāmans the gods crossed over the heavenly world; and since they thus crossed ((यदभ्यप्लवंत) (*(yadabhyaplavaṃta)*)), this rite got the name *abhiplava*: thus a purport suggesting the context of a story is stated. Besides this kind of explanation, showing that there is another reason for its getting this name, another kind of meaning is stated:

एतद्दस्मवै तद्विद्वानाह पैंग्यः । प्लवंत इव वाभिप्लनास्तिष्ठतीव पृष्ठः (*etaddasmavai tadvidvānāha paiṃgyaḥ | plavaṃta iva vābhiplanāstiṣṭhatīva pṛṣṭhaḥ*)
इति प्लवत इव ह्ययमंगैस्तिष्ठतीवात्मनेति । (*iti plavata iva hyayamaṃgaistiṣṭhatīvātmaneti |*)

(Śa. Brā. 12-2-4-8)

Paiṅgya, the disciple of Yājñavalkya, knew the natures of the *abhiplava* and the *pṛṣṭhya*. Knowing their essence, he said: "because it has a floating nature it is *abhiplava*, and because it has a steady nature it is *pṛṣṭhya*; this is the name of these two rites (*abhiplava* and *pṛṣṭhya*)": this purport too the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa explains. In sum, whether gods or men, by performing this

### Printed p. 51

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 2.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 51.*

rite they cross the calamity: thus an explanation that suggests its importance is stated. In the present sūkta also, since in the prayer द्यावा रक्षतं पृथिवी नो अभ्वात् (*dyāvā rakṣataṃ pṛthivī no abhvāt*) there is the plea "protect us from great fear", by reciting these mantras at the time of the Abhiplava sacrifice one can cross calamity. This *abhiplava* holds a principal place from the spiritual point of view as well. It is a part of the sacrifice called गवामयनं (*gavāmayanaṃ*). The गवाम यनवु (*gavāma yanavu*) is of the nature of the year. The Puruṣa too is of the nature of the year. If *abhiplava* is a part of the Gavāmayana, which is of the nature of the year, it must also be a part of the principle of the Puruṣa, who is of the nature of the year. Describing this, the Śatapatha says पुरुषो वै संवत्सरः . . . उरोऽभिप्लवः (*puruṣo vai saṃvatsaraḥ . . . uro 'bhiplavaḥ*); if the year reflects the Puruṣa principle then *abhiplava* is his chest, and so on: its description is given (Śa. Brā 12-1-4-1). *Abhiplava* is the main means of crossing the ocean in the form of the year (the ocean in the form of a *sattra*): प्रस्येयोऽभिप्लवः (*prasyeyo 'bhiplavaḥ*) (Śa. Brā. 12-2-1-1). Āruṇeya Śvetaketu, knowing this principle of the year and telling the greatness of *abhiplava*, said:

तदाहुः । कस्मादुभयतो ज्योतिषोऽभिप्लवा भवंत्यन्यतो ज्योतिः पृष्ठि (*tadāhuḥ | kasmādubhayato jyotiṣo 'bhiplavā bhavaṃtyanyato jyotiḥ pṛṣṭhi*)
इतीमे वै लोका अभिप्लवा उभयतो ज्योतिषो वा इमे लोकाः ॥ (*itīme vai lokā abhiplavā ubhayato jyotiṣo vā ime lokāḥ ‖*)

(Śa. Brā. 12-2-1)

The *abhiplava* shines with a twofold light. These worlds (heaven, earth and atmosphere) themselves are *abhiplava*. This *abhiplava* shines with a light in the form of terrestrial fire on the side of the earth, and with a light in the form of the sun on the side of the celestial world. त आदित्याः स्वर्गं लोक- मभ्यप्लवंत (*ta ādityāḥ svargaṃ loka- mabhyaplavaṃta*): by this boat in the form of *abhiplava* the Ādityas (with the Aṅgirases) reached the celestial world; thus the nature of *abhiplava* is described in many ways. Since Heaven and Earth are themselves of the form of *abhiplava*, the description and praise of Heaven and Earth at the time of this rite is natural. Moreover, telling the identity of the Jyotiṣṭoma and the other parts of the *abhiplava* with Heaven and Earth, the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa describes thus:

ज्योतिर्गौरायुरिति स्तोमेभिर्यंत्ययं वै लोको ज्योतिरंतरिक्षं (*jyotirgaurāyuriti stomebhiryaṃtyayaṃ vai loko jyotiraṃtarikṣaṃ*)
गौरस्लोक आयुः स एवैष उत्तरस्प्रह्मः ज्योतिर्गौरायुरिति त्रीण्यहानि (*gaurasloka āyuḥ sa evaiṣa uttarasprahmaḥ jyotirgaurāyuriti trīṇyahāni*)
गौरायुर्ज्योतिरिति त्रीणि । अयं वै लोको ज्योतिरसौ लोको (*gaurāyurjyotiriti trīṇi | ayaṃ vai loko jyotirasau loko*)
ज्योतिस्ते एते ज्योतिषी उभयतः संलोकेते । तेनैतेनोभयतो (*jyotiste ete jyotiṣī ubhayataḥ saṃlokete | tenaitenobhayato*)
ज्योतिषा षळहेन यंति तद्यदेतेनोभयतो ज्योतिषा षळहेन यंत्य- (*jyotiṣā ṣaḷahena yaṃti tadyadetenobhayato jyotiṣā ṣaḷahena yaṃtya-*)
नयोरेव तल्लोकयोरुभयतः प्रतितिष्ठंतो यंत्यस्मिंश्च लोकेS- (*nayoreva tallokayorubhayataḥ pratitiṣṭhaṃto yaṃtyasmiṃśca lokeS-*)
मुष्मिंश्चोभयोः ॥ (*muṣmiṃścobhayoḥ ‖*)

(Ai. Brā. 18-1)

### Printed p. 52

*Running head: [52 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. Ā. 24. Sū. 185] (the print has "ಆ." for "ಅ.", as supplied).*

For the six-day *abhiplava*, first, among the three days Jyotiṣṭoma, Gostoma and Āyuṣṭoma, the Jyotiṣṭoma is the earth, the Gostoma the atmosphere, and the Āyuṣṭoma the heavenly world. In the second part, the three days, the order is again Gostoma, Āyuṣṭoma and Jyotiṣṭoma. The Jyoti(ṣṭoma) of the first group that is of the form of the earth is the same as the Jyoti of the second part that is of the form of the heavenly world. They perform this sacrifice in the form of *abhiplava* with its six limbs at both ends, between the two worlds in the form of Jyoti as well. (Jyotiṣṭoma, Gostoma and Āyuṣṭoma belong to the first group. Gostoma, Āyuṣṭoma and Jyotiṣṭoma are the order of the second group.) Therefore (*abhiplava* has the name *ṣaḍaha*). Lighting up Jyoti at both ends and performing this *abhiplava* in the form of *ṣaḍaha*, they help the stability of the two worlds. And,

परियद्वा एतद्देवचक्रं यदभिप्लवः षळहस्तस्य यावभितोऽग्निष्टोमौ तौ (*pariyadvā etaddevacakraṃ yadabhiplavaḥ ṣaḷahastasya yāvabhito 'gniṣṭomau tau*)
प्रधी ये चत्वारो मध्य उक्थास्तन्नभ्यं । गच्छति वै वर्तमानेन यत्र (*pradhī ye catvāro madhya ukthāstannabhyaṃ | gacchati vai vartamānena yatra*)
कामयते तत्स्वस्ति संवत्सरस्य पारमश्नुते य एवं वेद । यो वै तद्वेद (*kāmayate tatsvasti saṃvatsarasya pāramaśnute ya evaṃ veda | yo vai tadveda*)
यत्प्रथमः षळहः स वै स्वस्ति संवत्सरस्य पारमश्नुते यस्तद्वेद यद्द्वि- (*yatprathamaḥ ṣaḷahaḥ sa vai svasti saṃvatsarasya pāramaśnute yastadveda yaddvi-*)
तीयो यस्तद्वेद यत्तृतीयो यस्तद्वेद यच्चतुर्थो यस्तद्वेद (*tīyo yastadveda yattṛtīyo yastadveda yaccaturtho yastadveda*)
यत्पंचमः ॥ (*yatpaṃcamaḥ ‖*)

(Ai. Brā. 18-15)

This *abhiplava* in the form of *ṣaḍaha* is a wheel-shaped circle for the gods. The two Agniṣṭomas at the two ends are the two felloes of the wheel; the four Ukthyas in the middle are its nave. He who knows this principle crosses the far shore of the year with the help of this wheel. Likewise, if one knows the full nature of the *abhiplava* and performs it as prescribed, all his desired objects of the whole year are fulfilled: thus the greatness of *abhiplava* is praised. Moreover,

सा यथा स्तुतिरंजसायन्येवमभिप्लवः षळहः स्वर्गस्य लोकस्याथ यथा (*sā yathā stutiraṃjasāyanyevamabhiplavaḥ ṣaḷahaḥ svargasya lokasyātha yathā*)
महापथः पर्याण एवं पृष्ट्यः षळहः स्वर्गस्य लोकस्य तद्यदुभाभ्यां (*mahāpathaḥ paryāṇa evaṃ pṛṣṭyaḥ ṣaḷahaḥ svargasya lokasya tadyadubhābhyāṃ*)
यंत्युभाभ्यां वै यन्न रिष्यत्युभयोः कामयोरुपाप्तैश्च यश्चाभिप्लवे (*yaṃtyubhābhyāṃ vai yanna riṣyatyubhayoḥ kāmayorupāptaiśca yaścābhiplave*)
षळहे यश्च पृष्ट्ये । (*ṣaḷahe yaśca pṛṣṭye |*)

(Ai. Brā. 18-3 [the print has "ಇ ಬ್ರಾ."])

This *abhiplava-ṣaḍaha* is the direct road that leads to heaven. The *pṛṣṭha* is the roundabout road. Proceeding further by both these means as well, the sacrificer his desired attainment (the sentence is cut off at the end of the window)

*[The window ends here, mid-sentence; the Special Topics continue on the next page.]*

### Printed p. 53

*Running head: [Ā. ೨. Ā. ೫. Va. ೨] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 53 (the print has "ಆ." for "ಅ." twice, as supplied). The window begins inside the Special Topics of Ṛk 1, continuing the account of the Abhiplava-ṣaḍaha from p. 52.*

… can strive for. The place of the Jyotiṣṭoma, Gostoma and Āyuṣṭoma in these *ṣaḍahas* will be told later, when the occasion arises. The greatness of the same *ṣaḍaha* is praised in the Kauṣītaki Brāhmaṇa thus: "The year is for the gods an immortal wheel. In it is contained food of six kinds, beginning with सध्यादिगळे (*sadhyādigaḷe*) (as supplied). Standing on the support of this wheel they perform the sacrifice in the form of *abhiplava*. All the gods and men who perform this sacrifice, standing on the support of this wheel in the form of the year, obtain food of six kinds. Because the Jyotiṣṭoma and the others are performed twice each, he obtains these six kinds of food doubled", it says.

The Mahāvrata too is one part of the sattra called गवां अयन (*gavāṃ ayana*) (Gavām Ayana). In the sattra in the form of Gavāmayana, which is performed for a full year (360 days), the Prāyaṇīya Atirātra is the first day and the Udayanīya Atirātra the last day. The rites preceding the very last Udayanīya Atirātra are called Mahāvrata (this is of the nature of the Agniṣṭoma). Just as the Caturviṃśa is the opening rite of this great sattra, the Mahāvrata is the concluding rite. The procedure of the Mahāvrata is described in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa thus:

यद्वै चतुर्निंशं तन्महाव्रतं बृहद्दिवेनात्र होता रेतः सिंचति तददो (*yadvai caturniṃśaṃ tanmahāvrataṃ bṛhaddivenātra hotā retaḥ siṃcati tadado*)
महाव्रतीयेनाह्ना प्रजनयति संवत्सरे संवत्सरे वै रेतः सिक्तं जायते (*mahāvratīyenāhnā prajanayati saṃvatsare saṃvatsare vai retaḥ siktaṃ jāyate*)
तस्मात्समानं बृहद्दिवः . . . . . . य एवं विद्वानेतदहरुपैति । (*tasmātsamānaṃ bṛhaddivaḥ . . . . . . ya evaṃ vidvānetadaharupaiti |*)

(Ai. Brā. 17-8 [the print has "ಇ. ಬ್ರಾ."])

The importance which the Caturviṃśa has at the beginning of the Gavām Ayana belongs to the Mahāvrata at the end. With the Bṛhaddiva mantras the Hotṛ pours seed in the form of *retas* and, on the day of the Mahāvrata, assists the act of generation. Any seed becomes fruit within a year. He who knows this principle crosses the bond of the year and obtains wealth of food and wealth of offspring. One important point understood from this description is that, as in the Pravargya rite, so also in the rite called Mahāvrata the purpose of the rite is to guard the sacrificial seed for a year and afterwards to make it fruitful. This rite also shows a connected relation between the year that is passing and the year that is to come. At the time of this rite the recitation of the sāman in the form of Mahaduktha is principal. This praises the Mahāvrata-graha. First the Mahāvrata sāman is recited and then the Mahaduktha,

### Printed p. 54

*Running head: [54 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 185].*

and both tell the greatness of the Mahāvrata. In this recitation the secret principle of Agni and Prajāpati lies hidden. This Mahaduktha has five parts and reflects the five divisions of the body-principle, which is a union of Agni and Prajāpati. How: (1) the Gāyatra sāman, of the nature of the bird and of the *trivṛt* stoma, reflects the head; (2) the Rathantara sāman, of the *pañcadaśa* stoma, the right wing; (3) the Bṛhat sāman, of the *saptadaśa* stoma, the left wing; (4) the Bhadra sāman, of the *ekaviṃśa* stoma, the tail; and (5) the Rājanya sāman, of the *pañcaviṃśa* stoma, the self (the body). Explaining the greatness and the spiritual principle of the Mahāvrata, the Mahāvrata sāman and the Mahaduktha, the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa praises their greatness thus:

प्रजापतिरिमान्नोकान्यप्सत् । स एतं वयोविधमात्मानमपश्यदग्निं तं व्यधत्त (*prajāpatirimānnokānyapsat | sa etaṃ vayovidhamātmānamapaśyadagniṃ taṃ vyadhatta*)
तेनेमं लोकमाप्नोत्स द्वितीयं वयोविधमात्मानमपश्यन्महाव्रतं (*tenemaṃ lokamāpnotsa dvitīyaṃ vayovidhamātmānamapaśyanmahāvrataṃ*)
तद्व्यधत्त तेनांतरिक्षमाप्नोत्स तृतीयं वयोविधमात्मानमपश्यन्महदुक्थं (*tadvyadhatta tenāṃtarikṣamāpnotsa tṛtīyaṃ vayovidhamātmānamapaśyanmahadukthaṃ*)
तद्व्यधत्त तेन दिवमाप्नोत् । (*tadvyadhatta tena divamāpnot |*)

Prajāpati took up the purpose of obtaining all these worlds. Then he created Agni in the form of a bird (the *agnicit*). After building it he obtained this earthly world. Afterwards the second bird-form, the Mahāvrata, was revealed to him; building it he obtained the atmosphere. At last the third bird-form, the Mahaduktha, was revealed to him; after building it he obtained the heavenly world.

अयं नाव लोकैएषोऽग्निश्चितः । अंतरिक्षं महाव्रतं द्यौर्महदुक्थं तस्मा- (*ayaṃ nāva lokaieṣo 'gniścitaḥ | aṃtarikṣaṃ mahāvrataṃ dyaurmahadukthaṃ tasmā-*)
देतानि सर्वाणि सहोपेयादग्निं महाव्रतं महदुक्थग्ं सह हीमे (*detāni sarvāṇi sahopeyādagniṃ mahāvrataṃ mahadukthagṃ saha hīme*)
लोका असृज्यंत इत्यधिदेवतं । (*lokā asṛjyaṃta ityadhidevataṃ |*)

This *agnicit* built by Prajāpati is the earthly world; the Mahāvrata is the atmosphere; the Mahaduktha is the heavenly world. Since all these worlds were created together by Prajāpati, the natures of the *agnicit*, the Mahāvrata and the Mahaduktha must be known together. Among them, since the earth was created first, the *agnicit*, whose deity is the earth, was built first. This was the description explaining the divine nature. In the same way, explaining the principle of the body,

अथाध्यात्मं । मन एवाग्निः प्राणो महाव्रतं वाङ्महदुक्कं . . . . . (*athādhyātmaṃ | mana evāgniḥ prāṇo mahāvrataṃ vāṅmahadukkaṃ . . . . .*)
आत्मैवाग्निः । प्राणो महाव्रतं वाङ्महदुक्कं . . . . . . . . (*ātmaivāgniḥ | prāṇo mahāvrataṃ vāṅmahadukkaṃ . . . . . . . .*)
शिर एवाग्निः । प्राणो महाव्रतमात्मा महदुक्कं . . . . . . . . (*śira evāgniḥ | prāṇo mahāvratamātmā mahadukkaṃ . . . . . . . .*)

(Śa. Brā. 10-1-2-1 to 9 [the print has "ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ. ೧೦-೧-೨-೧ ರಿಂದ ೯"])

### Printed p. 55

*Running head: [Ā. ೨ Ā. ೫. Va. ೨.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 55 (as supplied).*

in explaining the spiritual principle it is said that the mind is the *agnicit*, the breath (*prāṇa*) is the Mahāvrata and speech (*vāk*) is the Mahaduktha; and in explaining the principle of the body, that the self is the *agnicit*, the breath is the Mahāvrata and speech is the Mahaduktha. And telling the derivation of the word Mahāvrata,

. . . . . यजुष्टः प्राण एव महांस्तस्यान्नमेव व्रतं (*. . . . . yajuṣṭaḥ prāṇa eva mahāṃstasyānnameva vrataṃ*)
तन्महाव्रतग्० सामतः प्राण उ एवोक् तस्यान्नमेव थं तदुक्थं . (*tanmahāvratag0 sāmataḥ prāṇa u evok tasyānnameva thaṃ tadukthaṃ .*)

(Śr. Brā. 10-4-1-23 [as printed "ಶ್ರ. ಬ್ರಾ."])

Agni, the lord of the sacrifice, is *mahān* ("great"); his food is the *vrata*; therefore "Mahāvrata". Breath is *uk*, and its food is *tha*; therefore "uktha": thus the derivation from the spiritual side is given. And the greatness of the Mahāvrata (graha) is praised thus:

अधातो महाव्रतीयस्यैव । प्रजापतेर्ह वै प्रजाः सस्यजानस्य पर्वाणि वि (*adhāto mahāvratīyasyaiva | prajāpaterha vai prajāḥ sasyajānasya parvāṇi vi*)
सस्रग्ं सुः स विस्रस्तैःपर्वभिर्नशशाक संहातुं ततो देवा अर्चंतः (*sasragṃ suḥ sa visrastaiḥparvabhirnaśaśāka saṃhātuṃ tato devā arcaṃtaḥ*)
श्राम्यंतश्चेरुस्त एतं महाव्रतीयं ददृशुस्तमस्मा अगृह्णंस्तेनास्य (*śrāmyaṃtaścerusta etaṃ mahāvratīyaṃ dadṛśustamasmā agṛhṇaṃstenāsya*)
पर्वाणि समदधुः । (*parvāṇi samadadhuḥ |*)

After Prajāpati had created all the creatures, all the joints of his body became loosened by that exertion; therefore even rising up became an effort for him. Of all the gods

स सग्ंहितैः पर्वभिः।इदमन्नाद्यमभ्युत्तेस्थौ यदिदं प्रजापतेरन्नाद्यं यद्वै (*sa sagṃhitaiḥ parvabhiḥ|idamannādyamabhyuttesthau yadidaṃ prajāpaterannādyaṃ yadvai*)
मनुष्याणामशनं तद्देवानां व्रतं महद्वा इदं व्रतमभूद्येनायग्ं (*manuṣyāṇāmaśanaṃ taddevānāṃ vrataṃ mahadvā idaṃ vratamabhūdyenāyagṃ*)
समहास्तेति तस्मान्महाव्रतीयो नाम । (*samahāsteti tasmānmahāvratīyo nāma |*)

(Ś. Brā. 4-6-4-1 to 2 [as printed "ಶ. ಬ್ರಾ."])

(the supplied text breaks the Kannada sentence off at "ದೇವತೆಗಳೆಲ್ಲರ", and the Sanskrit continues in the next line.) When the joints had been put right, Prajāpati received this food in the form of the Mahāvratīya graha. What is food for Prajāpati and for the creatures created by him is food in the form of *vrata* for the gods also. Because it has the greatness of putting right the bodily distress even of Prajāpati and making him vigorous, the name Mahāvrata became famous for it: so its praise says. What the characteristic of such a great Mahāvrata is, is told very extensively in the Aitareya Āraṇyaka. As it is not possible to give the explanation fully here, it will be told briefly. The special points will be told later.

### Printed p. 56

*Running head: [56 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24 Sū. 185]. The window begins inside the Special Topics of Ṛk 1, continuing the account of the Mahāvrata from p. 55.*

अथ महाव्रतं । इंद्रो वै वृत्रं हत्वा महानभवद्यन्महानभवत्तन्महाव्रत- (*atha mahāvrataṃ | iṃdro vai vṛtraṃ hatvā mahānabhavadyanmahānabhavattanmahāvrata-*)
मभवत्तन्महाव्रतस्य महाव्रतत्वं । (*mabhavattanmahāvratasya mahāvratatvaṃ |*)

(Aitareya Āraṇyaka 1-1 [as printed "ಐತರೇಯ ಆರಣ್ಯಕಂ"])

Indra killed Vṛtra and manifested his greatness. Afterwards the Mahāvrata, which celebrates that greatness, began; therefore the rite called Mahāvrata also got this name. The story-context that, by this rite in the form of the slaying of the asura Vṛtra, the name Mahendra too became famous for Indra is explained by sentences such as “ इंद्रो वै वृत्रं हत्वा . . . . यन्महानिंद्रोऽभवत्तन्म- हेंद्रस्यमहेंद्रत्वं ” (*“ iṃdro vai vṛtraṃ hatvā . . . . yanmahāniṃdro 'bhavattanma- heṃdrasyamaheṃdratvaṃ ”*) in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa also (12-10) and in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā (6-5-5-3). Although the Aitareya Āraṇyaka by the sentence “ तन्महाव्रतस्य महाव्रतत्वं ” (*“ tanmahāvratasya mahāvratatvaṃ ”*) states the context that celebrates Indra's greatness, Sāyaṇa, in giving his commentary in this context, has given the derivation stated in the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa. As in the Śatapatha sentences cited above the context of the offering of the Mahāvrata for relieving Prajāpati's exhaustion is described, the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa also tells the same matter. The order of that account is as follows, and is somewhat different from the Śatapatha account:

प्रजापतिः प्रजाः सृष्ट्वा वृत्तोऽशयत् । तं देवा भूतानाग्ं रसं तेजः (*prajāpatiḥ prajāḥ sṛṣṭvā vṛtto 'śayat | taṃ devā bhūtānāgṃ rasaṃ tejaḥ*)
संभृत्य । तेनैनमभिषज्यन् । महानववर्तीति । तन्महाव्रतस्य महाव्रतत्त्वं । (*saṃbhṛtya | tenainamabhiṣajyan | mahānavavartīti | tanmahāvratasya mahāvratattvaṃ |*)
महद्व्रतमिति । तन्महाव्रतस्य महाव्रतत्वं । महतो व्रतमिति । तन्महाव्रतस्य (*mahadvratamiti | tanmahāvratasya mahāvratatvaṃ | mahato vratamiti | tanmahāvratasya*)
महाव्रतत्वं ॥ (*mahāvratatvaṃ ‖*)

(Tai. Brā. 1-2-6-1)

In this account it is told that Prajāpati, calmed after the work of creation, fell down exhausted; the gods acquired the brilliance that is the essence of all creatures and by its help relieved his exhaustion; therefore Prajāpati became great. This brilliance, which is the essence, is the oblation offered at the time of the Mahāvrata; thus it is explained why the Mahāvrata became famous under this name.

महानवर्तिष्ट अनेन कर्मणा इति व्युत्पत्ता तस्य कर्मणः महाव्रतमिति नाम (*mahānavartiṣṭa anena karmaṇā iti vyutpattā tasya karmaṇaḥ mahāvratamiti nāma*)
संपन्नं । यद्वा कर्मणोऽस्य महाफलहेतुत्वेन महत्वान् महच्च (*saṃpannaṃ | yadvā karmaṇo 'sya mahāphalahetutvena mahatvān mahacca*)
तद्व्रतमिति तद्व्युत्पत्तिः । अथवा महतः प्रजापतेरौषधरूपत्वान्महतो (*tadvratamiti tadvyutpattiḥ | athavā mahataḥ prajāpaterauṣadharūpatvānmahato*)
व्रतमिति व्युत्पत्तिः ॥ (*vratamiti vyutpattiḥ ‖*)

### Printed p. 57

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 2. Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 57.*

Sāyaṇa says that the rite has the name Mahāvrata either because the purport is that by the rite of offering oblations Prajāpati became great, or because it is a rite that has the capacity to give great fruit and fitness of nature, or because it is a medicine for Prajāpati, who became great. At the time of such a Mahāvrata the recitation of the present sūkta is prescribed. This itself

कतरा पूर्वा कतरापरायोरिति द्यावापृथिवीयं समानोदर्कं समानो- (*katarā pūrvā katarāparāyoriti dyāvāpṛthivīyaṃ samānodarkaṃ samāno-*)
दर्कं ना एतदहरेतस्याह्नो रूपं ॥ (*darkaṃ nā etadaharetasyāhno rūpaṃ ‖*)

(Ai. Ā. 1-5-3 [as printed "ಇ. ಆ."])

is told in these sentences. The explanation of the present ṛk by the Nirukta authors is as follows:

कतरा पूर्वा कतरापरा । एनयोः कथं जाते कवयः क एने (*katarā pūrvā katarāparā | enayoḥ kathaṃ jāte kavayaḥ ka ene*)
विजानाति । सर्वमात्मना बिभृतो यद्धैनयोः कर्म विवर्तेते (*vijānāti | sarvamātmanā bibhṛto yaddhainayoḥ karma vivartete*)
चैनयोरहनी अहोरात्रे चक्रियेव चक्रयुक्ते इवेति द्यावा- (*cainayorahanī ahorātre cakriyeva cakrayukte iveti dyāvā-*)
पृथिव्योर्महिमानमाचष्ट आचष्टे (*pṛthivyormahimānamācaṣṭa ācaṣṭe*)

(Ni. 2-22 [the print has Telugu letters and numerals here; see the Kannada text])

This says: Which is the earlier of these two deities? What is the order of their origin? O seers, which of you knows this secret? These two are the support of all the beings of the universe. Like the two wheels of a chariot, day and night revolve continuously around them. Durgācārya, explaining “ कतरा पूर्वा कतरा अपरा ” (*“ katarā pūrvā katarā aparā ”*), says “ किं पौर्वापर्येण, उत युगपत् भवेतां ” (*“ kiṃ paurvāparyeṇa, uta yugapat bhavetāṃ ”*): the question is whether one was born after the other or both were born together. In regard to the origin of these deities sometimes wonder, sometimes an uncertain conjecture, is natural.

किं स्विध्वनं क उ स वृक्ष आस यतो द्यावापृथिवी निष्ठ तक्षुः । (*kiṃ svidhvanaṃ ka u sa vṛkṣa āsa yato dyāvāpṛthivī niṣṭha takṣuḥ |*)
संतस्थाने अजरे इत ऊती अहानि पूर्विरुषसो जरंत ॥ (*saṃtasthāne ajare ita ūtī ahāni pūrviruṣaso jaraṃta ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 10-31-7)

The seer expresses his wonder, mixed with praise: from what forest and from what trees were these Heaven and Earth fashioned, which stand indestructible, unbroken and steady while countless days and dawns roll by? The curiosity expressed in the ṛk “ क्विं स्विध्वनं ” (*“ kviṃ svidhvanaṃ ”*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-31-7) is indicated in many other places too. In some places the fitting answer is also stated. For example,

### Printed p. 58

*Running head: [58 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 185].*

किंस्विदासीदधिष्ठानमारंभणं कथमत्सित्कथासीत् । (*kiṃsvidāsīdadhiṣṭhānamāraṃbhaṇaṃ kathamatsitkathāsīt |*)
यतो भूमिं जनयन् विश्वकर्मा वि द्यामौर्णेन्महिना विश्वचक्षाः ॥ (*yato bhūmiṃ janayan viśvakarmā vi dyāmaurṇenmahinā viśvacakṣāḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-81-2)

it is asked what was the basis for the making of these for Viśvakarman, who has all-seeing eyes, who created the earth and spread out the atmosphere. As the answer to this,

किग्ग् स्विध्वनं क उ स वृक्ष आसीत् । यतो द्यावापृथिवी निष्कृतक्षुः । मनीषिणो (*kigg svidhvanaṃ ka u sa vṛkṣa āsīt | yato dyāvāpṛthivī niṣkṛtakṣuḥ | manīṣiṇo*)
मनसा पृच्छतेदु तत् । यदध्यतिष्ठद्भुवनानि धारयन् । ब्रह्म वनं ब्रह्म (*manasā pṛcchatedu tat | yadadhyatiṣṭhadbhuvanāni dhārayan | brahma vanaṃ brahma*)
स वृक्ष आसीत् । यतो द्यावापृथिवी निष्कृतक्षुः । मनीषिणो मनसा विब्र- (*sa vṛkṣa āsīt | yato dyāvāpṛthivī niṣkṛtakṣuḥ | manīṣiṇo manasā vibra-*)
वीमि वः । ब्रह्माध्यतिष्ठद्भुवनानि धारयन् ॥ (*vīmi vaḥ | brahmādhyatiṣṭhadbhuvanāni dhārayan ‖*)

(Śai. Brā. 2-8-9-6-7 [as printed "ಶೈ. ಬ್ರಾ."])

the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa says that for the creation of these Heaven and Earth Brahman (the imperishable thing called Brahman) is the forest, and Brahman is the tree; from it comes their creation.

कवयः (*kavayaḥ*) — क्रांतदर्शन: (*krāṃtadarśana:*) अतींद्रियज्ञाः (*atīṃdriyajñāḥ*): in this way he has given the sense of the word *kavi*. Having cited the sentence of the Nirukta authors मेधावी कविः क्रांतदर्शनो भवति कवतेर्वा ” (*medhāvī kaviḥ krāṃtadarśano bhavati kavatervā ”*) (Ni. 12-13), the derivation of the word *kavi* was told earlier. What the nature and the power of a *kavi* are, and to tell what nature and powers the word *kavi* is used in the Ṛksaṃhitā, was also told earlier subtly. In the present ṛk it is asked: "who has known the secret of Heaven and Earth, which reflect the principle of the universe?" and with “ कवयः, को विवेद ” (*“ kavayaḥ, ko viveda ”*) it is asked: "O seers who know what is beyond the senses, who has fully known this principle?" That is, this secret is not to be understood by a merely ordinary and physical view. To pierce and understand this secret there must be an extraordinary power of fitness. He who has such an extraordinary power of vision is called a *kavi*.

समानं नीळं वृषणो वसानाः सं जग्मिरे महिषा अर्वतीभिः । (*samānaṃ nīḷaṃ vṛṣaṇo vasānāḥ saṃ jagmire mahiṣā arvatībhiḥ |*)
यतस्य पदं कवयो नि पांति गुहा नामानि दधिरे पराणि । (*yatasya padaṃ kavayo ni pāṃti guhā nāmāni dadhire parāṇi |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಪು. ಸಂ.] 10-5-2)

In ṛks like this the true nature of the *kavi* is described. The *kavi*s, having entered the path of *ṛta*, hold in the most hidden heart the names of the highest secret; and they make it manifest and make it known to the world: so it is described; that is, hidden and concealed, of the world… (the sentence is cut off at the end of the window).

*[The window ends here, mid-sentence.]*

### Printed p. 59

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 2.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 59. The window begins inside the Special Topics of Ṛk 1, continuing the note on *kavayaḥ* from p. 58.*

… the word is used in the sense of those who have the capacity, by knowing through their inner vision the aspects that do not appear to the eye, to make the world's sight also understand a little. In the present ṛk too, addressing seers who have obtained such supra-sensory knowledge, it asks: "What is the secret principle of Heaven and Earth?"

विश्वं त्मना बिभृतः (*viśvaṃ tmanā bibhṛtaḥ*) — it is praised that the deity of the heavenly world and the earth goddess, protectors of the universe, hold all the worlds in themselves and themselves bear them all.

नमो द्यावापृथिवीभ्यामिति तदाभ्यां द्यावापृथिवीभ्यां निह्नुवते ययोरिदं (*namo dyāvāpṛthivībhyāmiti tadābhyāṃ dyāvāpṛthivībhyāṃ nihnuvate yayoridaṃ*)
सर्वमधि । (*sarvamadhi |*)

(Śa. Brā. 3-4-3-21)

In many places like this it is stated that the existence of all the worlds, their bearing and all protective activity depend on Heaven and Earth. The same idea was explained earlier, in telling the special topics of the ṛk

उरुव्यचसा महिनी असश्चता पिता माता च भुवनानि रक्षतः । (*uruvyacasā mahinī asaścatā pitā mātā ca bhuvanāni rakṣataḥ |*)
सुधृष्टमे वपुष्ये ३ न रोदसी पिता यत्सीमभि रूपैरवासयत् ॥ (*sudhṛṣṭame vapuṣye 3 na rodasī pitā yatsīmabhi rūpairavāsayat ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ] 1-160-2 [the print has "೩" after "ವಪುಷ್ಯೇ" in the verse, as supplied; and "ಹಕ್ಕಿನ" for "ಋಕ್ಕಿನ" in the following sentence])

*[As supplied, the label line of the grammar page reads व्याकत इप्रक्रिया]* (*vyākata iprakriyā]**); standard label inserted.]*

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page, noted briefly)

कतरा (*katarā*) (किम् (*kim*), "which of two"): by किंयत्तदोः निर्धारणेद्वयोः (*kiṃyattadoḥ nirdhāraṇedvayoḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 5-3-42) the suffix *ḍatarac* when the sense of selection appears; because it has the mark *ḍ*, elision of the *ṭi* part; by चितः (*citaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-163) the last-syllable acute; because it ends in *a*, feminine *ṭāp*. अयोः (*ayoḥ*): when the form is अनयोः (*anayoḥ*) ("of these two"), elision of the *n* in the Vedic manner. कथा (*kathā*): by थाहेत् च छंदसि (*thāhet ca chaṃdasi*) (Pā. Sū. 5-3-326 [as printed]) the suffix *thā* after the word *kim*; because it has the case-ending name, the substitute *ka* by the sūtra किमः कः (*kimaḥ kaḥ*); the accent of the suffix, last-syllable acute. कवयः (*kavayaḥ*) (root कु (*ku*), "to sound"): the suffix *i*; by सार्वधातुकार्थ- धातुकयोः ऎंबुदरिंद (*sārvadhātukārtha- dhātukayoḥ èṃbudariṃda*) the *u* is replaced; vocative plural; the vocative *nighāta* accent. त्मना (*tmanā*): by मंत्रेष्वाज्यादेरात्मनः (*maṃtreṣvājyāderātmanaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-4-141) the elision of the initial *ā*. वर्तते (*vartate*) (root वृतु वर्तने. (*vṛtu vartane.*)): present tense, third person dual; the finite-verb *nighāta* accent arises. 

### Printed p. 60

*Running head: [60 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 1.85] (as supplied).*

चक्रियाइव (*cakriyāiva*) — by interchange in the sense of possession; by मतौचॆ (*mataucè*) (Pā. Sū. 4-4-136) the suffix *gha*; by the sūtra आयनेयी—सूत्रदिंद (*āyaneyī—sūtradiṃda*) the substitute *iya*; when a dual ending follows, by *sulug* of the case endings the substitute *ā*; the accent of the suffix, first-syllable acute.

#### Ṛk 2

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> भूरिं॒ द्वे अच॑रंती॒ चरं॑तं प॒द्वंतं॒ गर्भ॑म॒पदी॑ दधाते ।
> नित्यं॒ न सू॒नुं पि॒त्रोरु॒पस्थे॒ द्यावा॒ रक्ष॑तं पृथिवी नो॒ अभ्वा॑त् ॥ २ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> भूरिं । द्वे इति । आचरंती इति । चरंतं । पत्र्वंतं । गर्भं । अपदी इति । दधाते इति ।
> नित्यं । न । सूनुं । पित्रोः । उपस्थे । द्यावा । रक्षतं । पृथिवी इति । नः । अभ्वात् ॥२॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> अचरंती अविचले द्वे एवैते द्यावापृथिव्यौ भूरिं बहुतरं चरंतं पद्वंतं
> पादयुक्तं गर्भं गर्भवदाश्रितं कृत्स्नं प्राणिजातमपदी स्वयं पादरहिते दधाते
> धारयतः । अनयोर्मध्यॆ खलु सर्वं जगत्क्षेमेण वर्तते । धारणे दृष्टांतः ।
> पित्रोर्मातापित्रोरुपस्थ उत्संगे वर्तमानं नित्यं ध्रुवमात्मजं सूनुं न पुत्र-
> मिव यथा स्नेहेन वर्धयंत् धारयंत् मातापितरौ तद्वत् । अथ प्रत्यक्षेणाह ।
> हे द्यावा पृथिवी द्यावापृथिव्यौ । इतरेतरापेक्षया द्वित्वमुभयोः । नोऽस्मान-
> भ्वान्महतो भयहेतोः पापाद्रक्षतं पालयतं । यद्वा । अभ्वादभ्वं महत् ।
> आत्यर्थमित्यर्थः । अभ्येति महन्नाम ॥ द्वंद्वे कृतद्यावादेशस्य द्यावापृथिवीशब्दस्य
> मध्यॆ रक्षतमिति पदप्रयोगश्चांदसः । आमंत्रितस्याविद्यमानत्वेन निघाताभावः ॥

*Gist:* These two immovable ones, Heaven and Earth, themselves footless, hold the many footed and moving beings like an embryo, as parents hold a son on the lap; O Heaven and Earth, protect us from great, fear-causing sin.

### Printed p. 61

*Running head: [Ā ೨ Ā. ೫. Va. ೨] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 61 (as supplied).*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

पित्रोः (*pitroḥ*) — of the parents. उपस्थे (*upasthe*) — growing on the lap. नित्यं सूनुं न (*nityaṃ sūnuṃ na*) — as parents lovingly bear and cherish an own son. अचरंती (*acaraṃtī*) — themselves motionless अपदी (*apadī*) — and footless; द्वे (*dve*) — Heaven and Earth, both; भूरिं (*bhūriṃ*) — of many kinds पद्वंतं (*padvaṃtaṃ*) — possessing feet चरंतं (*caraṃtaṃ*) — moving गर्भं (*garbhaṃ*) — all living beings resting on these two as an embryo rests on its mother दधाते (*dadhāte*) — they hold. द्यावापृथिवी (*dyāvāpṛthivī*) — O Heaven and Earth नः (*naḥ*) — us अभ्वात् (*abhvāt*) — from sin that is great and a cause of fear रक्षतं (*rakṣataṃ*) — protect.

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads भानुवार (*bhānuvāra*); standard label inserted.]*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

Just as parents lovingly bear and cherish on their lap their own son who grows up there, so Heaven and Earth, themselves motionless and footless, hold all living beings of many kinds, possessing feet and moving, who rest on them as an embryo rests on its mother. O Heaven and Earth, protect us from sin that is great and a cause of fear.

*[The label line of the printed English reads, as supplied, "English Trnslation".]*

**Printed English translation**

Footless and motionless, they sustain numerous moving and footed races, as a son is even (nursed) on the lap of his parents: defend us, Heaven and Earth, from great (danger).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this ṛk the relation of Heaven and Earth to the remaining living beings, and the difference between the two groups (the supplied text has the garbled words "ಅವರ ಡಕ್ಕೂ" here, as supplied), is told. By the two epithets अचरंती (*acaraṃtī*) अपदी (*apadī*) the steadiness of Heaven and Earth is told, and by the epithets पद्वंतं (*padvaṃtaṃ*) चरंतं (*caraṃtaṃ*) the activity of going and so on of all living beings, or likewise their restlessness. Although by the words "of the parents", "on the lap", "ever", "son" a worldly simile common to all is shown, since the word गर्भं (*garbhaṃ*) that follows shows that all living beings are of the nature of an embryo, the word पित्रोः (*pitroḥ*) can also be taken to mean Heaven and Earth in the form of parents. As in the world parents hold their own son on their lap and cherish him, so Heaven and Earth, the parents of the universe, hold and cherish the living beings who are their own embryo-like offspring: this is the meaning.

### Printed p. 62

*Running head: [62 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 185]. The window begins inside the Special Topics of Ṛk 2, continuing p. 61.*

… if the word पित्रोः (*pitroḥ*) is taken to mean Heaven and Earth in the form of parents, the question then arises which of them is father and which mother. The Nirukta authors have explained: पितुः — पितृस्थानीयायाः पृथिव्याः मातुः — मातांतरिक्षं निर्मियंतेऽस्मिन् भूतानि (*pituḥ — pitṛsthānīyāyāḥ pṛthivyāḥ mātuḥ — mātāṃtarikṣaṃ nirmiyaṃte 'smin bhūtāni*) (Ni. 2-8). From the root *pā*, "to protect", the earth, which protects all beings, is called "father"; and since all beings are created in the atmosphere, the atmosphere is called "mother": so it is understood from the explanation of the Nirukta authors. This explanation is valid in a way from the point of view of the derivation of the word, but it cannot agree with the traditional sense. Ordinarily the sentences that designate the atmosphere as father and the earth as mother are the well-known ones:

तन्नो वातो मयोभु वातु भेषजं तन्माता पृथिवी तत् पिता दौर्य्य । (*tanno vāto mayobhu vātu bheṣajaṃ tanmātā pṛthivī tat pitā dauryya |*)
तद्गावाणः सोमसुतो मयोभुवस्तदश्विना शृणुतं धिष्ण्या युवं ॥ (*tadgāvāṇaḥ somasuto mayobhuvastadaśvinā śṛṇutaṃ dhiṣṇyā yuvaṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 1-89-4)

In many such contexts the designation of the atmosphere as father and of the earth as mother was told in detail earlier; and in sentences such as “ मधुद्यौरस्तु नः पिता ” (*“ madhudyaurastu naḥ pitā ”*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-90-7) the epithet "father" is established for the deity of the heavenly world. In the present ṛk they may be taken together as "parents": the meaning is that living beings grow in the lap (on the thighs) of such parents.

उपस्थे (*upasthe*) — the word उपस्थशब्दवन्नु (*upasthaśabdavannu*) is used in different senses. In many places Sāyaṇa has explained it as उपस्थः यज्ञः, आहवनीयो ना अंतरिक्षं वा (*upasthaḥ yajñaḥ, āhavanīyo nā aṃtarikṣaṃ vā*) and so on. In the present ṛk it means "in the lap of the parents". Ordinarily the words पित्रोः उपस्थे (*pitroḥ upasthe*) are used to indicate the relation of Agni to Heaven and Earth. For example,

त्रिमूर्धानं सप्तरश्मिं गृणीषेऽनूनमग्निं पित्तोरुपस्थे । (*trimūrdhānaṃ saptaraśmiṃ gṛṇīṣe 'nūnamagniṃ pittorupasthe |*)
निषत्तमस्य चरतो ध्रुवस्य विश्वा दिवो रोचनापप्रिवांसं ॥ (*niṣattamasya carato dhruvasya viśvā divo rocanāpaprivāṃsaṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 1-146-1)

here "Agni seated in the lap of his parents" means Agni sitting on the thighs of his parents, Heaven and Earth. But in the present ṛk only the relation of all the beings to Heaven and Earth is told. In नित्यं सूनुं ऎन्नुवाग (*nityaṃ sūnuṃ ènnuvāga*) the word नित्यं (*nityaṃ*) is given the sense "steady"; in the present context the steadiness of Heaven and Earth and the unsteadiness of living beings are described, telling the difference between them. Therefore

### Printed p. 63

*Running head: [Ā. ೨. Ā ೫. Va. ೨.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 63 (as supplied).*

here, for the word नित्यं (*nityaṃ*), the sense "their own" is more fitting than the sense "steady". The use of the word in the sense "natural" occurs in many places; in the present context it can be taken to mean "own son". In the present ṛk not only is the relation of Heaven and Earth as mother and father stated, but it is also prayed that the world be protected from great fear. The refrain महद्भयदिंद रक्षिसबेकॆंदु प्रार्थिसलागिदॆ. “ द्यावा रक्षतं पृथिवी (*mahadbhayadiṃda rakṣisabekèṃdu prārthisalāgidè. “ dyāvā rakṣataṃ pṛthivī*) is found in the ṛks 2-8 of the sūkta.

अभ्वात् (*abhvāt*) — the word अभ्वशब्दवु (*abhvaśabdavu*) is read among the names of water (Ni. ni. 24 [as printed]) and among the names of "great" (Ni. 3-23). But because the word is used in other senses than these two, Sāyaṇa too has given the sense according to the context; having understood this, the correct sense in the present ṛk can be settled.

नहि ते क्षत्रं न सहो न मन्युं वयश्चनामी पतयंत आपुः । (*nahi te kṣatraṃ na saho na manyuṃ vayaścanāmī patayaṃta āpuḥ |*)
नेमा आपो अनिमिषं चरंतीर्न ये वातस्य प्रमिनंत्यभ्वं ॥ (*nemā āpo animiṣaṃ caraṃtīrna ye vātasya praminaṃtyabhvaṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಪು. ಸಂ.] 1-25-6)

In this ṛk there is praise of Varuṇa. Here, by वातस्य ये गतिविशेषाः ते त्वदीयं अभ्वं वेगं न हिंसंति (*vātasya ye gativiśeṣāḥ te tvadīyaṃ abhvaṃ vegaṃ na hiṃsaṃti*) ("the speed of the wind also cannot hinder Varuṇa's speed"), he has taken the word अभ्वं (*abhvaṃ*) to mean "speed". And,

युष्मेषितो मरुतो मर्त्येषित आ यो नो अभ्व ईषते । (*yuṣmeṣito maruto martyeṣita ā yo no abhva īṣate |*)
वि तं युयोत शवसा व्योजसा वि युष्माकाभिरूतिभिः ॥ (*vi taṃ yuyota śavasā vyojasā vi yuṣmākābhirūtibhiḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ ಸಂ.] 1-39-8)

here the Maruts are praised; explaining यः कश्चिदभ्वः शत्रुः नः प्राप्नोति तं शवसा वि यः योत (*yaḥ kaścidabhvaḥ śatruḥ naḥ prāpnoti taṃ śavasā vi yaḥ yota*), he has taken the word *abhva* to mean "enemy".

असूत पृश्निर्महते रणाय त्वेषमयासां मरुतामनीकं । (*asūta pṛśnirmahate raṇāya tveṣamayāsāṃ marutāmanīkaṃ |*)
ते सप्सरासोऽजनयंताभ्वमादित्स्वधामिषिरां पर्यपश्यन् ॥ (*te sapsarāso 'janayaṃtābhvamāditsvadhāmiṣirāṃ paryapaśyan ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-168-9)

In this ṛk, by अभ्वं मेघं जनयंति । (*abhvaṃ meghaṃ janayaṃti |*), the word *abhva* is taken to mean "cloud"; and

अ युन्ने अभ्वं वनदः पनंतोशिग्भ्यो नामिमीत वर्णं। (*a yunne abhvaṃ vanadaḥ panaṃtośigbhyo nāmimīta varṇaṃ|*)
स चित्रेण चिकिते रंसु भासा जजुर्वा यो मुहुरा युवा भूत् ॥ (*sa citreṇa cikite raṃsu bhāsā jajurvā yo muhurā yuvā bhūt ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 2-4-5)

### Printed p. 64

*Running head: [64 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 185].*

in this place the word *abhva* praises the greatness of Agni; here it means "greatness". In the same way,

अर्हन्बिभर्षि सायकानि धन्वार्हन्निष्कं यजतं विश्वरूपं । (*arhanbibharṣi sāyakāni dhanvārhanniṣkaṃ yajataṃ viśvarūpaṃ |*)
आर्हन्निदं दयसे विश्वमभ्वं न वा ओजियो रुद्र त्वदस्ति ॥ (*ārhannidaṃ dayase viśvamabhvaṃ na vā ojiyo rudra tvadasti ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 2-33-10)

in this ṛk Sāyaṇa has taken, because it is read among the names of "great", विश्वं अभ्वं । महन्नामैतत् । अतिविस्तृतं जगत् । (*viśvaṃ abhvaṃ | mahannāmaitat | ativistṛtaṃ jagat |*) to mean "the very extended world". That is, in the 2-4-5 cited above the word indicates the spiritual greatness of Agni; here, in the ṛk 2-33-10, it indicates the physical extension of the world. To know what the original sense of the word *abhva* is, and how these derived senses become possible on that original sense, one has to notice the word *abhva* used in the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa sentence; then the original sense becomes known.

अथ ब्रह्मैव परार्धमगच्छत् । तत्परार्धं गत्वैक्षत कथं निमान्लोकान्प्र- (*atha brahmaiva parārdhamagacchat | tatparārdhaṃ gatvaikṣata kathaṃ nimānlokānpra-*)
त्यवेयामिति तद्वाभ्यामेव प्रत्यवैद्रूपेण चैव नाम्ना च स यस्य कस्य (*tyaveyāmiti tadvābhyāmeva pratyavaidrūpeṇa caiva nāmnā ca sa yasya kasya*)
च नामास्ति तन्नाम यस्कोऽपि नाम नास्ति यद्वेद रूपेणेदग्० रूपमिति (*ca nāmāsti tannāma yasko 'pi nāma nāsti yadveda rūpeṇedag0 rūpamiti*)
तद्रूपमेतावद्वा इदं यावद्रूपं चैव नाम च । ते हैते ब्रह्मणो (*tadrūpametāvadvā idaṃ yāvadrūpaṃ caiva nāma ca | te haite brahmaṇo*)
महती अभ्ये । स यो हैते ब्रह्मणो महती अभ्ये वेद महद्धैवाभ्वं (*mahatī abhye | sa yo haite brahmaṇo mahatī abhye veda mahaddhaivābhvaṃ*)
भवति ॥ (*bhavati ‖*)

(Śa. Brā. 11-2-3-3)

In these sentences it is said that name and form are the two great *abhvas* of Brahman. The *abhva* of Brahman is of two kinds, concrete and abstract (printed in English letters); therefore it means that the two kinds of great powers of Brahman, the concrete power and the abstract power, pervade the universe. From these original senses the remaining purports arise. In this way the pervasive sense, the principle of the universe in concrete and abstract form,

उदू अया उपवक्तेव बाहू हिरण्यया सविता सुप्रतीका । (*udū ayā upavakteva bāhū hiraṇyayā savitā supratīkā |*)
दिवो रोहांस्यरुहत्पृथिव्या अरीरमत्पतयत्कच्चिदभ्वं ॥ (*divo rohāṃsyaruhatpṛthivyā arīramatpatayatkaccidabhvaṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ ಸಂ.] 6-21-5)

is contained in the word *abhva* in this ṛk. Explaining the sense of this word, Sāyaṇa has given its pervasive purport as “ अभ्वं महत् सर्वं वस्तुजातं ” (*“ abhvaṃ mahat sarvaṃ vastujātaṃ ”*). Some (the sentence is cut off at the end of the window).

### Printed p. 65

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 2] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 65. The window begins inside the Special Topics of Ṛk 2, continuing the note on *abhva* from p. 64.*

… in some places a pervasive good power and in others an evil power is indicated. In the Ṛksaṃhitā the word is used in both senses.

प्र ये वसुभ्य ईवदा नमो दुर्ये मित्रे वरुणे सूक्तवाचः । (*pra ye vasubhya īvadā namo durye mitre varuṇe sūktavācaḥ |*)
अवैत्वभ्वं कृणुता वरीयो दिवस्पृथिव्योरवणा मदेम ॥ (*avaitvabhvaṃ kṛṇutā varīyo divaspṛthivyoravaṇā madema ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಪು. ಸಂ.] 5-49-5)

here, with अभ्यं महद्धनं तेजो ना । (*abhyaṃ mahaddhanaṃ tejo nā |*), the sense is great power of splendour;

द्यावो न यस्य पनयंत्यभ्वं भासांसि वस्ते सूर्यो न शुक्रः । (*dyāvo na yasya panayaṃtyabhvaṃ bhāsāṃsi vaste sūryo na śukraḥ |*)
वि य इनोत्यजरः पावकोऽश्नस्य चिच्छिश्नथत्पूर्वाणि ॥ (*vi ya inotyajaraḥ pāvako 'śnasya cicchiśnathatpūrvāṇi ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಪು ಸಂ,] 6-4-3)

here, with अभ्यं महत्कर्म । (*abhyaṃ mahatkarma |*), it is understood as great power of action. In both these contexts the word is used in the sense of a good power.

ता इन्ने ३ इव समना समानीरमीतवर्णा उपसच्चरंति । (*tā inne 3 iva samanā samānīramītavarṇā upasaccaraṃti |*)
गूहंतीरभ्वमसितं रुशद्भिः शुक्रास्तनूभिः शुचयो रुचानाः ॥ (*gūhaṃtīrabhvamasitaṃ ruśadbhiḥ śukrāstanūbhiḥ śucayo rucānāḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಪು ಸಂ.] 4-51-9)

in this ṛk the sense is a great pitch darkness. Thus, as the word *abhva* is used when an altered, violent elemental power is indicated, so it is used in the present ṛk too: here the prayer is "protect us from the great, fear-causing sin".

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page, noted briefly)

अचरंती (*acaraṃtī*) (root चर गतिभक्षणयोः (*cara gatibhakṣaṇayoḥ*)): the present-participle substitute for the tense suffix; by उगितश्च (*ugitaśca*) (Pā. Sū. 4-1-7 [as printed]) the feminine suffix *ṅīp*; when the *nañ*-tatpuruṣa compound is made, by तत्पुरुषेतुल्यार्थ (*tatpuruṣetulyārtha*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-2) the accent of the first member, the indeclinable, is retained. पद्वंतम् (*padvaṃtam*) ("that which has a foot"): *matup* by *tadasyāsti*; by झयः (*jhayaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 8-2-10) *m* becomes *v*; by interchange *matup* takes the acute; in the accusative singular, because it has the mark *uk*, the augment *num* (printed so). अपदी (*apadī*) ("those of which there is no foot"): by नञ्सुभ्याम् (*nañsubhyām*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-172) the last-syllable acute of the later member; the substitute *pad* for *pāda*; by पादोऽन्यतरस्याम् (*pādo 'nyatarasyām*) (Pā. Sū. 4-1-8) *ṅīp*; when the dual follows, by सुपां सुलुक् (*supāṃ suluk*) lengthening to the preceding class vowel.

*[A stray line "[5] Vol. 14", a page-footer artefact, ends p. 65 in the supplied text.]*

### Printed p. 66

*Running head: [66 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1 A. 24. Sū 185].*

दधाते (*dadhāte*) (root डुधाण् (*ḍudhāṇ*), in the sense of bearing and nourishing): present, third person dual; the finite-verb *nighāta* accent arises. द्यावापृथिवी (*dyāvāpṛthivī*) ("the sky and the earth"): by दिवोद्यावा (*divodyāvā*) (Pā. Sū. 6-3-29) the substitute *dyāvā* in the dvandva; in the Vedic manner a finite verb is used in the middle of the dvandva; by *sulug* of the case endings, with a dual following, lengthening to the preceding class vowel; because it stands at the beginning of the pāda there is no *nighāta*; by आमंत्रितस्य (*āmaṃtritasya*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-198) the first-syllable acute. रक्षतम् (*rakṣatam*) (root रक्षपालने (*rakṣapālane*), in the sense of protecting): imperative, second person dual; by आमंत्रितं (*āmaṃtritaṃ*) पूर्वमविद्यमानवत् (*pūrvamavidyamānavat*) (Pā. Sū. 8-1-72) the earlier word is as if non-existent, and since it does not follow a verb there is no *nighāta*; by तास्यनुदात्तेत् (*tāsyanudāttet*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-187) the present-tense suffix is unaccented and the accent of the root remains.
