#### Ṛk 3

*[As supplied, the label line of the Pada reads १ हदपाकि १ (*1 hadapāki 1*) (inside the Saṃhitā block); the label of the bhāṣya reads । (*|*) (inside the Pada block); standard labels inserted. The Saṃhitā's last word and the Ṛk number were split between two lines in the supplied text and have been joined.]*

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अ॒ने॒हो दा॒त्रमदि॑तेरन॒र्वं हु॒वे स्व॑र्वदव॒धं नम॑स्वत् ।
> तद्रो॑दसी जनयतं जरि॒त्रॆ द्यावा॒ रक्ष॑तं पृथिवी नो॒ अभ्वा॑त् ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अनेहः । दात्रं । अदितेः । अनर्वं । हुवे । स्वःऽवत् । अवधं । नमस्वत् ।
> तत् । रोदसी इति । जनयतं । जरित्रॆ । द्यावा । रक्षतं । पृथिवी इति । नः । अभ्वात् ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> अदितेः । एतदंतरिक्षस्याप्युपलक्षणं । अखंडनीयायाः पृथिव्यास्तादृशस्यां-
> तरिक्षस्य च संबंधि दात्रं धनं हुवे । आह्वयामि । स्पृहयामीत्यर्थः । कीदृशं
> तद्धनं । आनेहोऽपासं दुःखरहितं सुखात्मकं अनर्वमनरणं । अक्षीणमित्यर्थः ।

> *aditeḥ | etadaṃtarikṣasyāpyupalakṣaṇaṃ | akhaṃḍanīyāyāḥ pṛthivyāstādṛśasyāṃ-*
> *tarikṣasya ca saṃbaṃdhi dātraṃ dhanaṃ huve | āhvayāmi | spṛhayāmītyarthaḥ | kīdṛśaṃ*
> *taddhanaṃ | āneho 'pāsaṃ duḥkharahitaṃ sukhātmakaṃ anarvamanaraṇaṃ | akṣīṇamityarthaḥ |*

### Printed p. 67

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 2] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 67. The bhāṣya block continues from p. 66.*

> स्वर्वत् सर्वतः फलभूतेन स्वर्गेण तद्वत् अवधमहिंसितं नमस्वदन्नवत् । ईदृशं
> धनं हुवे । तदस्माभिः कांक्षितमुक्तलक्षणं धनं हे रोदसी द्यावापृथिव्य जरित्रे
> स्तोत्रे यजमानाय जनयतं । उत्पादयतं । द्यावेत्यादि व्याख्यातं ॥

*Gist:* I invoke the wealth of Aditi (the earth and the atmosphere), painless, undiminished, heaven-like, unharmed and full of food; O Heaven and Earth, grant such wealth to the praiser, and protect us from sin.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

अदितेः (*aditeḥ*) — of the indivisible (complete) atmosphere and earth. अनेहः (*anehaḥ*) — painless (hence of the nature of happiness). अनर्वं (*anarvaṃ*) — free from decay. स्वर्वत् (*svarvat*) — equal to heaven full of fruit. अवधं (*avadhaṃ*) — unharmed. नमस्वत् (*namasvat*) — abounding in food. दात्रं (*dātraṃ*) — wealth. हुवे (*huve*) — I desire. रोदसी (*rodasī*) — O Heaven and Earth. तत् (*tat*) — that wealth desired by us. जरित्रॆ (*jaritrè*) — to the praising sacrificer. जनयतं (*janayataṃ*) — bring about. द्यावापृथिवी (*dyāvāpṛthivī*) — O earth and atmosphere (as printed). अभ्वात् (*abhvāt*) — from the sin that causes fear. नः (*naḥ*) — us. रक्षतं (*rakṣataṃ*) — protect.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

I desire the wealth that belongs to the complete earth and atmosphere, that is happiness itself through the absence of pain, free from decay, equal to heaven full of fruit, unharmed and abounding in food. O Heaven and Earth, bring about for the praising sacrificer that wealth which we desire. Protect us from the sin that causes fear.

**Printed English translation**

I solicit of Aditi wealth, without pain or decay, like heaven (in its fullness of enjoyment), exempt from injury, and abounding in food: grant, Heaven and Earth, such wealth to him who praises you: defend us, Heaven and Earth, from great (danger).

*[Repaired by the dispatcher on Nov 9 2026: this paragraph had been left empty in window pp. 65-67 by a slip; the text is that of the supplied page, see vol98/review/edits_log.tsv.]*

*[As supplied, the label line of the Special Topics reads विशेषविवरगळु]* (*viśeṣavivaragaḷu]**); standard label inserted.]*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this ṛk there is the prayer दात्रं हुवे (*dātraṃ huve*), "I desire wealth"; and अदितेः, अनेहः, अनर्वं, स्वर्वत्, अवधं, नमस्वत् (*aditeḥ, anehaḥ, anarvaṃ, svarvat, avadhaṃ, namasvat*) are all epithets describing the nature of दात्रं (*dātraṃ*), that is, of wealth.

आदितेः (*āditeḥ*) — for this word Sāyaṇa has given the sense as follows: एतदंतरिक्षस्याप्युलक्षणं । अखंडनीयायाः पृथिव्याः तादृशस्यांतरिक्षस्य च संबंधि दात्रं । (*etadaṃtarikṣasyāpyulakṣaṇaṃ | akhaṃḍanīyāyāḥ pṛthivyāḥ tādṛśasyāṃtarikṣasya ca saṃbaṃdhi dātraṃ |*). The word *aditi* may refer to the atmosphere, and may denote the nature of the indivisible earth (the first letter is printed with a different vowel from the epithet in the previous sentence, as supplied).

### Printed p. 68

*Running head: [68 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24 Sū. 185]. The window begins inside the Special Topics of Ṛk 3, continuing the note on *aditi* from p. 67.*

In the present ṛk, as an epithet of both earth and atmosphere, it has been explained as the wealth belonging to the indivisible earth and atmosphere together.

The Nirukta authors have given for the word *aditi* the sense अदीना (*adīnā*) (Ni. 4-23): "not poor", of indivisible nature, not decaying. This is an epithet that tells the endlessness of Agni, of the divine mother Aditi and so on. Concerning the special purport of this word the scholar Max Müller has said thus:

" Aditi, an ancient God or Goddess is in reality, the earliest name invented to express the infinite. That was called A-diti, the unbound, the un-bounded. It is derived from Diti, bond and the negative particle and meant there-fore, originally what is free from bonds of any kind whether of spice or time, free from physical weakness, free from moral guilt."

( S. B. E. Vol. XXXII. p. 241. )

The principle that is endless, not limited by place, time and the like, immeasurable: to tell the nature of that principle the word is used in many places in the Ṛksaṃhitā. The seers have expressed a profound idea by using this word. Explaining the negative sense of the word *diti* (meaning "to be bound, to be limited, to be regulated"), he has said that it is a word indicating an endless principle that has neither physical limit nor moral deficiency.

अदितिद्यौरदितिरंतरिक्षमदितिर्माता स पिता स पुत्रः । (*aditidyauraditiraṃtarikṣamaditirmātā sa pitā sa putraḥ |*)
विश्वे देवा अदितिः संच जना अदितिर्जातमदितिर्जनित्वं ॥ (*viśve devā aditiḥ saṃca janā aditirjātamaditirjanitvaṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ] 1-89-10)

As described in ṛks like these, Aditi is the mother of the universe and of the nature of the universe. A goddess who is such a mother of the world may be said to preside over the whole physical world and over moral conduct. But it must be examined whether the word *aditi* indicates the nature of one separate deity, or is an epithet of Heaven and Earth and the like, of the nature of the Ādityas. There is also a view that, except in the sūkta Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-72, everywhere else the word *aditi* indicates only the nature of the Ādityas, the sons of Aditi. For example,

" Aditi is not a prominent deity in the Veda, she is celebrated rather in her sons,
Adityas, than in her own person . . . . . . ."

( S. B. E. XXXII. p. 243 )

### Printed p. 69

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 2.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 69.*

Such is the opinion of Max Müller and other scholars. In the present context that examination is not so necessary, so this matter will be examined later at a suitable occasion. In the present ṛk too the prayer is that immeasurable wealth connected with Heaven and Earth, of an endless and indivisible nature, may be obtained.

अनेहः (*anehaḥ*) — for this word Sāyaṇa has given the senses अपारं, दुःखरहितं, सुखात्मकं (*apāraṃ, duḥkharahitaṃ, sukhātmakaṃ*). In some places it also means "free from enemies".

अनर्वं (*anarvaṃ*) — अनरणं, अक्षीणं (*anaraṇaṃ, akṣīṇaṃ*): it is told that it means "free from decay". By अनर्वा- (*anarvā-*) (Ni. 6-23) one sense is "without support from others and without dependence", and another sense, "eternal, without old age and death", becomes possible. The sense stated in the ṛk "tri-nābhi cakram ajaram anarvam" (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-164-2) is the same for the word *anarva* in the present mantra. Its special senses will be explained where the words *anarvan*, *anarvāṇam* and so on occur.

स्वर्वत् (*svarvat*) — "equal to the heaven full of fruit": an epithet of दात्रं (*dātraṃ*). What the root of the word स्वर् (*svar*) is and how this word is derived was explained earlier, together with the derivation of the Nirukta authors स्वः आदित्यो भवति ; सु अरणः (*svaḥ ādityo bhavati ; su araṇaḥ*) (Ni. 2-14), and the use of the word स्वः (*svaḥ*) in different senses was told. As there is the word स्वर्वत् (*svarvat*), so स्वर्दृशः, (*svardṛśaḥ,*), स्वर्विद् (*svarvid*) and the like are used in many places and give special senses suited to those contexts. Here only a few contexts in which the word स्वर्वत् (*svarvat*) occurs are cited and its sense explained.

या इंद्र भुज आभरः स्वर्वा असुरेभ्यः । (*yā iṃdra bhuja ābharaḥ svarvā asurebhyaḥ |*)
स्तोतारमिन्मघवन्नस्य वर्धय ये च त्वे व्यक्तबर्हिषः ॥ (*stotāraminmaghavannasya vardhaya ye ca tve vyaktabarhiṣaḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ ಸಂ] 8-97-1)

Here: स्वर्वान्—सुखवान् ; स्वर्गवान् वा । अथवा स्वः शब्दः सर्वपर्यायः । सर्वं भूतजातं आत्मन एवोत्सन्नत्वात् तद्वान्॥ (*svarvān—sukhavān ; svargavān vā | athavā svaḥ śabdaḥ sarvaparyāyaḥ | sarvaṃ bhūtajātaṃ ātmana evotsannatvāt tadvān‖*), that is, since the word स्वः (*svaḥ*) can mean heaven, happiness or "all", *svarvān* means one with happiness, one with heaven, or one who has obtained everything.

तं शिशीता स्वध्वरं सत्यं सत्वानमृत्वियं । (*taṃ śiśītā svadhvaraṃ satyaṃ satvānamṛtviyaṃ |*)
उतो नु चिद्य ओहत आंडा शुष्णस्य भेदत्यजैः सर्वतीरपोनभंतामन्यके (*uto nu cidya ohata āṃḍā śuṣṇasya bhedatyajaiḥ sarvatīraponabhaṃtāmanyake*)
समे ॥ (*same ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-40-11)

### Printed p. 70

*Running head: [70 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. Ā. 24. Sū. 185] (the print has "ಆ." for "ಅ.", as supplied).*

In such places, when *svarvatīḥ* is an epithet of "water" (*apaḥ*), "divine" means water born in the divine world (in heaven). The same epithet also describes the natures of Agni and other deities. For example,

ज्योतिष्मतीमदितिं धारयत् क्षितिं स्वर्वतीमा सचेते दिवेदिवे जागृवांसा (*jyotiṣmatīmaditiṃ dhārayat kṣitiṃ svarvatīmā sacete divedive jāgṛvāṃsā*)
दिवेदिवे । (*divedive |*)
ज्योतिष्मत् क्षत्रमाशाते आदित्या दानुनस्पती ॥ (*jyotiṣmat kṣatramāśāte ādityā dānunaspatī ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-136-3)

here the word स्वर्वती (*svarvatī*) is an epithet of Aditi. Here, through the accomplishment of the sacrifice, the sense "possessing heaven" is stated.

आ ते शुष्मो वृषभ एतु पश्चादोत्तरादधरादा पुरस्तात् । (*ā te śuṣmo vṛṣabha etu paścādottarādadharādā purastāt |*)
आ विश्वतो अभि समेत्वर्नाजिंद्र द्युम्नं स्वर्वद्हेह्यस्मे ॥ (*ā viśvato abhi sametvarnājiṃdra dyumnaṃ svarvadhehyasme ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-19-9)

In such contexts it is said to mean "wealth together with happiness". In the same way in the present ṛk too the sense of "equal to heaven" is: wealth (*dātra*) that gives happiness like the happiness obtained in heaven.

रोदसी (*rodasī*) — the derivation and purport of this word were told earlier subtly. Its special senses will be told in more detail later under the Special Topics.

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page, noted briefly)

अनेहः (*anehaḥ*) (root हन (*hana*), in the sense of killing and going): by नञ् हनविहच (*nañ hanavihaca*) (Uṇ. Sū. 4-663 [as printed]) the suffix *asi*; the substitute *eha* for the root; by नलोपोनञ् (*naloponañ*) the elision of the *n* of *nañ*; by तस्मान्नडचि (*tasmānnaḍaci*) the augment *nuḍ* to the following *u*; with a dual accusative following, in the neuter by स्वमोर्नपुंसकात् (*svamornapuṃsakāt*) (Pā. Sū. [printed ೭ವ೧-೨೩, with a stray letter between the digits]) the elision of the case ending; by the accent of a word ending in a *kṛt* suffix, last-syllable acute. दात्रम् (*dātram*) (root डुदाञ् दाने (*ḍudāñ dāne*), "to give"): by दादिभ्यश्चंदसि (*dādibhyaścaṃdasi*) (Uṇ. Sū. 4-609) the suffix *tran*; by interchange last-syllable acute. हुवे (*huve*) (root ह्वेञ् (*hveñ*), in the sense of challenge and sound): present, first person singular; because it stands at the beginning of the pāda there is no *nighāta*; by the suffix accent, last-syllable acute. स्वर्वत् (*svarvat*) (*svar* means heaven; "that which has it"): the suffix *matup*; because it is *pit* it is unaccented; by नजिस्वरौस्वरिता (*najisvarausvaritā*) (printed "ಫಿ ಸೂ. ೭೪") the *svarita* accent of *svar*.

*[The window ends here, within the grammar page.]*

### Printed p. 71

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 2.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 71. The window begins inside the grammar page of Ṛk 3, continuing p. 70.*

अवधम् (*avadham*) (root हन (*hana*), "to kill and go"): in connection with the suffix *ap*, the substitute *vadha* for the root. "That of which there is no killing": by नञ्सुभ्याम् (*nañsubhyām*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-172) the last-syllable acute of the later member. जनयतम् (*janayatam*) (root जनी (*janī*), "to be born"): by हेतुमति च (*hetumati ca*) the suffix *ṇic*; since it has the name *mit* (by the rule beginning क्कसुरंजोमांताश्च (*kkasuraṃjomāṃtāśca*)), by मितां ह्रस्वः (*mitāṃ hrasvaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-4-92) the penultimate vowel is shortened; after the *ṇic* ending, imperative, second person dual; because it does not follow a verb, the *nighāta* accent arises (the print has "ಪರದಲ್ಲಿರುವುದರಿಂನ" as supplied).

#### Ṛk 4

*[The Saṃhitā and Pada of this ṛk were a picture in the supplied text. The picture was viewed and agrees with the machine text, which has been used with the lines joined; in the Saṃhitā word देवानामुभयेभिरन्नां (*devānāmubhayebhirannāṃ*) (and in the Pada) the conjunct after the first *a* could be read as ನ್ನ or ನ್ಹ on the picture, and the machine text's ನ್ನ is kept; the bhāṣya and Pratipadārtha print ಅಹ್ನಾಂ.]*

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अत॑प्यमाने॒ अव॒सावं॑ती॒ अनु॑ ष्याम॒ रोद॑सी दे॒वपु॑त्रे ।
> उ॒भे दे॒वाना॑मु॒भये॑भि॒रन्नां॒ द्यावा॒ रक्ष॑तं पृथिवी नो॒ अभ्वा॑त् ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अतप्यमाने इति । अवसा । अवंती इति । अनु । स्याम । रोदसी इति । देव- पुत्रे इति देवपुत्रे ।
> उभे इति । देवानां । उभयेभिः । अन्नां । द्यावा । रक्षतं । पृथिवी इति । नः । अभ्वात् ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> अवसा स्वतोऽधिकेन केनचित्कृतेनावनेन समृद्ध्यातप्यमाने अनीश्वरे ॥
> तप ऐश्वर्यकर्मण इदं रूपं ॥ अतप्यमाने अन्वैरपीड्यमाने । अवसान्नेना-
> वंती तर्पयंत्यौ देवपुत्रे देवा व्यवहर्तारो मनुष्याः पुत्रस्थानीया ययो-
> स्तादृश्यौ । लोकद्वयस्था मनुष्याश्च देवाश्च तदुपजीव्यत्वात्पुत्रा इत्युच्यंते ।

> *avasā svato 'dhikena kenacitkṛtenāvanena samṛddhyātapyamāne anīśvare ‖*
> *tapa aiśvaryakarmaṇa idaṃ rūpaṃ ‖ atapyamāne anvairapīḍyamāne | avasānnenā-*
> *vaṃtī tarpayaṃtyau devaputre devā vyavahartāro manuṣyāḥ putrasthānīyā yayo-*
> *stādṛśyau | lokadvayasthā manuṣyāśca devāśca tadupajīvyatvātputrā ityucyaṃte |*

### Printed p. 72

*Running head: [72 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24 Sū. 185].*

> ईदृशावुभे रोदसी द्यावापृथिव्यौ देवानामह्नां । एतद्रात्रेरप्युपलक्षणं ।
> द्योतमानानामह्नां रात्रीणां च संबंधिभिरुभयविधैः शीतोष्णादिरूपैर्विलक्षणै-
> र्धनैर्निमित्तभूतैः । तेषां लाभायेत्यर्थः । तदर्थं युवामनु स्याम । अनु-
> भवेम । शिष्टो व्याख्यातः ॥

*Gist:* Heaven and Earth, not oppressed by others (or sovereign), nourish with food the gods and men who are their sons; for the wealth that comes from days and nights, with their cold and heat, may we serve them.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

अवसा (*avasā*) — with abundant lordship. अतप्यमाने (*atapyamāne*) — those who have no better lord than themselves, अतप्यमाने (*atapyamāne*) — [or: not oppressed by others; अवसा (*avasā*) — with food; अवंती (*avaṃtī*) — who satisfy (all)] देवपुत्रे (*devaputre*) — having gods (and men) as sons. देवानां (*devānāṃ*) — shining अह्नां (*ahnāṃ*) — of days and nights उभयेभिः (*ubhayebhiḥ*) — endowed with the double characteristics such as cold and heat. उभे रोदसी (*ubhe rodasī*) — both Heaven and Earth अनु स्याम (*anu syāma*) — may we serve with devotion. द्यावापृथिवी (*dyāvāpṛthivī*) — O Heaven and Earth अभ्वात् (*abhvāt*) — from terrible calamity. नः (*naḥ*) — us रक्षतं (*rakṣataṃ*) — protect.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

May we serve with devotion Heaven and Earth, who are not oppressed by others, who satisfy all with food, who have gods and men as their sons, and who are endowed with the double characteristics, cold and heat and so on, of the shining days and nights. O Heaven and Earth, protect us from terrible calamity.

**Printed English translation**

May we ever be (devoted to) that Heaven and Earth, who are not subject to annoyance, who satisfy (all beings) with food, who have the gods (and men) for sons, and who are both endowed with the double (condition) of the divine days (and nights): defend us, Heaven and Earth, from great (danger).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

अवसा (*avasā*) — the word अवस् (*avas*) is read among the names of food (Ni. 3-8). On that basis, in the present ṛk one sense can be given: "with lordship in the form of food". Or, in the Ṛksaṃhitā verse cited by the Nirukta authors in another place (Ni. 10-3),

हिरण्यस्तूपः सवितर्यथा त्वांगिरसो जुह्वे वाजे अस्मिन् । (*hiraṇyastūpaḥ savitaryathā tvāṃgiraso juhve vāje asmin |*)
एवा त्वार्चन्नवसे वंदमानः सोमस्येवांशुं प्रति जागराहं ॥ (*evā tvārcannavase vaṃdamānaḥ somasyevāṃśuṃ prati jāgarāhaṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-149-5)

### Printed p. 73

*Running head: [Ā. ೨. Ā. ೫ Va. ೨.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 73 (as supplied).*

as the word अवसे (*avase*) is given the sense अवनाय (*avanāya*), that is, रक्षणाय (*rakṣaṇāya*) ("for protection"), so in the present ṛk too "by protection (in the form of food)" may be said. Sāyaṇa has given two kinds of meaning in this context. First, for the word अतप्यमाने (*atapyamāne*) he has given the sense अनीश्वरे (*anīśvare*) ("without a lord"); explaining how, he says तपेः ऐश्वर्यकर्मणः इदं रूपं (*tapeḥ aiśvaryakarmaṇaḥ idaṃ rūpaṃ*) (the sentence ends with "!" in the supplied text), that is, "this is the form of the root *tap*, meaning lordship": the meaning is "not subject to any other lord, themselves lords of the whole world". Then अवसा अतप्यमाने (*avasā atapyamāne*) means that they are lords of the world through abundance of wealth. Second, अतप्यमाने (*atapyamāne*) अन्न्यैरपीड्य (*annyairapīḍya*) माने (*māne*) means "not oppressed by others", and अवसा अवंती (*avasā avaṃtī*) means "protecting the world with protections".

देवपुत्रे (*devaputre*) — देवाः, व्यवहर्तारो मनुष्याः पुत्रस्थानीयाः ययोस्तादृश्यौ । (*devāḥ, vyavahartāro manuṣyāḥ putrasthānīyāḥ yayostādṛśyau |*) लोकद्वयस्थाः मनुष्याश्च देवाश्च तमपजीव्यत्वात् पुत्रा इत्युच्यंते । (*lokadvayasthāḥ manuṣyāśca devāśca tamapajīvyatvāt putrā ityucyaṃte |*). Relying on the heavenly world, men by its favour such as rain, and, relying on the earth, the gods by the offering of sacrificial oblations, depend on these two; therefore, as having all these as sons, Heaven and Earth are addressed.

इमौ वै लोकौ सहास्तां तौ वैतां नावर्षन्न समपतत्ते पंचजना न सम- (*imau vai lokau sahāstāṃ tau vaitāṃ nāvarṣanna samapatatte paṃcajanā na sama-*)
जानत तौ देवाः समनयंस्तौ संयंतावेतं देवविवाहं व्यवहेतां । (*jānata tau devāḥ samanayaṃstau saṃyaṃtāvetaṃ devavivāhaṃ vyavahetāṃ |*)

(Ai. Brā. 19-5 [the print has "ಐ, ಬ್ರಾ,"])

The conjugal relation of Heaven and Earth described in this sentence, and the point that they are the support of the activity of all the worlds, were told earlier too.

य इमे द्यावापृथिवी जनित्री रूपैरपिंशद्भवनानि विश्वा । (*ya ime dyāvāpṛthivī janitrī rūpairapiṃśadbhavanāni viśvā |*)
तमद्य होतरिषितो यजीयान् देवं त्वष्टारमिह यक्षि विद्वान् ॥ (*tamadya hotariṣito yajīyān devaṃ tvaṣṭāramiha yakṣi vidvān ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಖ. ಸಂ.] 11-10-9)

Here Heaven and Earth are described as the mother and father of the whole universe: the meaning is that they are the begetters of the whole world of gods, men and so on. In the same way,

उपहूते द्यावापृथिवी पूर्वजे ऋतावरी देवी देवपुत्रे इति । (*upahūte dyāvāpṛthivī pūrvaje ṛtāvarī devī devaputre iti |*)

(Śa. Brā. 1-8-1.-29 [as printed])

in Brāhmaṇa sentences like this too, the epithet "having the gods as sons" for Heaven and Earth is established. In the present ṛk too the same point is told. And "Heaven and Earth are the parents, the support and protectors of the whole universe": thus their greatness is described; the sentence is cut off at the end of the window.

### Printed p. 74

*Running head: [74 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 185]. The window begins inside the Special Topics of Ṛk 4, continuing p. 73.*

… how they are described in the … will be told in this same part later, in the special topics of the ṛk 1-185-6.

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page, noted briefly)

अतप्यमाने (*atapyamāne*) (root तप (*tapa*), in the sense of lordship; divādi class): the present-participle substitute *śānac*; by the rule beginning दिवादिभ्यः (*divādibhyaḥ*) the suffix *śyan*; by आनेमुक् (*ānemuk*) (Pā. Sū. 7-2-82) the augment *muk*; when the *nañ*-tatpuruṣa compound is made, by तत्पुरुषे तुल्यार्थ (*tatpuruṣe tulyārtha*) the accent of the first member, the indeclinable, is retained; dual form. अवंती (*avaṃtī*) (root अव (*ava*), "to protect"): the present-participle substitute; the feminine suffix *ṅīp* by उगितश्च (*ugitaśca*); when the dual ending follows, by सुपां सुलुक्—सूत्रदिंद (*supāṃ suluk—sūtradiṃda*) lengthening to the preceding class vowel. स्याम (*syāma*) (root अस (*asa*), "to be"): optative, first person plural; the *nighāta* accent of a finite verb; by उपसर्गप्रादुर्भ्यां (*upasargaprādurbhyāṃ*) (Pā. Sū. 8-3-87) the root's *s* becomes *ṣ*. देवपुत्रे (*devaputre*) (देवाः पुत्राः ययोस्ते देवपुत्रे. (*devāḥ putrāḥ yayoste devaputre.*), "those whose sons are the gods"): by बहुव्रीहौ प्रकृत्यापूर्व- (*bahuvrīhau prakṛtyāpūrva-*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-1) the accent of the first member is retained; dual form (the supplied text has "ದ್ವಿಪಚನಾಂತರೂಪ", as supplied). उभयेभिः (*ubhayebhiḥ*): by उभयोन्यत्र (*ubhayonyatra*) the suffix *ayac* on *ubha*; because the rule बहुलं- (*bahulaṃ-*) छंदसि (*chaṃdasi*) makes the substitute *ais* of *bhis* optional, it does not occur; the suffix has the first-syllable acute.

#### Ṛk 5

*[As supplied, the label line of the Saṃhitā reads शंखशापाकि]* (*śaṃkhaśāpāki]**), of the Pada ⟦⟧ (with a lone danda line after the second part of the Pada), and of the bhāṣya ⟦⟧; standard labels inserted. The Saṃhitā and Pada words जामिा (*jāmiā*) and the Pada's final word are printed as supplied.]*

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> सं॒गच्छ॑माने युव॒ती समं॑ते॒ स्वसा॑रा जा॒मिा पि॒त्रोरु॒पस्थे॑ ।
> अ॒भि॒जिघ्रं॑ती॒ भुव॑नस्य॒ नाभिं॒ द्यावा॒ रक्ष॑तं पृथिवी नो॒ अभ्वा॑त् ॥ ५ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> संगच्छमाने इति संगच्छमाने । युवती इति । समंते इति संअंते । स्वसारा ।
> जामिा इति । पित्रोः । उपस्थे ।

> *saṃgacchamāne iti saṃgacchamāne | yuvatī iti | samaṃte iti saṃaṃte | svasārā |*
> *jāmiā iti | pitroḥ | upasthe |*

### Printed p. 75

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5 Varga 2] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 75.*

> अभिजिघ्रंती इत्यभिजिघ्रंती । भुवनस्य । नाभिं । द्यावा । रक्षतं । पृथिवी इति । नः । अभ्यात् ॥ ५ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> संगच्छमाने परस्परमुपकारित्वेन सह युज्यमाने । वृष्टिहविषोश्च परस्पर-
> मुपकार्योपकारकभावः । यद्वा । पूर्वं संस्कृष्टे एव सत्यौ पश्चाद्वियुज्य वृष्टि-
> हविषी अकुर्वंत्यौ पश्चान्मनुष्यैः प्रार्थितैर्देवैर्विवाहिते सत्यौ संगते अभूता-
> मित्याहुः । आयमर्थो द्यावापृथिवी सहास्तां । तै. ब्रा. १-१-३-२ । इत्यादि
> ब्राह्मणे समाम्नातः । युवती नित्यतरुण्यॆ मिश्रयंत्यौ ना सर्वॆषु भावेषु
> समंते समानांतिके समानसर्यंते वा स्वसारा परस्परं स्वसृभूते जामी बंधु-
> भूते । प्रजापतेः सकाशात्सहोत्पन्नत्वात्परस्परं जामित्वं । तथा च निगमौ दिवं च
> पृथिवीं चांतरिक्षमथो स्वः । ऋ. १०-१९०-३ । यतो द्यावापृथिवी निष्टतक्षुः ।
> ऋ. १०-३१-७ । इति । पित्रोः सर्वस्य पितृस्थानीययोः पालकयोस्तयोरुपस्थ
> उत्संगे स्थितं भुवनस्य भूतजातस्य नाभिं बंधकमुदकमभिजिघ्रंती अभिघ्राणं
> कुर्वंत्यौ स्पृशंत्यौ । समानमेतदुदकं । ऋ. १-१६४-५१ । इत्यादिमंत्रवर्णा-
> दुभयोरुदकप्रदत्वं प्रसिद्धं । ईदृश्यौ नो रक्षतं ॥

*Gist:* Heaven and Earth, always together, benefiting each other (rain and oblation), ever young, equal in extent, sisters and kin through Prajāpati, hold the water, the navel of the beings of the world, in the lap of their parents; may they protect us.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

संगच्छमाने (*saṃgacchamāne*) — (with the wish to help each other) always staying together. युवती (*yuvatī*) — "ever young" (the supplied text prints "ನಿತ್ಯಕರ್ಮರೂ", as supplied). समंते (*samaṃte*) — of equal extent. स्वसारा (*svasārā*) — mutually loving like elder and younger sisters. जामिा (*jāmiā*) — (being children of Prajāpati) mutually kin. पित्रोः (*pitroḥ*) — of the parents or protectors. उपस्थे (*upasthe*) — placed on the lap (thigh). भुवनस्य (*bhuvanasya*) — for all beings living in the world. नाभिं (*nābhiṃ*) — the water that is the support. आभिजिघ्रंती (*ābhijighraṃtī*) — touching (holding) it. द्यावापृथिवी (*dyāvāpṛthivī*) — O Heaven and Earth. अभ्वात् (*abhvāt*) — from great fear. नः (*naḥ*) — us. रक्षतं (*rakṣataṃ*) — protect.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Heaven and Earth, you two are always together and ever young. As children of Prajāpati you are loving and kin to each other like sisters. You hold the water, the support of all beings. Such as you, protect us from great fear.

### Printed p. 76

*Running head: [76 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 185].*

**Printed English translation**

Going always together, equally young, and of the like termination, sisters, and kindred, and scenting the navel of the world, placed on their lap as its parents : defend us, Heaven and Earth, from great danger.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

The seer Agastya has shown his skill of composition well in this sūkta. The extraordinary deeds of Heaven and Earth, the deities of this sūkta, and their protection of the world are described in an excellent and natural body of words. That is, in describing or praising these deities the words अतप्यमाने, अवंती, देवपुत्रे, संगच्छमाने, युवती, समंते, स्वसारा, जामी, अभिजिघ्रंती, उर्वि, सद्मनी, बृहती, जनित्रि, सुप्रतीके, पृथ्वी, बहुले, दूरेअंते, सुभगे, सुप्रतूर्ति (*atapyamāne, avaṃtī, devaputre, saṃgacchamāne, yuvatī, samaṃte, svasārā, jāmī, abhijighraṃtī, urvi, sadmanī, bṛhatī, janitri, supratīke, pṛthvī, bahule, dūreaṃte, subhage, supratūrti*) have been used. These words are natural, of profound sense and fitting to the context, and moreover the use of these words in praising the qualities of the gods gives dignity and devotion to the poetry. Therefore there is no need to say separately that the composition of the ṛks of this sūkta is on a very high level. Besides, the profound principles propounded in the first ṛk of this sūkta in the form of question and answer, and the similes used here and there, are excellent examples of the seer's skill in poetic composition.

संगच्छमाने (*saṃgacchamāne*) — this word means "those who go or are together". Sāyaṇa says in his bhāṣya परस्परमुपकारित्वेन सह युज्यमाने । वृष्टिहविषोश्च परस्पर- मुपकार्योपकारकभावः । यद्वा । पूर्वं संसृष्टे एव सत्यौ पश्चाद्वियुज्य वृष्टिहविषी अकुर्वंत्यौ पश्चान्मनुष्यैः प्रार्थितैर्देवैर्निवाहिते (*parasparamupakāritvena saha yujyamāne | vṛṣṭihaviṣośca paraspara- mupakāryopakārakabhāvaḥ | yadvā | pūrvaṃ saṃsṛṣṭe eva satyau paścādviyujya vṛṣṭihaviṣī akurvaṃtyau paścānmanuṣyaiḥ prārthitairdevairnivāhite*) तामित्याहुः (*tāmityāhuḥ*) (the print has "ನಿವಾಹಿತೇ" for "ವಿವಾಹಿತೇ" in the bhāṣya as quoted here, as supplied). That is, they are together in order to benefit each other. The benefit here is rain-water (rain) and the oblation offered to the gods. Because rain makes plants and the like arise and gives the grains needed for the oblation, it is described here that rain and oblations are obtained by mutual co-operation. If it is asked what the relation of Heaven and Earth to rain is, the seer explains that too in this same ṛk by the sentence अभिजिघ्रंती भुवनस्य नाभिं (*abhijighraṃtī bhuvanasya nābhiṃ*): since in the atmosphere (*dyāvā*) the clouds gather together and produce rain, plants and the like grow on the earth (*pṛthivī*); therefore it is meant that Heaven and Earth are mutually helpful to rain and to the origin of plants.

Or, although Prajāpati created Heaven and Earth together, that is, at one time, they are for other reasons separate and distinct, and the rain which is very necessary for the world (the sentence is cut off at the end of the window).

### Printed p. 77

*Running head: [Ā. ೨. Ā ೩. Va. ೨.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 77 (as supplied). The window begins inside the Special Topics of Ṛk 5, continuing the second explanation of *saṃgacchamāne* from p. 76.*

… not producing (the rain), at the prayer of men the gods performed the marriage of these Heaven and Earth and made them one: this other sense Sāyaṇa has given. On the point that Heaven and Earth were together, Sāyaṇa has cited

द्यावापृथिवी सहास्त्रां । ते वियती अब्रूतां ॥ (*dyāvāpṛthivī sahāstrāṃ | te viyatī abrūtāṃ ‖*)

(Tai. Brā. 1-1-3 2 [as printed "ತ್ರೈ. ಬ್ರಾ,"])

the sentence of the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa. On the point that Prajāpati created Heaven and Earth,

प्रजापतिरकामयत प्रजायेय भूयान्या मिति सतपोऽतप्यत स तपस्त- (*prajāpatirakāmayata prajāyeya bhūyānyā miti satapo 'tapyata sa tapasta-*)
स्वेमाल्लोकानसृजत पृथिवीमंतरिक्षं दिवं। (*svemāllokānasṛjata pṛthivīmaṃtarikṣaṃ divaṃ|*)

(Ai. Brā. 25-7 [as printed "ಇ ಬ್ರಾ."])

there is this sentence of the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa. Prajāpati (Brahman), desiring to create, performed austerity. Afterwards, by the greatness of that austerity, he created these worlds, that is, the worlds called earth, atmosphere and heaven: such is the purport of this mantra. This matter is stated in the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā itself:

सूर्याचंद्रमसौ धाता यथापूर्वमकल्पयत् । (*sūryācaṃdramasau dhātā yathāpūrvamakalpayat |*)
दिवं च पृथिवीं चांतरिक्षमथो स्वः ॥ (*divaṃ ca pṛthivīṃ cāṃtarikṣamatho svaḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಪು ಸಂ.] 10-190-3)

In this ṛk it is said that Brahman, the creator, when creating, created the sun and the moon and the worlds called heaven, earth and atmosphere. Moreover,

जनिता दिवो जनिता पृथिव्याः पिबा सोमं मदाय कं शतक्रतो । (*janitā divo janitā pṛthivyāḥ pibā somaṃ madāya kaṃ śatakrato |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-36-4)

यन्मातरं च पितरं च साकमजनयथास्तन्वगः स्वायाः । (*yanmātaraṃ ca pitaraṃ ca sākamajanayathāstanvagaḥ svāyāḥ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-54-3)

in such ṛks it is said that Indra himself created Heaven and Earth. But since in many contexts the identity of Indra and Prajāpati is stated, there is no contradiction in this. And,

सोमापूषणा जनना रयीणां जनना दिवो जनना पृथिव्याः । (*somāpūṣaṇā jananā rayīṇāṃ jananā divo jananā pṛthivyāḥ |*)
जात् विश्वस्य भुवनस्य गोप् देवा अकृण्वन्नमृतस्य नाभिं । (*jāt viśvasya bhuvanasya gop devā akṛṇvannamṛtasya nābhiṃ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 2-40-1)

in this ṛk it is said that the deities Soma and Pūṣan produced Heaven and Earth.

### Printed p. 78

*Running head: [78 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1 Ā. 24 Sū. 185] (the print has "ಆ." for "ಅ.", as supplied).*

स वां यज्ञेषु मानवी इंदुर्जनिष्ट रोदसी । (*sa vāṃ yajñeṣu mānavī iṃdurjaniṣṭa rodasī |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-98-9)

प्र हिन्वानो जनिता रोदस्यो रथो न वाजं सनिष्यन्नयासीत् । (*pra hinvāno janitā rodasyo ratho na vājaṃ saniṣyannayāsīt |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-90-1)

सोमः पवते जनिता मतीनां जनिता दिवो जनिता पृथिव्याः । (*somaḥ pavate janitā matīnāṃ janitā divo janitā pṛthivyāḥ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-96-5)

in such ṛks it is said that the Soma deity created Heaven and Earth.

नाभ्या आसीदंतरिक्षं शिष्णोर्दौः समवर्तत । (*nābhyā āsīdaṃtarikṣaṃ śiṣṇordauḥ samavartata |*)
पद्भ्यां भूमिर्दिशः श्रोत्रात्तथा लोका अकल्पयन् ॥ (*padbhyāṃ bhūmirdiśaḥ śrotrāttathā lokā akalpayan ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 10-90-14)

In this ṛk it is said that from the navel region of the Supreme Person the atmospheric world arose, from his head the heavenly world, and from his feet the earthly world. Some seers, astonished at Heaven and Earth, have indicated doubt: who made them, how did they make them, from what substance did they make them?

किं स्विध्वनं क उ स वृक्ष आस यतो द्यावापृथिवी निष्कृतक्षुः । (*kiṃ svidhvanaṃ ka u sa vṛkṣa āsa yato dyāvāpṛthivī niṣkṛtakṣuḥ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-31-7)

In this ṛk the seer named Kavaṣa Ailūṣa, and in

किं स्विध्वनं क उ स वृक्ष आस यतो द्यावापृथिवी निष्कृतक्षुः । (*kiṃ svidhvanaṃ ka u sa vṛkṣa āsa yato dyāvāpṛthivī niṣkṛtakṣuḥ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-81-4)

this ṛk the seer named Viśvakarman Bhauvana, indicate doubt and wonder: "From which forest, from which tree, did they fashion Heaven and Earth?" In answer to this question, in the ṛks 2 and 3 of the same sūkta,

किं स्विदासीदधिष्ठानमारंभणं कतमत्स्वित् किमासीत् । (*kiṃ svidāsīdadhiṣṭhānamāraṃbhaṇaṃ katamatsvit kimāsīt |*)
यतो भूमिं जनयन् विश्वकर्मा विद्यामौर्णोन्महिना विश्वचक्षाः । (*yato bhūmiṃ janayan viśvakarmā vidyāmaurṇonmahinā viśvacakṣāḥ |*)
विश्वतश्च क्षुरुत विश्वतो मुखो विश्वतो बाहुरुत विश्वतस्सात् । (*viśvataśca kṣuruta viśvato mukho viśvato bāhuruta viśvatassāt |*)
सं बाहुभ्यां धमति सं पतत्रैर्द्यावाभूमी जनयन् देव एकः ॥ (*saṃ bāhubhyāṃ dhamati saṃ patatrairdyāvābhūmī janayan deva ekaḥ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-81-2, 3)

"What was there before creation? What was the nature of the thing that was there at the beginning? From what thing did the Supreme Brahman named Viśvakarman create Heaven and Earth by his greatness?"

### Printed p. 79

*Running head: [Ā ೨ Ā. ೫. Va. ೨] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 79 (as supplied).*

asking himself thus, the seer states the answer there too: "The one god (the Supreme Self named Viśvakarman), who has eyes everywhere, that is, the power to see everything everywhere at all times, and who has faces, arms and feet in all directions, that is, who is all-powerful, created these Heaven and Earth." On the marriage of Heaven and Earth, the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa says thus:

इमे वै लोकौ सहास्तां तौ व्येतां नावर्षन्न समतपत्ते पंचजना न (*ime vai lokau sahāstāṃ tau vyetāṃ nāvarṣanna samatapatte paṃcajanā na*)
समजानत तौ देवाः समनयंस्तौ संयंतावेतं देवविवाहं व्यवहेतां ॥ (*samajānata tau devāḥ samanayaṃstau saṃyaṃtāvetaṃ devavivāhaṃ vyavahetāṃ ‖*)

(Ai. Brā. 19-5 [the print has "ಐ. ಬ್ರಾ"])

That is: formerly these worlds, Heaven and Earth, were together in one place; for some reason they became separate. Then the rain stopped, and there was no sunshine either; the "five peoples", that is, those of the Brāhmaṇa and the other classes and all beings, were distressed. They had no happiness. Then the gods, for the welfare of the world, performed a marriage for these Heaven and Earth in accordance with the divine rule, united them and brought happiness to the world: so it is said. This same matter Sāyaṇa has told in his bhāṣya.

युवती—युवकरु, (*yuvatī—yuvakaru,*) — "youths, the young". Ordinarily it is the custom of the seers to describe all the gods as young, that is, not subject to the changes of old age, always powerful and beautiful. Especially in regard to Agni, Indra, Uṣas, the Aśvin deities, Heaven and Earth and other deities this epithet is used most. In

अग्निनाग्निः समिध्यते कविर्गृहपतिर्युवा । (*agnināgniḥ samidhyate kavirgṛhapatiryuvā |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-12-6)

this word is used of Agni; in

पुरां भिंदुर्युवा कविरमितौजा अजायतॆ । (*purāṃ bhiṃduryuvā kaviramitaujā ajāyatè |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-11-4)

it is used of Indra; in

युवं तुग्राय पूर्वभिरेवैः पुनर्मन्यानभवतं यवाना । (*yuvaṃ tugrāya pūrvabhirevaiḥ punarmanyānabhavataṃ yavānā |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-117-14)

of the Aśvin deities; in

आ नो जने श्रवयतं युवाना श्रुतं मे मित्रावरुणा हवेमा । (*ā no jane śravayataṃ yuvānā śrutaṃ me mitrāvaruṇā havemā |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-62-5)

and such ṛks of Mitra and Varuṇa; and in

एषा दिवो दुहिता प्रत्यदर्शि व्युच्छंती युवतिः शुक्रवासाः । (*eṣā divo duhitā pratyadarśi vyucchaṃtī yuvatiḥ śukravāsāḥ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-113-7)

and such ṛks of the Uṣas deity (the sentence continues; the window ends here).

### Printed p. 80

*Running head: [80 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 185]. The window begins inside the Special Topics of Ṛk 5, continuing the note on *yuvatī* from p. 79.*

उत स्वसारा युवती भवंती आदु ब्रुवाते मिथुनानि नाम । (*uta svasārā yuvatī bhavaṃtī ādu bruvāte mithunāni nāma |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 3-54-7)

in such ṛks this word is used of Heaven and Earth too.

समंते (*samaṃte*) — समानांतिके समानपर्यंते वा (*samānāṃtike samānaparyaṃte vā*): the two worlds Heaven and Earth have one end, or equal extent; that is, the other world is as extensive as one world; the meaning is that the extent of the two is the same. In the Taittirīya Saṃhitā (in the Yajurveda),

अंतर्हितो हि देवलोको मनुष्यलोकात् । (*aṃtarhito hi devaloko manuṣyalokāt |*)

(Tai. Saṃ. [printed ತೈ. ಸಂ.] 6-1-1.1)

it is said that the human world (the earth) and the divine world (heaven) are woven into each other; that is, the earth and heaven (the atmosphere) are of equal extent.

स्वसारा (*svasārā*) — परस्परं स्वसृभूते (*parasparaṃ svasṛbhūte*): here the use of the word *svasṛ* does not indicate "sisters", but is meant to say "loving each other like sisters and of the same position (rank)". The words *yuvatī*, *samante* and *svasārā* of the present ṛk are words that indicate the same sense as the epithets of Heaven and Earth in the seventh ṛk, दूरेअंते (*dūreaṃte*) and so on:

समान्या वियुते दूरे अंते ध्रुवे पदे तस्थतुर्जागरूके । (*samānyā viyute dūre aṃte dhruve pade tasthaturjāgarūke |*)
उत स्वसारा युवती भवंती आदु ब्रुवाते मिथुनानि नाम ॥ (*uta svasārā yuvatī bhavaṃtī ādu bruvāte mithunāni nāma ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 3-54-7)

and are found in such ṛks.

जामि (*jāmi*) — those who are related to each other. It is explained that because Prajāpati alone created Heaven and Earth at one time, there is mutual kinship. The word जामि (*jāmi*) is read among the names of water (Ni. 2-24), but here the bhāṣyakāra has taken the sense "kinship". Yāska in his Nirukta says:

न जामयो भगिन्यै । जामिरन्येऽस्यां जनयंति जामपत्यं । जमतेर्वा (*na jāmayo bhaginyai | jāmiranye 'syāṃ janayaṃti jāmapatyaṃ | jamatervā*)
स्याद्गतिकर्मणो निर्गमनप्राया भवति ॥ (*syādgatikarmaṇo nirgamanaprāyā bhavati ‖*)

(Ni. 3-7 [the print has "ಸಿ."])

that is, the word *jāmi* means *bhaginī* (sister), because in this woman others, that is, the husband, beget sons, she is called *jāmi*; or because the word *jāmi* has the sense of going (that is, after marriage this woman leaves the house of the father who begot her and goes to the house of her husband (printed so)),

### Printed p. 81

*Running head: [Ā. ೨. Ā. ೫. Va. ೩.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 81 (the print has "ಅ." as supplied; the varga number reads ೩).*

the sister is called *jāmi*: so he has stated the derivation. It is fitting that in this ṛk the general sense "kinship" is stated.

पित्रोः उपस्थे (*pitroḥ upasthe*) — पित्रोः सर्वस्य पितृस्थानिययोः पालकयोस्तयोरुपस्थ उत्संगे स्थितं । (*pitroḥ sarvasya pitṛsthāniyayoḥ pālakayostayorupastha utsaṃge sthitaṃ |*): the word is explained as "in the lap of these Heaven and Earth, who are like father and mother protectors of all beings in the world". On the point that Heaven and Earth are the parents of the world,

उरुव्यचसा महिनी असश्चता पिता माता च भुवनानि रक्षतः । (*uruvyacasā mahinī asaścatā pitā mātā ca bhuvanāni rakṣataḥ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಋ. ಸಂ.] 1-160-2)

तन्नो वातो मयोभु वातु भेषजं तन्माता पृथिवी तत्पिता द्यौः । (*tanno vāto mayobhu vātu bheṣajaṃ tanmātā pṛthivī tatpitā dyauḥ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-89-4)

इदं व्यावापृथिवी सत्यमस्तु पितर्मातर्यदिहोपब्रुवे वां । (*idaṃ vyāvāpṛthivī satyamastu pitarmātaryadihopabruve vāṃ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-185-11 [the print has "ವ್ಯಾವಾಪೃಥಿವೀ" in the verse, as supplied])

आ सुष्टुती नमसा वर्तयध्यै द्यावा वाजाय पृथिवी अमृध्रे । (*ā suṣṭutī namasā vartayadhyai dyāvā vājāya pṛthivī amṛdhre |*)
षिता माता मधुवचाः सुहस्ता भरेभरे नो यशसावविष्टां ॥ (*ṣitā mātā madhuvacāḥ suhastā bharebhare no yaśasāvaviṣṭāṃ ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-43-2)

in many such ṛks it is described that the earth is the mother of the whole world and heaven (the atmosphere) the father. As parents seat their sons on their lap, so the seer describes that Heaven and Earth, the parents of the world, hold on their lap, that is, in the atmosphere, for the nourishment of the world, the water of the clouds that has been collected for rain, which stands in the position of a son.

अभिजिघ्रंती (*abhijighraṃtī*) — अभिघ्राणं कुर्वंत्यौ, स्पृशंत्यौ । (*abhighrāṇaṃ kurvaṃtyau, spṛśaṃtyau |*): "those who smell", that is, "who touch", is Sāyaṇa's explanation. But this sense does not fit here, and why the seer has used this word, and what his intention is, cannot be clearly understood. Since this word is used nowhere else in the Ṛgveda, it is not possible to know what the opinion of other seers on the sense of the word was. Probably, since it is seen here and there that in the world people smell an object that is dear to them and keep it safe with themselves, the seer's intention may be that these Heaven and Earth lovingly hold within themselves the water in the mass of clouds for the use of people.

भवनस्य नाभिं (*bhavanasya nābhiṃ*) — the word भुवनशब्दवु (*bhuvanaśabdavu*) is read among the names of water (Ni. 2-24). And,

*[A stray line "[6] Vol. 14", a page-footer artefact, follows in the supplied text.]*

### Printed p. 82

*Running head: [82 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 185].*

नीचीनबारं वरुणः कवंधं प्र ससर्ज रोदसी अंतरिक्षं। (*nīcīnabāraṃ varuṇaḥ kavaṃdhaṃ pra sasarja rodasī aṃtarikṣaṃ|*)
तेन विश्वस्य भुवनस्य राजा वयं न वृष्टिवर्व्युनत्ति भूम ॥ (*tena viśvasya bhuvanasya rājā vayaṃ na vṛṣṭivarvyunatti bhūma ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ ಸಂ] 5-85-3)

for the word भुवनस्य (*bhuvanasya*) in this ṛk Yāska has given the derivation यास्करु-सर्वस्य भुवसन्य (भूतजातकस्योदकस्य वा) ऎंदु (*yāskaru-sarvasya bhuvasanya (bhūtajātakasyodakasya vā) èṃdu*) (Ni. 10-4); *bhuvana* means "the world" or "the beings of the world". But here the bhāṣyakāra has taken only the sense "water". For the word नाभि (*nābhi*) Yāska

संबंधनान्नाभिः सन्नहनान्नाभ्या सन्नद्धा गर्भा जायंत इत्याहुरेतस्मादेव (*saṃbaṃdhanānnābhiḥ sannahanānnābhyā sannaddhā garbhā jāyaṃta ityāhuretasmādeva*)
ज्ञातीन् सनाभय इत्याचक्षते । (*jñātīn sanābhaya ityācakṣate |*)

(Ni. [printed ೩. ೪-೨೦ ])

has given the derivation; that is, the word *nābhi* may be given the sense "the central region that binds together". Sāyaṇa, in explaining the ṛk

पृच्छामि त्वा परमंतं पृथिव्याः पृच्छामि यत्र भुवनस्य नाभिः । (*pṛcchāmi tvā paramaṃtaṃ pṛthivyāḥ pṛcchāmi yatra bhuvanasya nābhiḥ |*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-164-34)

says भुवनस्य भूतजातस्य नाभिः संनाहो बंधनं । यत्र सर्वं संनद्धं भवति तमित्यर्थः (*bhuvanasya bhūtajātasya nābhiḥ saṃnāho baṃdhanaṃ | yatra sarvaṃ saṃnaddhaṃ bhavati tamityarthaḥ*), that is, that in which everything gathers and joins is called *nābhi*. In the present ṛk the intention is the water of the clouds gathered together in one place for the sake of rain, that is, the gathered water.

*[As supplied, the label line of the grammar page reads ವ್ಯಾಕೆತಪ್ರಕ್ರಿಯಾ; standard label inserted.]*

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page, noted briefly)

संगच्छमाने (*saṃgacchamāne*) (root गम्‌ल्य गतौ. (*gamlya gatau.*), "to go"): because the prefix *sam* is joined, by समोगमृ- च्छिभ्याम् (*samogamṛ- cchibhyām*) (Pā. Sū. 1-3-29) the middle voice; the present-participle substitute *śānac*; by the rule तास्यनुदात्तेत् (*tāsyanudāttet*) the present-tense suffix is unaccented and the accent of the root remains; in the compound the accent of a word ending in a *kṛt* suffix; in the feminine, because it ends in *a*, *ṭāp*. युवती (*yuvatī*): by यूनस्तिः (*yūnastiḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 4-1-77) the suffix *ti* after the word *yuvan* in the feminine; by अन् (*an*) (Pā. Sū. 6-4-167) it keeps its original form, so the elision of *ṭi* does not occur; by नलोवति प्रातिपदिकांतस्य (*nalovati prātipadikāṃtasya*) (Pā. Sū. 8-2-7) elision of the *n*; by the suffix accent, last-syllable acute; when a dual follows, by सुपां सुलुक् (*supāṃ suluk*) lengthening to the preceding class vowel. समंते (*samaṃte*) ("those two of which the nearness is the same"): elision of the *k* of the word *antika*; by समानस्य छंदस्य (*samānasya chaṃdasya*) (Pā. Sū. 6-3-84) the substitute *sa* for the word *samāna*; by the rule हौप्रकृत्या (*hauprakṛtyā*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-1) the accent of the first member is retained.

*[The window ends here, within the grammar page.]*

### Printed p. 83

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 3.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 83 (the print has "ಆ." for the second abbreviation, as supplied). The window begins inside the grammar page of Ṛk 5, continuing p. 82.*

स्वसारा (*svasārā*): by सुपां सुलुक् (*supāṃ suluk*) the dual ending *au*; the substitute *a* for the case ending (as printed, with *sulug* of the case endings). अभिजिघ्रंती (*abhijighraṃtī*) (root "to smell", printed ಫ್ರಾ, in the sense of taking in scent): the present-participle substitute for the tense suffix; by पाघ्राध्मा-(पा. (*pāghrādhmā-(pā.*) (Pā. Sū. 7-3-78) the substitute for the root; in the feminine, by "ugit" the suffix (printed "ಜೇಪ್"); because the present-tense suffix is unaccented the accent of the root remains; in a compound with a prefix the accent of a word ending in a *kṛt* suffix; with a dual following, lengthening to the preceding class vowel. ‖ 5 ‖ (the Ṛk number stands at the end of the grammar line in the supplied text).

#### Ṛk 6

*[The Saṃhitā and Pada of this ṛk were a picture in the supplied text. The picture was viewed and agrees with the machine text, which has been used with the lines joined; the Saṃhitā prints पथिवी (*pathivī*) for *pṛthivī*, and the Pada prints सुप्रतीके इति सुप्रतीके (*supratīke iti supratīke*) (the word repeated after "iti"); both kept as supplied.]*

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> उ॒र्वी सद्म॑नी बृह॒ती ऋ॒तेन॑ हु॒वे दे॒वाना॒मव॑सा॒ जनि॑त्री ।
> द॒धाते॒ ये अ॒मृतं॑ सु॒प्रती॑के॒ द्यावा॒ रक्ष॑तं पथिवी नो॒ अभ्वा॑त् ॥ ६ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> उर्वी इति । सद्मनी इति । बृहती इति । ऋतेन । हुवे । देवानां । अवसा । जनित्री इति ।
> दधाते इति । ये इति । अमृतं । सुप्रतीके इति सुप्रतीके । द्यावा । रक्षतं । पृथिवी इति । नः । अभ्वात् ॥ ६ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> उर्वी विस्तीर्णे सद्मनी सदनाधारभूते बृहती महत्यौ महानुभावे
> देवानां । उपलक्षणमेतत् । देवमनुष्यादीनामवसा प्रीत्या निमित्तेन जनित्री
> वृष्टिसस्ययोर्जनित्र्यौ । वृष्ट्यादेर्देवानामवसा तर्पणेन निमित्तेन ऋतेन यज्ञेन
> च निमित्तभूतेन हुवे । आह्वयामि । देवानां हविरर्थमस्मदृज्ञायेत्यर्थः ।
> ये सुप्रतीके शोभनरूपे अमृतमुदकं दधाते धारयतः ते युवामाह्व-
> यामि । शिष्टं स्पष्टं ॥

*Gist:* I invoke, for the nourishment of the gods and for sacrifice, Heaven and Earth, the wide, mighty mothers of rain and plants, who in beautiful form hold the waters; may they protect us.

### Printed p. 84

*Running head: [84 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 185].*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

उर्वी (*urvī*) — very extensive. सद्मनी (*sadmanī*) — the support of everything. बृहती (*bṛhatī*) — mighty. जनित्री (*janitrī*) — mothers (of rain and plants). ये (*ye*) — who सु प्रतीके (*su pratīke*) — having a beautiful form, अमृतं (*amṛtaṃ*) — the rain-water equal to ambrosia, दधाते (*dadhāte*) — have held (such Heaven and Earth). देवानां (*devānāṃ*) — of the gods (and men). अवसा (*avasā*) — causing satisfaction. ऋतेन (*ṛtena*) — as the occasion of the sacrifice. हुवे (*huve*) — I call, with praise. द्यावापृथिवी (*dyāvāpṛthivī*) — O Heaven and Earth. अभ्वात् (*abhvāt*) — from great fear. नः (*naḥ*) — us. रक्षतं (*rakṣataṃ*) — protect.

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads ⟦⟧; standard label inserted.]*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

I invoke with praise, as the occasion of the sacrifice that causes the satisfaction of the gods and men, Heaven and Earth, who are very extensive, the support of everything, mighty, the mothers of rain and plants, of beautiful form, and who hold the rain-water that is like ambrosia. O Heaven and Earth, save us from great calamity and protect us.

**Printed English translation**

I Invoke to the sacrifice, for the preservation of gods (and men), the two vast, all supporting, and mighty parents (of the rain and corn); who, beautiful in form, sustain ambrosial (showers); defend us, Heaven and Earth, from great danger.

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads ⟦⟧; standard label inserted.]*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

As in the previous ṛk, in this ṛk too there is a description of the qualities of Heaven and Earth. It may be said that the arrangement of beloved words that is seen in the Vedas began with Heaven and Earth. In nature there are no other things so firmly related to each other as Heaven and Earth; therefore this gradually gave room to the idea that these two are a married couple. In the Ṛgveda there are only six sūktas that indicate Heaven and Earth. There is no sūkta praising only the heavenly deity; only one small sūkta of three ṛks (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-84-3) praises the Earth, and even in that there is a reference to the heavenly deity.

उर्वी (*urvī*) — from the word उरु (*uru*) the word उर्वी (*urvī*) is formed. The word उरु (*uru*) is read among the names of "many" (Ni. 2-13): the sense is "very extensive". सद्मनी (*sadmanī*) — the word सद्म (*sadma*) has been read by Yāska among the names of house (Ni. 3-13); here सद्मनी (*sadmanī*) means the earth, which has houses fit for dwelling.

### Printed p. 85

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 3.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 85.*

बृहती (*bṛhatī*) — great, or having extra greatness. देवनामवसा जनित्री (*devanāmavasā janitrī*) — Sāyaṇa's explanation is that they pour rain for the protection of the gods (and men) and thereby cause the arising of plants. Since the word जनित्री (*janitrī*) means "producing", it may be taken to mean those who produce creatures, animals, plants and so on.

ऋतेन (*ṛtena*) — the extensive sense of the word ऋतशब्दद (*ṛtaśabdada*) we have explained many times before. Although the word *ṛta* has the senses truth, water, sacrifice and so on, Sāyaṇa has given here the sense "sacrifice".

दधाते ये अमृतं (*dadhāte ye amṛtaṃ*) — the word अमृत (*amṛta*) is read among the names of water (Ni. 2-14) and among the names of gold (Ni. 2-10). Besides, Yāska,

रुशद्वत्सा रशती श्वेत्यागादारैगु कृष्णा सदनान्यस्याः । (*ruśadvatsā raśatī śvetyāgādāraigu kṛṣṇā sadanānyasyāḥ |*)
समानबंधू अमृते अनूची द्यावा वर्णं चरत आमिनाने ॥ (*samānabaṃdhū amṛte anūcī dyāvā varṇaṃ carata āmināne ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 1-113-2)

having cited this ṛk, explains अमृते (*amṛte*) as अमरधर्माणौ (*amaradharmāṇau*) ("those who never die", Ni. 2-20); and having cited

यत्रा सुपर्णा अमृतस्य भागमनिमेषु विदथाभिस्वरंति । (*yatrā suparṇā amṛtasya bhāgamanimeṣu vidathābhisvaraṃti |*)
इनो विश्वस्य भुवनस्य गोपाः स मा धीरः पाकमत्रा विवेश ॥ (*ino viśvasya bhuvanasya gopāḥ sa mā dhīraḥ pākamatrā viveśa ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ. ಸಂ.] 1-164-21)

this ṛk, he explains अमृतस्य उदकस्य (*amṛtasya udakasya*) as "of water" (Ni. 3-11). In the present ṛk *amṛta* means water: the meaning is that they hold, in the atmosphere in the form of clouds, the rain-water that is the support of all beings.

सुप्रतीके (*supratīke*) — प्रतीकं मुखं प्रकाशाख्यं दर्शनं वा (*pratīkaṃ mukhaṃ prakāśākhyaṃ darśanaṃ vā*): the word प्रतीकं (*pratīkaṃ*) means either "face" or "a radiant appearance". The Nirukta authors, having cited

यावन्मात्रमुषसो न प्रतीकं सुवर्णो ३ वसते मातरिश्वः । (*yāvanmātramuṣaso na pratīkaṃ suvarṇo 3 vasate mātariśvaḥ |*)
तावद्वधात्युप यज्ञमायन् ब्राह्मणो होतुरवरो निषीदन् ॥ (*tāvadvadhātyupa yajñamāyan brāhmaṇo hoturavaro niṣīdan ‖*)

(Ṛ. Saṃ. [printed ಮ ಸಂ.] 10-88-19)

give the sense "radiant appearance". Here it means "those of beautiful form, charming to the eye, full of water, plants and so on".

### Printed p. 86

*Running head: [86 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū 185]. The window begins at the head of the grammar page of Ṛk 6, continuing the Special Topics of p. 85.*

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page, noted briefly)

सद्मनी-षद्‌ल्य (*sadmanī-ṣadlya*) (root *sad*, with the senses of loosening, going and sinking): by अन्येभ्योपिदृश्यंते (पा. सू. ३-२-७३६) (*anyebhyopidṛśyaṃte (pā. sū. 3-2-736)*) (Pā. Sū. 3-2-736, as printed) the suffix *manin*; because it has the mark *n*, the first-syllable acute. बृहती (*bṛhatī*): by the vārttika बृहन्महतोरुपसंख्यानं (*bṛhanmahatorupasaṃkhyānaṃ*) the suffix *ṅīp* takes the acute. सुपां सुलुक् (*supāṃ suluk*): with a dual following, the single substitution by lengthening to the preceding class vowel. जनित्री (*janitrī*) (root जनी (*janī*), "to be born"): the suffix *tṛn* in the agent sense; the augment *iṭ*; by यन्नेभ्यो जतीप् (पा. सू. ४-१-४) (*yannebhyo jatīp (pā. sū. 4-1-4)*) (Pā. Sū. 4-1-4) the feminine suffix *ṅīp*; the *yaṇ* substitution; because the suffix has the mark *n*, the first-syllable acute. दधाते (*dadhāte*): because it stands at the beginning of the pāda there is no *nighāta*; the suffix accent, first-syllable acute. सुप्रतीके (*supratīke*) ("those of which the face is good"): by परादिच्छंदसि बहुलम् (पा. सू. ६-२-१९९) (*parādicchaṃdasi bahulam (pā. sū. 6-2-199)*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-199) the first-syllable acute of the later member.

#### Ṛk 7

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> उ॒र्वि पृ॒थ्वी ब॑हु॒ले दू॒रेअं॑ते॒ उप॑ ब्रुवे॒ नम॑सा य॒ज्ञे अ॒स्मिन् ।
> द॒धाते॒ ये सु॒भगे॑ सु॒प्रतू॑र्ति॒ द्यावा॒ रक्ष॑तं पृथिवी नो॒ अभ्वा॑त् ॥ ७ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> > उर्वी इति । पृथ्वि इति । बहुले इति । दूरेअंते इति दूरेअंते । उप । ब्रुवे । > नमसा । यज्मे । अस्मिन् ।
> > दधाते इति । ये इति । सुभगे इति सुभगे । सुप्रतूर्ति इति सुप्रतूर्ति । द्यावा । > रक्षतं । पृथिवी इति । नः । अभ्वात् ॥ ७ ॥

> *urvī iti | pṛthvi iti | bahule iti | dūreaṃte iti dūreaṃte | upa | bruve | > namasā | yajme | asmin |*
> *dadhāte iti | ye iti | subhage iti subhage | supratūrti iti supratūrti | dyāvā | > rakṣataṃ | pṛthivī iti | naḥ | abhvāt ‖ 7 ‖*

### Printed p. 87

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 3] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 87 (as supplied).*

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads सहयभाष्यं (*sahayabhāṣyaṃ*) (inside the bhāṣya block); standard label inserted.]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> उर्वी उर्वौ महत्यौ पृथ्वि पृथिव्य बहुले अनेकप्रकारेण प्रथमाने
> बह्वाकारे दूरेअंते विप्रकृष्टांतदेशे । अपारे इत्यर्थः । ईदृश्यौ युवामस्मि-
> न्यज्ञॆ नमसा नमस्साधनेन स्तोत्रेणोप ब्रुवे । उपेत्य ब्रवीमि । सौमात्यर्थः ।
> पुनः कीदृश्यौ । ये सुभगे शोभनभाग्ये सुप्रतूर्ति सुप्रतरणे शोभनदाने
> दधाते विश्वं जगत् ते युवामुप ब्रुवे सौमि ॥

*Gist:* O Heaven and Earth, vast, many-formed, endless, fortunate and good givers, who support the whole world: at this sacrifice I approach and address you with praise.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

उर्वी (*urvī*) — having a great form. पृथ्वि (*pṛthvi*) — very extensive. बहुले (*bahule*) — of many-formed shape. दूरेअंते (*dūreaṃte*) — endless (and) ये (*ye*) — who सुभगे (*subhage*) — being auspicious, सुप्रतूर्ति (*supratūrti*) — being good givers, दधाते (*dadhāte*) — hold (the whole world), such Heaven and Earth, अस्मिन् यज्ञे (*asmin yajñe*) — in this sacrifice. नमसा (*namasā*) — with praise accompanied by salutation. उपब्रुवे (*upabruve*) — I approach and pray. द्यावापृथिवी (*dyāvāpṛthivī*) — O Heaven and Earth. नः (*naḥ*) — us अभ्वात् (*abhvāt*) — from great fear. रक्षतं (*rakṣataṃ*) — protect.

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads भानुवार (*bhānuvāra*); standard label inserted.]*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

I approach and pray, at this sacrifice, with praise accompanied by salutation, to Heaven and Earth, who have a great form, are very extensive, many-formed, endless, auspicious, good givers, and who hold the whole world. O Heaven and Earth, save us from terrible sin and protect us.

*[As supplied, the label line of the printed English reads English Trnslation (*English Trnslation*); standard label inserted.]*

**Printed English translation**

I glorify with reverence, at this sacrifice, (the two), who are vast, expansive, multiform, infinite, auspicious; who support (all beings) by their bounty : defend us, Heaven and Earth, from great danger. (*I glorify with reverence, at this sacrifice, (the two), who are vast, expansive, multiform, infinite, auspicious; who support (all beings) by their bounty : defend us, Heaven and Earth, from great danger.*)

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

बहुले (*bahule*) — "having a form of many kinds, through the many kinds of sights such as mountains, rivers, forests, trees and houses". दूरे अंते (*dūre aṃte*) — *anta* means "end"; its end is very far away, that is, it extends to a very great distance and is immeasurable: this is the meaning. This word is used in the Ṛgveda only in this ṛk and in

### Printed p. 88

*Running head: [88 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū 185].*

समान्या वियुते दूरे अंते ध्रुवे पदे तस्थतुर्जागरूके । (*samānyā viyute dūre aṃte dhruve pade tasthaturjāgarūke |*) उत स्वसारा युवती भवंती आदु बृवाते मिथुनानि नाम ॥ (*uta svasārā yuvatī bhavaṃtī ādu bṛvāte mithunāni nāma ‖*) (म. सं. ३-५४-७) (*(ma. saṃ. 3-54-7)*)

nowhere else. There too it praises the qualities of Heaven and Earth.

दधाते (*dadhāte*) — although this word means only "to hold", here it means "those who hold the whole world by nourishment and so on".

सुप्रतूर्ति (*supratūrti*) — "those who give well", that is, those who provide the rain, plants, food and all the means of wealth that are most necessary for the living beings of the world.

नमसा (*namasā*) — the word *namas* has several senses: salutation, food, praise; here the bhāṣyakāra has taken the sense "praise".

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page, noted briefly)

उर्वी (*urvī*) (the word *uru* is a quality word ending in *a*): by वोतोगुणवचनात् (पा. सू. ४-१-८४) (*votoguṇavacanāt (pā. sū. 4-1-84)*) (Pā. Sū. 4-1-84) the feminine *ṅīp* with *yaṇ* substitution; by उदात्तयणोहल् पूर्वात् (पा. सू. ६-१-१७४) (*udāttayaṇohal pūrvāt (pā. sū. 6-1-174)*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-174) the *ī* takes the acute; with a dual following, by सुपां सुलुक् (*supāṃ suluk*) lengthening to the preceding class vowel; in all the words ending in *i* here the lengthening to the preceding class vowel has come. ब्रुवे (*bruve*) (root ब्रूञ् व्यक्तायां वाचि. (*brūñ vyaktāyāṃ vāci.*), "in the sense of speaking clearly"): present tense, first person singular; the *nighāta* accent arises. सुभगे (*subhage*): by आद्युदात्तं द्वैच् छंदसि (पा. सू. ६-२-११९) (*ādyudāttaṃ dvaic chaṃdasi (pā. sū. 6-2-119)*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-119) the first-syllable acute of the later member. सुप्रतूर्ति (*supratūrti*) ("those of which the gift is good"): by परादिश्चंदसि बहुलम् (पा. सू. ६-२-१९९) (*parādiścaṃdasi bahulam (pā. sū. 6-2-199)*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-199) the first-syllable acute of the later member; as before, lengthening to the preceding class vowel in the dual. ‖ 7 ‖

> दे॒वान्वा॒ यच्च॑कृ॒मा कच्चि॒दागः॒ सखा॑यं वा॒ सद॒मिजा स्प॑तिं

### Printed p. 89

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 3] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 89 (as supplied). The Saṃhitā of Ṛk 8 began at the end of p. 88 (previous window, block incomplete); the remaining two lines are given here as a separate block, and the Pada follows.*

**Saṃhitā-pāṭha** (continuation of Ṛk 8)

> इयं धीर्भूया अवयानमेषां द्यावा रक्षतं पृथिवी
> नो अभ्वात् ॥ ८ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

*[In the supplied text the Pada of this ṛk was a picture; it was viewed and agrees with the machine text, which has been used with the lines joined.]*

> देवान् । वा । यत् । चकृम । कत् । चित् । आगः । सखायं । वा । सदं । इत् । जाःपतिं । वा ।
> इयं । धीः । भूयाः । अव्ययानं । एषां । द्यावा । रक्षतं । पृथिवी इति । नः । अभ्वात् ॥ ८ ॥

> *devān | vā | yat | cakṛma | kat | cit | āgaḥ | sakhāyaṃ | vā | sadaṃ | it | jāḥpatiṃ | vā |*
> *iyaṃ | dhīḥ | bhūyāḥ | avyayānaṃ | eṣāṃ | dyāvā | rakṣataṃ | pṛthivī iti | naḥ | abhvāt ‖ 8 ‖*

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads साहणभाष्यं (*sāhaṇabhāṣyaṃ*) (inside the bhāṣya block); standard label inserted.]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे द्यावापृथिव्यौ वयं देवान् देवान्प्रति यत्कच्चिदागोऽपराधं तत्तद्या-
> गकाले तेषां तेषामयागलक्षणं सदमित्सर्वदैव चकृम कृतवंतो वयं ।
> सखायं वा प्रियं मित्रं वा प्रति यच्चक्रम । जास्पतिं । जाः पुत्रः । तासां पतिं
> जामातरं वा जायापतिं प्रति दोषारोपणकलहोत्पादनादिरूपं यत्सर्वदैव
> चकृम । एषामुक्तरूपाणां पापानामपगमं कर्तुमियं धीर्युष्मत्सुतिरूप-
> मिदं कर्म भूयाः । भूयात् । भवतु । द्यावेत्यादि गतं । अत्र सर्वत्र प्रकारांत-
> रेण मंत्रमंतरेण रक्षतं रक्षतमिति प्रार्थनं तयोरश्विन्यादिबहुमननासंभवा-
> दुचितमेव । तस्मात्स्थान एव स्थिते अनुकूले भवतमित्येवं प्रार्थ्यते ॥

*Gist:* The offences we have committed against the gods, friends or a son-in-law: may this praise-rite be their expiation; protect us from great fear.

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads ॥ प्रतिदर्थ ॥ (*‖ pratidartha ‖*) (inside the bhāṣya block); standard label inserted. The following paragraph is the Pratipadārtha.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

(O Heaven and Earth) वयं (*vayaṃ*) — we. देवान् वा (*devān vā*) — whether towards the gods. यत्कच्चिदागः (*yatkaccidāgaḥ*) — whatever offence, whether a fault or a transgression. सदमित् (*sadamit*) — always. चकृम (*cakṛma*) — we may have done (likewise). सखायं वा (*sakhāyaṃ vā*) — or towards friends. जास्पतिं (*jāspatiṃ*) — towards a son-in-law (whatever offence we may have committed; the stray syllable ना (*nā*) stands in the supplied text). एषां (*eṣāṃ*) — of such offences. अवयानं (*avayānaṃ*) — as a means of atonement. इयं धीः (*iyaṃ dhīḥ*) — this action consisting of praise. भूयाः (*bhūyāḥ*) — may it be. द्यावापृथिवी (*dyāvāpṛthivī*) — O Heaven and Earth. नः (*naḥ*) — us. अभ्वात् (*abhvāt*) — from great fear. रक्षतं (*rakṣataṃ*) — protect.

### Printed p. 90

*Running head: [90 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 185] (the print has "ಆ." for the second abbreviation, as supplied).*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Heaven and Earth, whatever offences we have committed on all the occasions of the sacrifices we perform, against the gods, and likewise whatever offences we have committed against friends and sons-in-law: may this rite of praise be the atonement for all such offences. O Heaven and Earth, save us from terrible sin and protect us.

**Printed English translation**

May this sacrifice be the means of expiating those offences which we may have committed against the gods, against a friend at any time, against a sun-in-law [sic] : defend us, Heaven and Earth, from great danger.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this ṛk the ṛṣi humbly prays to the gods to forgive the many faults and offences that may arise in the sacrificial rite that he performs or is now performing.

आगः—पाप, (*āgaḥ—pāpa,*) — the meaning is sin, offence. Explaining the derivation of this word, Yāska quotes the ṛk

यस्मै त्वं सुद्रविणो ददाशोऽनागास्त्वमदिते सर्वताता । (*yasmai tvaṃ sudraviṇo dadāśo 'nāgāstvamadite sarvatātā |*)
यं भद्रेण शवसा जोदयासि प्रजावता राधसा ते स्याम ॥ (*yaṃ bhadreṇa śavasā jodayāsi prajāvatā rādhasā te syāma ‖*)

(मि. सं. १-९४-१५) (*(mi. saṃ. 1-94-15)*) (as printed; the book prints "ಮಿ. ಸಂ.")

and then gives the derivation: आग आज्‌पूर्वाद्धमेः (अवश्यमेवैतदागच्छति कर्तारमित्यागः) एन एतेः. किल्बिषं सुकृतकर्मणो भयं कीर्तिमस्य भिनत्तीति वा । (*āga ājpūrvāddhameḥ (avaśyamevaitadāgacchati kartāramityāgaḥ) ena eteḥ. kilbiṣaṃ sukṛtakarmaṇo bhayaṃ kīrtimasya bhinattīti vā |*) ("it comes inevitably to the doer, therefore *āgaḥ*"; from *ā* + *i*, "to go"; or "it breaks the fear and the fame of one of good deeds"; this is the sense of the Sanskrit). Since the sin that may arise from the faults committed by the sacrificer in sacrifices and the like follows the sacrificer and comes to him without fail, such a sin or offence is called *agaḥ*; having so explained, he gives by the way the meaning of the word *kilbiṣa* too, which is similar.

It need not be thought that the offence the ṛṣi mentions concerns the gods alone: it is said that he expresses repentance also with regard to the people who take part in the sacrificial rites (the priests and so on), friends, the son-in-law and other relatives. The offence against people is that mutual fault-finding, quarrels and the like are likely to arise, and the ṛṣi prays that these be forgiven.

जास्पतिं— (*jāspatiṃ—*) — the word जाः (*jāḥ*) is read among the names of offspring ((नि. ३. २.) (*(ni. 3. 2.)*)); it means a daughter. The husband of a daughter is well known as *jāspati* or *jāmātā*. Yāska gives the derivation of the word *jāmātā* as

### Printed p. 91

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 3.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 91 (as supplied).*

जामाता जा अपत्यं तन्निर्माता (*jāmātā jā apatyaṃ tannirmātā*); to this Durgācārya, in his commentary, says: व्याख्यानदल्लि—जा अपत्यं स्त्रीत्वं हि मैथुनेन व्यवहारेणासौ निर्मिणोति तेनासौ दुहितुः पतिर्जामाता भवति (नि. ६-९) (*vyākhyānadalli—jā apatyaṃ strītvaṃ hi maithunena vyavahāreṇāsau nirmiṇoti tenāsau duhituḥ patirjāmātā bhavati (ni. 6-9)*) (the supplied text runs the word व्याख्यानदल्लि—जा (*vyākhyānadalli—jā*) together with the preceding dash). Because he produces offspring in the daughter, the son-in-law is called *jāmātā*.

धीः—इयं धीर्युष्मत्सुतिरूपं कर्म । (*dhīḥ—iyaṃ dhīryuṣmatsutirūpaṃ karma |*) — "this *dhī* is the rite consisting of your praise"; the word *dhī* is read among the names of actions ((नि. ३-२) द्यावापृथिविगळाद (*(ni. 3-2) dyāvāpṛthivigaḷāda*)). The ṛṣi means: the rite of praise that I perform in your honour, O Heaven and Earth.

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page, noted briefly)

चक्रम—यद्योगविरुवुदरिंद (*cakrama—yadyogaviruvudariṃda*) ("because of the word *yat* (the relative) the verb-*nighāta* does not occur; by the suffix accent the last syllable is acute"). जास्पतिम्—जायंते (*jāspatim—jāyaṃte*) ("those who are born" इति जाः. (*iti jāḥ.*)): by the sense of the word "seeing" the suffix *ḍa* although no upapada is present; because of the mark *ḍ* the loss of *ṭi*; plural by the nature of the word; "the husband of the *jā*s"; the first-member's own accent. आग:— (*āga:—*) (root इण् गतौ धातु. (*iṇ gatau dhātu.*), "to go"): by इण आगोपराधेच (उ. सू. ४-६५१) (*iṇa āgoparādheca (u. sū. 4-651)*) (Uṇ. Sū. 4-651, as printed) the suffix *asun*; the substitute *āga* for the root; because of the mark *n* the first-syllable acute. भूयाः— (*bhūyāḥ—*) (root भू सत्तायां धातु. (*bhū sattāyāṃ dhātu.*), "to be"): by व्यत्ययो बहुलम् (पा. सू. ३-१-८५) (*vyatyayo bahulam (pā. sū. 3-1-85)*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-85) the second-person singular ending in place of the third-person singular of the optative (benedictive); because it follows a non-verb the *nighāta* accent arises.

#### Ṛk 9

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> उ॒भा शंसा॒ नर्या॒ माम॑विष्टामु॒भे मामू॒ती अव॑सा सचेतां ।
> भूरि॑ चिद॒र्यः सु॒दास्त॑राये॒षा मदं॑त इषयेम देवाः ॥ ९ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> उभा । शंसा । नर्या । मां । अविष्टां । उभे इति । मां । ऊती इति । अवसा ।
> सचेतां ।

*[The Pada of Ṛk 9 is incomplete at the end of p. 91 and continues on the next page.]*

### Printed p. 92

*Running head: [92 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 185]. The window begins inside the Pada of Ṛk 9, continuing p. 91.*

**Pada-pāṭha** (continued)

> भूरि । चित् । आर्यः । सुदाःतराय । इषा । मदंतः । इषयेम । देवाः ॥ ९ ॥

> *bhūri | cit | āryaḥ | sudāḥtarāya | iṣā | madaṃtaḥ | iṣayema | devāḥ ‖ 9 ‖*

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads रायणभाष्यं (*rāyaṇabhāṣyaṃ*) (inside the Pada block); standard label inserted.]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> उभा शंसा द्यावापृथिव्योरुभयोर्विषयावुभावपि शंसौ नर्या नरेभ्यो
> हितावीदृश्यावुभयाश्रये स्तुती मामविष्टां । रक्षतां । यद्वा । द्यावापृथिव्यभि-
> मानिदेवयोरेव शंसशब्देनाभिधानात्पल्लिंगता । तथोभे ऊती रक्षिके द्यावा-
> पृथिव्यॆ मामवसा रक्षणेन सचेतां । यद्वा । प्राणिभ्यो हितकरावुभावैहिकामुष्मिक-
> विषयौ शंसौ मामविष्टां । प्राप्नुतां । तथोभे अप्यूती तयोः संबंधिन्योरभि-
> मानिदेवतयोरवसास्मत्तर्पणेन निमित्तेन सचेतां । हे देवा द्यावापृथिव्योरंत-
> र्भूताः सर्वेऽपि देवाः अर्यः स्तोतारो वयं सुदास्तरायातिशयेन शोभन-
> दातृत्वायेषान्नेन सोमलक्षणेन मदंतो मादयंतः संतो भूरि चित् । चित्
> पूजायां । अभिपूजितं धनमिषयेम । इच्छेम । यद्वा । अर्य इति षष्ठा
> रूपं । ईश्वराद्राजादेरपि सुदास्तरायेत्यर्थः । शिष्टं समानं ॥

*Gist:* Both heaven-and-earth praises, beneficial to men, protect and aid me; may all the gods between them, we praising them with soma-food, wish for abundant wealth.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

नरा (*narā*) — beneficial to men. उभा शंसा (*ubhā śaṃsā*) — Heaven and Earth, who are the object of praises of both kinds, or these praises addressed to both. मां (*māṃ*) — me. अविष्टां (*aviṣṭāṃ*) — may (they) protect. उभे ऊती (*ubhe ūtī*) — these two protectors. मां (*māṃ*) — me. अवसा (*avasā*) — by the act of protection. सचेतां (*sacetāṃ*) — may they unite (with me). देवाः (*devāḥ*) — O all gods (included between Heaven and Earth). आर्यः (*āryaḥ*) — we the praisers. इषा (*iṣā*) — with food in the form of oblation. मदंतः (*madaṃtaḥ*) — satisfying you. सुदास्तराय (*sudāstarāya*) — for making excellent gifts. भूरिचित् (*bhūricit*) — very great wealth. इषयेम (*iṣayema*) — we desire.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

May Heaven and Earth, who do good to men and are fit objects of praises of both kinds, protect me. May these two protectors join me with the act of protection. O all gods included between Heaven and Earth, we the praisers, satisfying you with food in the form of oblation, desire abundant wealth for the purpose of becoming excellent givers.

**Printed English translation**

May both these, accepting praise, and friendly to man, be favourable to me : may they both be associated as guardians for my protection: deities, we your adorers, propitiating you with (sacrificial) food, desire ample (wealth), for the sake of making liberal donations.

*[The printed English runs over the page break between p. 92 and p. 93 after the word "deities,"; it is given here as one paragraph.]*

### Printed p. 93

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 3. Varga 3.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 93 (the print has "ಅ. ೩" for the second abbreviation, as supplied).*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

चित्—ई (*cit—ī*) — this word, as Yāska says, चिदित्येषोs नेककर्मा (*cidityeṣos nekakarmā*) ("*cit* is a word of many kinds of action"), is used in many senses. In आचार्यश्चिदिदं ब्रूयादिति पूजायां आचार्यने (*ācāryaścididaṃ brūyāditi pūjāyāṃ ācāryane*) ("let the teacher say this", sense of honour) the word shows reverence; in दधिचिदित्युपमार्थे (*dadhicidityupamārthe*) (नि. १-४) (*(ni. 1-4)*) ("*cit* in the sense of comparison": "like curds") it shows comparison. In the present ṛk the sense of honour is meant.

आर्यः—ईरयिता (*āryaḥ—īrayitā*) — आर्यः—ईरयिता (*āryaḥ—īrayitā*) ... स्तुतेरीश्वरो वा । (*stuterīśvaro vā |*) ("one who urges praise, or the lord of the composition of praise"): this meaning is explained. In the present ṛk it means "we, who are praising". The Nirukta-author has read this word among the names of the lord ((नि. ३-११) (*(ni. 3-11)*)), and the word is used in the sense of "lord" in

प्रतत्ते अद्य शिविविष्ट नामार्यः शंसामि वयुनानि विद्वान् । (*pratatte adya śiviviṣṭa nāmāryaḥ śaṃsāmi vayunāni vidvān |*)
तं त्वा गृणामि तवसमतव्यान् क्षयेतमस्य रजसः पराके ॥ (*taṃ tvā gṛṇāmi tavasamatavyān kṣayetamasya rajasaḥ parāke ‖*)

((पु. सं. ७-१००-५) (*(pu. saṃ. 7-100-5)*), as printed; the book prints "ಪು. ಸಂ.")

Having quoted this ṛk, he explains: अर्योऽहमीश्वरः स्तोमानामर्यस्त्वमसीति वा (नि. ५-९) (*aryo 'hamīśvaraḥ stomānāmaryastvamasīti vā (ni. 5-9)*) ("I am the lord, *arya*; or, you are the lord of the praises"). Here the vṛttikāra explains: यस्मादर्योऽहमस्मि ईश्वरः स्तुतीनामुदीरणे यष्मद्गुणाभिज्ञः अथवा अर्यस्त्वमसि आर्य ईश्वरः मदनुग्रहाय समर्थः । (*yasmādaryo 'hamasmi īśvaraḥ stutīnāmudīraṇe yaṣmadguṇābhijñaḥ athavā aryastvamasi ārya īśvaraḥ madanugrahāya samarthaḥ |*), that is, "since I, being the lord in uttering praises, know your qualities, [*arya* means] I am able to know your qualities in order to praise; or *arya* is you, the lord, able to bestow favour on me". The word is used in the sense "venerable" in many places. For example

पृथू रथो दक्षिणाया अयोज्यैनं देवासो अमृतासो अस्तुः । (*pṛthū ratho dakṣiṇāyā ayojyainaṃ devāso amṛtāso astuḥ |*)
कृष्णादुदस्थादर्या ३ विहायाश्चि कित्संती मानुषाय क्षयाय । (*kṛṣṇādudasthādaryā 3 vihāyāści kitsaṃtī mānuṣāya kṣayāya |*)

((म. सं. १-१२३-२) (*(ma. saṃ. 1-123-2)*), as printed; the book prints "ಮ. ಸಂ.")

in this ṛk the adjective कृष्णादुदस्थादर्या (*kṛṣṇādudasthādaryā*) (printed with a figure ೩ after it) occurs in describing the qualities of the goddess Uṣas, understood from the context. Sāyaṇa has explained this word as "venerable". And its use in the sense "lord of all" is the more frequent.

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page, noted briefly)

उभा—सुपां (*ubhā—supāṃ*) — by सुलुक्—(पा. सू ७-१-३९) (*suluk—(pā. sū 7-1-39)*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-39, printed "ಸೂ" without the full stop) the substitute *ā* for the dual ending; likewise in the following words.

### Printed p. 94

*Running head: [94 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24 Sū. 185].*

अविष्टाम्—अव (*aviṣṭām—ava*) (root रक्षणे (*rakṣaṇe*), "to protect"): the third-person dual of the imperative; by —तस् थस् थमिपां- (*—tas thas thamipāṃ-*) (पा. सू. ३-४-१०१) (*(pā. sū. 3-4-101)*) (Pā. Sū. 3-4-101) the substitute *tām*; by the force of the word *bahulam* in सिब्बहुलं लेटि (पा सू. ३-१-३४) (*sibbahulaṃ leṭi (pā sū. 3-1-34)*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-34) *sip* also in the imperative; by अर्धधातुकस्येड्वलादेः (पा. सू. ७-२-३३६) (*ardhadhātukasyeḍvalādeḥ (pā. sū. 7-2-336)*) (Pā. Sū. 7-2-336) the augment *iṭ* for it; because it follows a non-verb the *nighāta* accent arises. सचेताम्—षच (*sacetām—ṣaca*) (root सचेताम्—षच (*sacetām—ṣaca*) "to join"): by धात्वादेः षः सः (पा. सू. ६-१-६४) (*dhātvādeḥ ṣaḥ saḥ (pā. sū. 6-1-64)*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-64) *s* for the initial; imperative, second-person dual form; the verb-*nighāta* arises. आर्यः— (*āryaḥ—*) (root ऋ गत्. धातु. (*ṛ gat. dhātu.*), "to go"): by आर्यः स्वामिवैश्ययोः अर्यस्यस्वाम्याख्याचेत् (*āryaḥ svāmivaiśyayoḥ aryasyasvāmyākhyācet*) ... (फि. सू. १७) (*(phi. sū. 17)*) (Phiṭ-sūtra 17, as printed "ಫಿ.") the last-syllable acute. सुदास्तराय (*sudāstarāya*) ("one who gives well" ददातीति सुदाः. (*dadātīti sudāḥ.*)): root डुदाञ् दाने धातु. (*ḍudāñ dāne dhātu.*), the suffix *asun*; the first-member keeps its accent (*kṛd-uttarapada-prakṛti-svara*); on it the suffix *tarap* in the sense of excess; being marked *p* it is unaccented. मदंतः—मद (*madaṃtaḥ—mada*) (root तृप्तियोगे. धातु. (*tṛptiyoge. dhātu.*), "in the sense of satisfaction"): the causal sense is implied (*antarbhāvita-ṇyartha*); the substitute *śatṛ* for the present suffix; by the sūtra तास्यनुदात्तेत्—सूत्रदिंद (*tāsyanudāttet—sūtradiṃda*) the present-tense *sārvadhātuka* suffix is unaccented, so the accent of the root remains. इषयेम— (*iṣayema—*) (root इषु इच्छायाम्. (*iṣu icchāyām.*), "to wish"): the optative after the causal; first person plural; because it follows a non-verb the verb-*nighāta* arises.

#### Ṛk 10

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

*[In the supplied text this Saṃhitā was a picture; it was viewed and agrees with the machine text below, which has been used with the lines joined.]*

> ऋ॒तं दि॒वे तद॑वोचं पृथि॒व्या अ॑भिश्रा॒वाय॑ प्रथ॒मं सु॑मे॒धाः ।
> पा॒ताम॑व॒द्याद्दु॑रि॒ताद॒भीके॑ पि॒ता मा॒ता च॑ रक्षता॒मवो॑भिः ॥ १० ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> ऋतं । दिवे । तत् । अवोचं । पथिव्यै । अभिश्रावाय । प्रथमं । सुमेधाः ।

> *ṛtaṃ | dive | tat | avocaṃ | pathivyai | abhiśrāvāya | prathamaṃ | sumedhāḥ |*
